# 14 — FLOW: GO (the server engine)

> **Owner:** the flow of the Go server. **Scope:** every `*.go` in the repository ROOT (the whole server is ONE flat package — there is no package boundary to infer ownership from, so ownership can only be established by reading).
> **Measured scope:** **154 files · 82,737 lines · 4,028,971 bytes** (as of 2026-09-19).
> **Coverage proof:** `19_coverage_ledger.md`. A file's row becomes `read` only when its entry exists below WITH its ranges recorded in the ledger.
> **Unrelated:** `tools/flow/` (menu-pipeline string testers) is not part of this flow.

## HOW TO READ THIS DOCUMENT

Written **incrementally, in file order**, one entry per file. Every claim cites lines. Where intent is genuinely ambiguous the entry says **`ambiguous - cited lines only`** and guesses NOTHING. Nothing here is inferred from a filename.

## ENTRY SCHEMA (identical in every flow RAG, so `17_aspects_flow.md` can be derived)

```
### `<path>` - <n> lines
- **PURPOSE:**       what it does, one sentence, from the code
- **OWNS:**          the state it is the single owner of
- **ENTRY POINTS:**  exported funcs / http routes / js.Global() bridges
- **CALLS OUT:**     the cross-domain things it reaches for
- **CALLED BY:**     MEASURED (grep/graph), never guessed
- **STATE & LOCKS:** mutexes it takes, maps it touches
- **BUILD TAGS:**    e.g. `//go:build !js && !wasm`
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** with `file:line` cites
- **VERDICT:**       owner | duplicate | dead | generated | ambiguous
```

## ENTRIES

### `tenant_vault.go` — 163 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the tenant vault REGISTRY — a vault-only address per tenant, with the vault-only rule enforced in code rather than asked for in a document (:20-22).
- **OWNS:** `TenantVaultRegistry.vaults map[string]*TenantVault` (:44) + file `tenant_vaults.json` (:26). The registry is constructed in `newLobby` and `Load`ed at boot.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `NewTenantVaultRegistry()` :47 · `Register(l, tenant, vault, label)` :52 · `Snapshot()` :104 · `Save(l)` :119 · `Load(l)` :139.
- **CALLS OUT:** `isRegisteredPlayerWallet` :67 → `l.playerBalances` + `l.leaderboard` (:90, :95) · `l.getDataPath` :128, :143 · `l.saveBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked` :134.
- **CALLED BY:** `server.go` (construction + boot Load); **`Register` has NO caller yet** — no route reaches it.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `r.mu` for map access (:70, :105, :154) · `l.mutex.RLock()` inside `isRegisteredPlayerWallet` (:88) — taken and RELEASED before `r.mu.Lock()` (:70), so the two are acquired SEQUENTIALLY, never nested: no inversion.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **`:115-118` comment CONTRADICTS `:134` code.** The comment states "THE CHAIN RECORD IS NOT DISPATCHED YET, deliberately"; `Save` **does** dispatch it (`l.saveBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked(NotePrefixTenantVaultSnapshot, snapshot)` :134). The dispatch arrived with the A6 family and the comment above it was not updated — a comment asserting the opposite of the code is the false-statement class.
  2. **`:134` calls a `...Locked` sibling while holding only `r.mu`** (released at :106) — **not** `l.mutex`. The tree's `...Locked` suffix means "the caller holds the lobby lock", so this call site is a **contract violation to be confirmed** against `saveBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked`'s definition in `economy_service.go` (flagged, not asserted, until that file is read).
  3. **Case sensitivity:** the duplicate check is case-insensitive (:73) but the map is keyed by the RAW spelling (:78) and `Load` preserves it (:159). A mixed-case vault therefore cannot be duplicated — but any EXACT-key lookup misses it. This is the repo's recurring defect family (a wallet cannot see itself); every lookup must go through `EqualFold`.
  4. **`Load` does NOT re-validate the vault-only rule** (:157-161): a hand-edited `tenant_vaults.json` naming a player wallet becomes a registered vault silently. The rule is enforced at Register only.
  5. `tenantVaultStateProposed` (:28) is the ONLY state vocabulary — `proven` exists as a bool (:37) but no `proven`/`revoked` state constants, because the proof is Stage A build 2.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the tenant vault record) — correct in its locks and its refusals; defects 1-4 are corrections to make inside the unit that wires the proof.

### `note_vocabulary.go` — 542 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-542)
- **PURPOSE:** THE SINGLE OWNER of every on-chain note prefix, so a prefix cannot drift between the client and the server (:25-30). Three scopes: `money_door` (client→vault), `checkpoint` (vault→vault), `audit_log` (vault→chain).
- **OWNS:** the prefix constants, the `noteVocabulary` catalogue (key → prefix → scope → direction → description), and `assertNoteVocabularyPolicy()` which PANICS at `init()` on an empty/duplicated/malformed/newly-ambiguous prefix (:32-35).
- **ENTRY POINTS:** the constants + catalogue + served `GET /api/notes/vocabulary`.
- **CALLS OUT:** `strings` only (:6).
- **CALLED BY:** every money door (`VerifyBuyInTransaction`'s final argument), the checkpoint writer/reader, `dispatchBlockchainSnapshot`, and the client via the served vocabulary.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none — package-level declarations, asserted at init.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **THE FILE IS MOJIBAKE-CORRUPTED ON DISK, AND THE RECORD SAYING IT WAS REPAIRED IS FALSE.** Measured at the BYTE level (2026-09-19): **688 occurrences of U+00E2**. Raw bytes of line 9: `2F 2F 20 C3 A2 E2 80 A2 C2 90 C3 A2 E2 80 A2 C2 ...` = `â` + `•` + U+0090 — the double-encoded form of `═` (U+2550 = `E2 95 90`, read as CP1252, re-encoded as UTF-8). The handoff records *"695 mojibake characters → 0 in ONE pass"*; **688 ≈ 695 means the final WRITE never reached disk.** Affected: the box-drawing rule lines (:9, :42), the em-dashes (:10, :64), the `→` arrows (:15, :22), and **SERVED `Description` strings** (:201 "the item registry â€” kind, stats", :203). The repair verified the **in-memory string**, never a read-back of the file — which is precisely how a repair reports 0 while the disk still holds the damage.
  2. Same class in `record_families.go` (**209 occurrences**) — the note there records the last pass as `213 → 0`.
  3. **`assertNoteVocabularyPolicy` (:479-540) has NO assertion about TEXT ENCODING** — and that is exactly the hole the damage came through. It validates: every field non-empty, unique keys, unique prefixes, the trailing colon, the A-Z/0-9/`_` charset, a known scope, a known direction, zero unacknowledged ambiguity, and that every money-door prefix is addressable. A Description carrying mojibake satisfies EVERY one of those rules, so the corruption passes the init panic, the test suite and all nine repo gates.
  4. **The ambiguity allow-list is EMPTY** (`notePrefixAmbiguitiesAcknowledged` :449) — so today ANY newly-ambiguous prefix panics at boot. That is the correct strictness and worth recording as a deliberate property, not an accident.
  5. **Consequence to state plainly:** the damaged strings are SERVED (`GET /api/notes/vocabulary` carries these Descriptions), and nothing catches it.
- **VERDICT:** owner, and its policy assertion is the right idea — but it needs a **SIXTH rule**: refuse a Description containing the mojibake signature (U+00E2 / U+00C3), so the repair cannot regress a third time. That single rule would have converted a silent client-facing corruption into a boot panic.


### `signature_verification.go` — 80 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the ONE owner of the "this wallet signed this message" primitive — EVM (personal_sign recovery) and AVM (address decode + ed25519 verify) — so two copies of a signature check can never disagree (:7-11).
- **OWNS:** nothing persistent. Owns the accept/refuse DECISION; each caller keeps its own message construction and nonce policy, stated deliberately at :13-15.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `verifyEVMSignature(wallet, nonce, signatureHex)` :36 · `verifyAVMSignature(wallet, message, signatureBase64)` :62 · `evmPersonalSignMessage(nonce)` :30. **CALLED BY NOTHING** (measured: untracked; no call site rewired).
- **CALLS OUT:** `crypto/ed25519` :18 · `types.DecodeAddress` :63 (`algorand/go-algorand-sdk/v2/types` :24) · `ethcrypto.Keccak256/SigToPub/PubkeyToAddress` :25, :37-52.
- **CALLED BY:** its own test only (`signature_verification_test.go`).
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none — pure functions.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `//go:build !js && !wasm` :1
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **`:76` verifies RAW ed25519 — WRONG for AVM doors.** The algosdk v2.11.1 `crypto.VerifyBytes` (`crypto/crypto.go:175`) PREPENDS a domain-separation prefix (`msgParts := [][]byte{bytesPrefix, message}`) and `SignBytes` (:165) prepends the SAME prefix before signing. An SDK-signed client is therefore REJECTED by this function, and a tenant proof could never succeed. The admin gate (`handlers_admin.go:2163`) is CORRECT — it uses the SDK helper; the oracle (`oracle_service.go:122`) is a **different, deliberate** raw-digest protocol.
  2. **Its passing test is GREEN-BUT-WRONG:** `signature_verification_test.go` signs with raw `ed25519.Sign`, so it tests MY encoding rather than the CLIENT'S. It must sign via `crypto.SignBytes` and require REFUSAL of a raw-signed message.
  3. **Unconsumed** — which is currently the only reason this is not yet a defect in production.
- **VERDICT:** owner of the primitive; its **AVM half is DEFECTIVE** until Phase 1 lands. Its EVM half is a genuine de-duplication (the two prior copies were verbatim identical).

### `tenant_vault_contract.go` — 39 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the SERVED setup contract the tenant wizard reads, in the honest "refuses and states why" form the lease catalogue already established (:7-8).
- **OWNS:** the wizard vocabulary — the 7 steps :27-35, the custody statement :20, the toll rule :21-26, the seed rule :36.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `tenantVaultSetupContract()` :12 — **NOT ROUTED** (measured: no caller in any handler or server).
- **CALLS OUT:** `recordsDispatchEnabled()` :37.
- **CALLED BY:** `tenant_vault_contract_test.go` only.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none — returns a fresh map.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **The blocker list is STALE in TWO of its three entries** — a false statement about state, which this repo treats as a defect class: `:16` "the tenant vault registry does not exist yet" (it now exists, is constructed in `newLobby`, and is loaded at boot); `:17` "the vault-only rule is not enforced yet" (**it is** — `Register` refuses a registered player wallet, a duplicate vault in any spelling, and a vault equal to the tenant wallet). Only `:18` (the proof not wired to ONE verifier) remains TRUE.
  2. **It does not publish the SIGNING CONVENTION** (the named prefix + the exact message template). A tenant's bot that followed this contract today could not construct a signature that any verifier here accepts — the same gap that makes `signature_verification.go:76` wrong.
- **VERDICT:** owner — but its blocker list must be corrected **in the same change** that wires the proof and routes it (plan Phases 3-4), or the contract keeps claiming blockers that no longer exist.


### `handlers_dashboard.go` — 65 lines
- **PURPOSE:** two read-only World Dashboard data doors — `/api/rewards` (:22) and `/api/dividends` (:47) — added so the Competition▸Rewards and Economy▸Dividends tabs stop failing.
- **OWNS:** nothing. Declares the SHAPE its loaders read: `{success, rewards[], total}` (:34-38) and `{success, available_micro, dividends[], total_pools}` (:59-64).
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `handleRewards` :22 · `handleDividends` :47 — GET only, POST → 405 (:24-28, :49-53).
- **CALLS OUT:** `encoding/json`, `net/http` — **no engine state whatsoever**.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none — touches no map, takes no lock.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **Both are permanent stubs answering `success:true` with an empty payload.** `:30-31` lowercases the wallet then DISCARDS it (`_ = wallet // wallet-specific lookup deferred`); `:55` discards it again; `:61` returns `available_micro: 0` unconditionally. The two tabs can therefore NEVER show data, and an always-empty payload is indistinguishable from "you have nothing" — the false-statement class.
  2. **A SECOND owner of dividend data already exists** — `/api/invest/dividends/history` reads real state. Two surfaces for one fact, one of them dead, is the duplication this repo forbids.
- **VERDICT:** **duplicate/dead** — read the real state, or serve an explicit refusal; never `success:true` + empty.

### `nautilus_dex_path.go` — 97 lines
- **PURPOSE:** a "simulated market buy" of $VBV that pays browser-based creators, plus a VOI→VBV swap simulation (:12-15). PILLAR 2, non-custodial settlement claims.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `ExecuteMarketBuy(l, creatorWallet, vbvAmountMicro)` :21 — takes the lock and DELEGATES ✓ · `ExecuteMarketBuyLocked` :29 · `SimulateVoiToVbvSwap(voiAmountMicro)` :78.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `l.mutex.Lock()` :22 → `ExecuteMarketBuyLocked` :24 — the correct door/`...Locked` pair.
- **WRITES:** `l.playerBalances[targetWallet] += vbvAmountMicro` :55 · `l.applyDynamicScalingLocked()` :58 · `logAdminAuditLocked("NAUTILUS_DEX_PAYOUT", …)` :61 · `sendToClientLocked` :66.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **SILENT MINTING — the Ledger's cardinal sin.** `:55` CREDITS a player balance with **no offsetting debit anywhere in the function**: `:45-51` explicitly states it does NOT touch `faucetBalanceMicro`, justifying the credit as a "liability shift" funded by console revenue. No ledger entry records that inflow, so the credit has no source. Architecture Ledger: *"No silent minting… every transaction must reconcile."*
  2. **It bypasses the token-sink router** — no `RouteCriminalTax`/sink call, so the credit never passes the ONE fee/route door every other movement uses.
  3. `:86` `penaltyBps := voiAmountMicro / 1000000000` — integer division means EVERY swap below 1,000,000,000 micro has ZERO slippage, while the comment claims 1 bp per 1,000 $VBV.
  4. `:68` carries a `float64` division in the notification text — permitted (display-only) and recorded as the allowed category.
- **VERDICT:** **owner but economically unsafe** — reconcile the credit (a sink/treasury entry) or mark the path simulation-only so it can never touch `playerBalances`.

### `career.go` — 130 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the daily salary dispenser — pays club employees from club treasuries, applies corporate and outlaw taxes, and prunes a contract whose club is gone or insolvent (:13-14, :17).
- **OWNS:** the salary loop and its tax arithmetic.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `StartSalaryDispenser(l)` :18 — a 24-hour ticker (:19).
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `l.mutex.Lock()` :24 … `Unlock()` :120 — the WHOLE loop under the write lock.
- **WRITES:** `club.TreasuryMicro -= stats.Salary` :34 · `l.playerBalances[wallet] += netSalaryMicro` :69 · `l.faucetBalanceMicro += totalTaxMicro` :73 · `l.faucetBalance = float64(l.faucetBalanceMicro) / 1000000.0` :74 · `l.leaderboard[wallet] = stats` :81, :108 · `l.CorporateTaxTotal/Count` :42-43 · `delete(club.Staff, strings.ToLower(wallet))` :100.
- **RECONCILING?** YES on the money: the club treasury is debited by the gross and the credits are the player's net plus the faucet's tax — the Industrial Loop's shape (no mint, no burn).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **FLOAT ARITHMETIC ON LEDGER MONEY — prohibited by the Architecture Ledger.** `:41` `uint64(float64(stats.Salary)*0.02 + 0.5)` · `:58` `outlawTaxRate = float64(stats.WantedLevel) * 0.02` · `:65` `uint64(float64(stats.Salary)*outlawTaxRate + 0.5)` · `:74` writes a FLOAT derived from the ledger back into `l.faucetBalance`. The float display uses in the log/notification (:83-87) are permitted; the four above are not.
  2. **`CalculateReputation` is called UNDER the write lock (:80, :107) — a SELF-LOCK CANDIDATE.** `CalculateReputation` sits in the unfenced-map gate's CONTRACT baseline as an "exported door whose callers are unconstrained"; if it takes `l.mutex` itself, this is the process-freezing self-deadlock class. **To be confirmed against its definition — not asserted.**

  1. **`:134` calls `allAchievements()` INSIDE the inner loop** — the whole 17-entry slice is re-allocated and re-scanned once per achievement per player, under the held read lock.
  2. **`handleUnlockAchievement` takes the wallet from the REQUEST BODY with no caller authentication** (:161-194), so any caller can unlock any wallet's trophy; the only gate is that the id exists in the canonical list.
- **VERDICT:** owner — 1 is a cost defect, 2 is an authorization gap.

### `identity_bridge.go` — 208 lines
- **PURPOSE:** server-authoritative cross-platform identity links — link, unlink, resolve, snapshot, plus `ResolvePrimaryWallet` (:13-14).

- **STATE & LOCKS:** every handler takes the lobby lock; both mutations dispatch the snapshot WHILE holding the write lock ✓ consistent with the `...Locked` naming.

- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:**
  1. **`Verified: true` is set with NO verification anywhere** (:80, :86): the client asserts an address and the bridge records it as verified.
  2. **`:121` `filtered := info.Linked[:0]` REUSES the backing array in place** — the removed element's memory is overwritten while any other holder of the old slice sees mutated contents (the aliasing family).
  3. The duplicate-owner check walks EVERY linked wallet (:67-74) holding the lobby WRITE lock.
- **VERDICT:** owner — 1 is the substantive gap; 2 is an aliasing hazard; 3 is a cost issue.

  1. **`computeIdentityScore` casts a SIGNED reputation to `uint64`** (`uint64(p.TotalReputation)` :182) — a punished player with a negative total wraps to an astronomically HIGH score: **the worse the reputation, the closer to LEGENDARY**.
  2. **`handleIdentityRecord` (:238-252) accepts a client-declared `impact`** and stores it with `Permanent: true` for the caller's own wallet — self-awarded reputation through a public door.
  3. The global is unsnapshotted although the file's own header promises "nothing important should disappear".
  4. `GetProfile`/`GetEvents` return live pointers/slices.
- **VERDICT:** owner, with a signedness bug that inverts the score's meaning and a client-writable reputation door.

### `ui_tree_theming.go` — 352 lines
- **PURPOSE:** §10.7 — ONE unique placeholder frame per UI tree, with `rewards` and `achievements` PINNED to Crypto-seraph (:5-34).
- **OWNS:** `uiTreePin` + `uiTreePins` (2 pins, frames `npc-crypto-seraph-003`/`-004`) (:45-53) · **`uiTreeCanonicalOrder`** (:59+, every category then its features) · `UiTreeArtFor(extra)` · `uiTreePinViews` :312 · `pinCharacterName` :322 · `uiTreeArtAsserted` :331 · `handleUiTreeArt` :273 (public, wallet-independent, `?ids=` appended after the canonical set).
- **GUARANTEES, STATED AND IMPLEMENTED:** uniqueness is **computed** from what was handed out, never asserted (:255-264) · the pool is deterministic (round-robin across characters, no RNG) · a pinned frame is removed from the pool first · the art served is the LIGHT rendition when derived, with the shipped frame named beside it (:28-30) · a pinned frame the media policy refuses fails LOUDLY at the door (:336-347).

### `record_envelope_test.go` — 409 lines
- **PURPOSE:** pins the record ENVELOPE — the note grammar that makes a whole-map record travel at all (:14-15).
- **ASSERTS:** a small record needs exactly ONE note carrying `<prefix><tag>.`; it parses to nonce/chunk/total with `Legacy=false`; assembly reports NO gap; the reassembled body decodes to the exact bytes written (:16-52) · **the AVM cap is not a preference** — a realistic 2,000-record payload must need MORE than one note, EVERY note must be ≤ `avmNoteMaxBytes`, reassembly (performed exactly as the reader does) yields one complete record with the right chunk count (:58-95) · **a THIRD PARTY writing the same prefix with a LATER block time must be IGNORED** — the returned block time is the newest CHUNK's, and the reader CONTINUES the writer's nonce sequence (seeded 9 → next 10) so a restart cannot reuse a nonce (:320-377) · an empty chain is `found=false` with nil data and 0, and that is explicitly NOT an error (:382-396).
- **OWNS:** `recordTestPayload(n)` (:398-408) — a deterministic, LOW-REDUNDANCY generator, with the reason stated: a compressible payload would make a chunk-count assertion an accident (3,000 identical bytes gzip to one chunk).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none.
- **VERDICT:** owner (test) — the third-party-ignore and nonce-continuation assertions are the load-bearing ones.

### `record_envelope.go` — 418 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the ONE owner of how a record is CARRIED and put back together (:16-51).
- **THE DESIGN FACTS IT STATES (each load-bearing):** the AVM caps a transaction note at **1024 bytes**, so the previous writer's `prefix + base64(gzip(json))` in ONE note **could not have been accepted** — "unbounded by construction and had never been exercised (the dispatch gate is OFF)" (:20-26) · the nonce is a per-family **SEQUENCE**, because a sequence makes a MISSING update detectable while a clock makes a gap look like a quiet period (:28-33) · the grammar `<prefix>R1.<nonce>.<chunk>.<total>.<base64(gzip(data))>`, and `.` is not in the base64 alphabet so header and body can never be confused (:35-40) · the body is UNCHANGED, so `snapshot_guard.go`'s 8 MiB/64 MiB caps keep applying at the SAME boundary (:42-44) · a note WITHOUT the envelope is still read as one chunk of nonce 0 **and FLAGGED legacy** — accepted AND flagged, because refusing would discard state and accepting silently would hide a format change (:46-50).
- **OWNS:** `avmNoteMaxBytes` 1024 · `recordEnvelopeTag` `"R1"` (a future format is refused **by name**) · `recordMaxChunks` 8192 · `recordGapReportLimit` 8 (:53-65) · `recordNote`/`recordSelection`/`recordGap` (:69-89) · `encodeRecordNotes` · `parseRecordNote` · `assembleRecords` (:~300-334) · `gapFromChunks` :337 · `recordGapSummary` :359 (caps the indices shown so a broken log cannot flood the boot log) · the nonce source `recordNonceNext` / `recordNonceSeed` / `recordNonceCurrentValue` / `recordNonceReset` (:381-418).
- **STATED LIMIT (honest, in the file):** **the nonce seed is per-PROCESS** (:376-380) — no record has ever been written on chain, so there is no history to continue yet; when the seed is planted (D1) the highest nonce per family must become part of the restored state, "and this note must be revised WITH that state rather than left to drift".
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** **display limit, recorded** — the window `:90-311` (the encoder, the note parser and the assembler) was truncated by the tool; its behaviour is pinned by `record_envelope_test.go`, read in full.
- **VERDICT:** owner — the per-process nonce seed is the one named gap, and the file names it itself.

### `market_nodes_alias_gate_test.go` — 422 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the third lock gate — **ALIASING**: one map, ONE owner mutex (:15-56).
- **WHY IT EXISTS:** the other three gates cannot see the class — `selflock_gate_test.go` and `mutex_relock_gate_test.go` model **ONE** mutex each and `lock_order_gate_test.go` models the **ORDER** of two. `Lobby.marketNodes` and `TokenSinkRouter.MarketNodes` are THE SAME MAP (assigned in `server.go`, in `getOrCreateMarketNodeLocked`, and on restore in `lobby_manager.go`), so a spelling is not a lock (:22-25).
- **WHY IT IS WORSE:** a self-lock or an ABBA inversion leaves the process alive and diagnosable; a concurrent map access is `fatal error: concurrent map read and map write` / `… iteration and map write` — not an error value, not catchable with `recover`, and it takes every connection down (:27-30).
- **MEASURED WHEN WRITTEN — every finding was LIVE:** the only runtime WRITER held `{Lobby.mutex}` alone; the 15-minute persistence worker, the dividends-history route and the revenue-distribution walk each read/iterated the SAME map holding `{TokenSinkRouter.Mu}` and NO lobby lock; and the market-vitality range (`theme_engine.go`) and the hourly dividend daemon's `len(...)` held **NO map lock at all** — disjoint lock sets over ONE map (:32-39).
- **THE SUBJECT IS DERIVED, NOT CURATED:** an alias pair comes from an **ASSIGNMENT between two field selectors of the SAME declared map type**, mutex identities canonicalised through the struct declarations, and the sibling gate's machinery is **REUSED rather than copied** (`loDeriveFields`, `loVarsFor`, `loCanonFieldOf`, the MUST-held walker and `...Locked` depth-1 seeding) (:41-46).
- **LIMITS, STATED IN THE FILE:** MUST-HELD with the sibling semantics; an expression whose receiver type cannot be resolved **without guessing** is SKIPPED AND COUNTED (`a.skipped` printed on every run); a map reached through a LOCAL (`m := l.marketNodes`) does not resolve and is skipped, **never assumed safe**; `...Locked` callees walked per call site, depth 1; and it is a **STATIC census** (:48-56).
- **PINS + CONTROL:** the pinned identities and the ONE owner mutex are CONSTANTS asserted by the gate, so a rename or retype FAILS and asks for the rule to be re-derived "rather than quietly measuring nothing" (:58-60). The control carries 3 MUST-REPORT shapes (an unfenced walk, an unlocked range, an unfenced rebind) and 4 MUST-NOT (a fenced rebind, the owner lock held, a legal snapshot helper that takes and RELEASES inside itself, and a **within-object** bind), asserts the control's **own** alias was derived, that `solo`/`alt` did NOT enter the watch set, that ZERO accesses is FATAL — and pins the exact counts (**4 findings, with the rebind counted TWICE** because it writes one field and reads the other) (:369-422).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none in the file; the census it guards is described above and in `Problems.md` §35. **Display limit:** `:61-325` truncated.
### `auction_service.go` — 472 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the Art Gallery / auction surface — list, create, bid, settle, and the ARC-200 "pull" that moves a losing/holding bidder's tokens into the vault (:25-27, stateless service).
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `HandleGetAuctions` · `HandleCreateAuction` · (in the undisplayed window) the bid/settle handlers · `pullTokensToVault`-style ARC-200 `transferFrom` (:383-408) · `TransferBundleItems` (:429).
- **OWNS:** the response envelope discipline for the gallery — **ONE envelope `{success, auctions[], count}`**, because "the previous bare array meant the Markets 'Auctions' section could never populate", and the array is always `[]`, never `null` (:32-60).
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `l.auctions` is read under `RLock` and COPIED TO A POINTER SLICE, then released (`:31-38`) · the Envoi name resolution happens OUTSIDE the lock deliberately (:40-50) · the ARC-200 pull mutates `l.faucetBalanceMicro` + its float mirror under `Lock()` and routes the audit through `tokenSinkRouter.Audit.InterceptAndAudit("AUCTION_PULL", …)` in the same critical section (:410-421) · `TransferBundleItems` is documented as **"assumes the lobby mutex is held"** (:428) — a lock-expected helper, matching the map gate's CONTRACT class.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **A FLOAT ON LEDGER MONEY.** `StartPrice float64 json:"start_price"` is the listing price and is validated as `<= 0` (:68, :79-82) — the Architecture Ledger prohibits a float for a price; the client supplies it and no integer form exists.
  2. **A FLOAT MIRROR WRITTEN AS STATE UNDER THE LOCK.** `l.faucetBalance = float64(l.faucetBalanceMicro) / 1000000.0` (:412) — the same float-mirror family as `career.go`; the mirror is display-only by rule but is stored on the Lobby.
  3. **A RACE ON THE SHARED `Auction` STRUCT.** `HandleGetAuctions` copies pointers under `RLock`, releases, then WRITES `a.SellerName` / `a.HighestBidderName` on the still-shared objects with no lock held (:43-50) — "updating 'a' populates the master record for all future requests" states the intent, but two concurrent readers (or a reader and a settle) write the same fields.
  4. **IGNORED ERRORS ON THE PAYMENT PATH.** `types.DecodeAddress(from)` / `DecodeAddress(vaultAddr)` / `strconv.ParseUint(rewardAsset, 10, 64)` are all `_`-discarded (:385-397), so a malformed address or an empty `rewardAssetID` yields a **zero-value address / appID 0** and the transaction is still built and signed.
- **Display limit:** the window `:102-369` was truncated by the tool.
- **VERDICT:** owner — carries one float price, one float mirror, one struct race and four discarded payment-path errors (recorded, not repaired).
