### `card_view_skins.go` — 483 lines
- **PURPOSE:** §23.5.5 — **the viewer-scoped card display layer**: "Player 1 can change how they see player 2's cards" (:6-8).
- **THE GUARANTEE IS AN ABSENT FIELD, NOT A CONVENTION:** a record is keyed by the viewer's own wallet and names only an asset that viewer owns AND holds, so **there is nowhere to name another player's card or entity** (:10-13).
- **WHAT IT EXPLICITLY DOES NOT DO:** it **never writes `registry.Bindings`** — the only branding writer remains `bonded_branding.go`'s `BindBondedAssetToTarget` — so a card cannot become a branding target *through* this feature, "because this feature cannot write branding at all" (:15-18). A viewer cannot mutate another player's state; the match authority, the replay and the opponent's client never see the record (:19-21).
- **FAIL-CLOSED ORDER (stated as a discipline):** (1) the ENTITY-NAMING field is card-trapped FIRST via `IsCardTargetKind` + the alias list; (2) **`scope` is the one field the substring scan must NOT touch** — the audience vocabulary legitimately contains "card" because it says WHICH cards are in view (`foreign_cards`/`own_cards`/`all_cards`), so scope is exhaustively allowlisted while the alias check still refuses an entity-naming spelling such as `"deck_card"`; (3) **a bonded asset is REQUIRED** — `engine` (off) | `asset` (wear one of your own), there is no asset-free mode, so the layer can never theme a card with art the player does not own; (4) `DisallowUnknownFields()` means a `target_kind`/`target_id`/`card_id` payload **cannot even be parsed**; (5) §23.5.1 capacity is served on the read path so a player is "never refused without being told why" (:26-47).
- **INTEGER DISCIPLINE:** no money and no float live here — the only numeric value is a timestamp (:49-50).
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `handleCardView` (`GET` reads, `POST` sets, else JSON method-not-allowed) · `cardViewRead` — **a wallet-less visitor still gets the RULES + `wallet_required:true`** rather than an unexplained empty control (:391-406) · `cardViewSet` (8 KiB body cap, decoder refusal message states a card request cannot be expressed) · `handleClearCardView` (POST-only).
- **REFUSALS CARRY THE BUDGET:** a refused set answers with the wallet's own `capacity` object (:439-447).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none. **Display limit:** the window `:66-379` (the vocabulary constants, `CardViewPolicy`, the store/read resolvers and the ownership gates) was truncated by the tool; those paths are pinned by `card_view_skins_test.go`.
- **VERDICT:** owner.

### `bonded_market_test.go` — 454 lines
- **PURPOSE:** §10.8 proof for the player-to-player market: money moves EXACTLY, ownership moves with it, **every refusal moves nothing at all**, a listing can never outlive the ownership it was written under, the price is never client-supplied, and a sale survives a restart (:4-7).
- **ASSERTS (the core trade):** buyer pays the listing's price, seller receives price − fee, and the **fee really reaches the sink** ("a fee must never be burned silently"), with the reconciliation asserted as an identity — `buyer_after + seller_after + sink == buyer_before` (:77-89) · the fee arithmetic is pinned to a literal (`10,000 $VBV` → `250 $VBV` at `BondedMarketFeeBps`) so a bps change fails loudly (:63-66).
- **(window `:89-372`, not displayed) the rest of the arithmetic:** the refusal classes, the stale-listing rule, and the **write-through** checks (a sold and a cancelled listing both survive a fresh `Load`).
- **ASSERTS (HTTP boundary):** a buy body naming `price_micro` and a list body naming `seller_wallet` are **refused at the decoder** and leave both the balance and the ownership untouched; `POST` on the read route is `method not allowed` ("a read must never write"); and the GET read serves `fee_bps`, `acquisition_rule`, `min_price_micro`, `max_price_micro` and `capacity` (:393-453).
- **NOTE ON THE FIXTURE:** `marketTestMedia()` exists because the **market requires art** even though the mint path does not validate it (:19-27) — a test asset must carry media to be listable.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none (it is the fix's pin). **Display limit:** the window `:89-372` was truncated.
- **VERDICT:** owner (test).

### `asset_life_engine.go` — 785 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the three bonded-asset life surfaces — §26.4 Companion Pets (breedable, maturity-gated), §25.6 Vehicles (level-gated, custom-named), §25.7 World Content (deployable region entities) — all deterministic integer math, **no cloud RNG** (:5-9).
- **OWNS `BreedPet`** (:29-78): gates in a strict order — both parents found → both owned by the caller → **neither black-market-adopted** (§27.7.3) → **both certified** (§26.4.1) → **both mature** — and only then charges, so "a refused breeding can never cost a player a single micro-unit" (:52-53). The fee is **SERVER-authoritative** (`PetBreedFeeMicro`; the handler ignores any body fee, "because a client must never price its own account upgrade") (:25-28) and is charged through `chargeBondedAssetFeeLocked` (:54). Traits are deterministic: `(sire.Traits & 0x5555…) | (dam.Traits & 0xAAAA…)` — even-indexed bits from the sire, odd from the dam (:22-23, :57). Offspring: 30-day maturity, `Stats = meanEntityStats(sire, dam)` (the lineage stat floor that makes the grooming ladder compound), `PetLevel 1`, an empty `Grooming` map, certified with clean lineage (:58-76).
- **A STATED LOCK-DISCIPLINE NOTE:** `BreedPet` runs under `l.mutex.Lock()` and therefore calls the **lock-free core** of the ThemeEngine outcome hook rather than the self-locking form (:80-81) — a live instance of the `...Locked` convention being followed.
- **(window `:81-688`, not displayed)** the rest of the file: `SpawnPet`/`SpawnVehicle` (purchase, class table, trait-derived stat floor), `GroomPet` + the grooming ladder, `UpgradeVehicle` + the six-part ladder, buy/deploy for world content, and the region readers.
- **OWNS the §26.4.3 / §25.6.2 deck-card bonus** (:698-750): `BondedDeckBoostDivisor = 60` (every 60 household stat points = +1% card power) and `BondedDeckBoostMaxPct = 10`; the bonus is computed only from **certified, non-black-market** pets and vehicles ("only legitimate assets fight for you"), the intent being "rather than inventing a parallel combat system, the pet + vehicle stat vector lends the owner's OWN deck cards a capped integer percentage" that composes with the coalition/regional boosts; it is snapshotted into `MatchState` so Server and WASM apply an IDENTICAL constant; integer only (:700-721). `BondedDeckBoostPct` is the public door (takes the lock) delegating to `bondedDeckBoostPctLocked` (caller holds it) — the ONE-OWNER PAIR (:726-750).
- **`addEntityStats`** clamps each axis at `StatMax*64`, "well above StatMax so a large kennel is not truncated before the percent cap binds" (:752-770). **`InitAssetLife`** nil-initialises the three maps under `Lock` (:772-785).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none on the displayed ranges. **CARRY-FORWARD (known):** `l.pets` / `l.vehicles` / `l.worldContent` historically had **no snapshot**; the current cadence batch carries all three, so that is now covered — verify in `economy_service.go`'s entry.
- **Display limit:** the window `:81-688` was truncated by the tool.
- **VERDICT:** owner — the only file that defines the household stat roll-up and the deck-card bonus.


### `bonded_branding.go` — 949 lines
- **PURPOSE:** §23.5 — bonded assets are the **ecosystem-wide branding layer**: a player-owned NFT carrying the player's OWN media, wearable by any entity the player owns (item, theme, bot, pet, vehicle, church, club, shop, world content) (:5-11).
- **THE CARD EXCLUSION IS STRUCTURAL, IN THREE PLACES** (:13-20): `IsCardTargetKind` refuses a card kind **BEFORE any other check**; the allowlist may not contain one because **`assertBondedBrandingPolicy()` runs at `init()` and PANICS the boot**; and `IsCardAssetType` refuses a card-typed asset at mint.
- **THE SUBSTRING RULE IS DELIBERATE:** any kind containing `"card"` is refused, PLUS the 12 aliases that do not literally contain it (`card, cards, deck, decks, deck_card, hand, hands, hand_card, leader_card, religious_leader_card, card_deck, card_hand`) — "so a future card surface is refused by default instead of leaking in as a bondable target" (:72-97). `normalizeTargetKind` folds case, `-` and space to `_`, so `"World-Content"` and `"world content"` are ONE kind (:111-118).
- **OWNERSHIP DISCIPLINE:** §27.8 owner==holder==caller (both must match), §27.7.3 (`BlackMarketAdopted` may never brand), and **every refusal happens before state is written** (:22-25, :482-499). The bind order is stated as a contract: cards (never) → unknown kinds (never) → missing/foreign/off-ledger asset (never) → a target the caller does not own (never) → only then the write (:456-501). Unbind is **idempotent**: a binding that is not there reports `removed=false`, not an error (:503-527).
- **WHAT THE READ PATH NEEDS NO WALLET FOR:** `BondedBrandingForTarget` is public because branding is cosmetic public data (like an NFT image); **a card target returns nothing** (:529-551).
- **THE MEDIA POLICY (§23.5, :162-338):** 8 MiB bytes, 8192 px per side, 1024-char URI, schemes `https|http|ipfs|ar`, a 10-MIME allowlist, and a REQUIRED sha256 hex. `data:` is refused on purpose (an unbounded inline blob) and `javascript:`/`file:`/`blob:` as unsafe (:183-194). **The ONE scheme-less form** is `BondedMediaLocalPrefix = "/Assets/"` — judged by `isSameOriginAssetsPath` (prefix, no `..`, no `//`, unreserved characters only) BEFORE the scheme list "so a same-origin path cannot be smuggled in as a scheme and a remote URL cannot be smuggled in as a local path"; the pack was renamed `<Prefix>-NNN.png` **precisely so every frame satisfies this** (:187-237). `ValidateBondedMedia` **canonicalises** (lowercases the hash, trims URI/mime) so two spellings cannot produce two stored forms, accepts a nil media (an asset may be minted unpainted), and states the honest limit: **the bytes are never fetched** — `HashHex` is the declared integrity reference a client re-checks (:252-266).
- **THE SERVED POLICY IS THE ONLY DECLARATION** (`BondedBrandingPolicy`, :565-584): bindable kinds with labels, the blocked alias list, the asset-type catalogue, `cards_excluded: true`, the rule text, the legitimacy rule, the media limits, the **capacity rule** and `creation_requires`. The client renders this instead of hard-coding policy.
- **§23.5.1 CAPACITY (:586-683):** the basis is the wallet's `PlayerStats.Inventory` `CARD-*` keys — **the same bookkeeping `TransferBundleItems` maintains** for auctions/loans/black-market/jail, so "a client can never raise its own cap" (:590-593); quantities are SUMMED, non-positive ignored, and a wallet with no record has a supply of **0, which is a real answer, not an error** (:615-646). `BondedAssetCapacityForWallet` takes one engine lock for the supply and one registry lock for the used count — **never nested** (:648-668) — and clamps `Remaining` at 0 while reporting the TRUE used count when a gift put the wallet over its cap. `assertBondedAssetCapacity` refuses BEFORE anything is written and states the numbers (:670-683). The stated swap point: **`CardNFTSupplyForWallet` is the ONE function that changes when wallet-NFT sourcing lands** (:595-596).
- **LOCK DISCIPLINE, STATED AS A RULE:** `bondedTargetOwner` (:359-422) and `bondedTargetsOfKind` (:690-786) each take **exactly ONE engine lock and release it before building any view**, "so no two locks are ever held at once and the registry is never read under an engine lock" — the anti-deadlock design, mirroring §23.5's own binding rule (:340-344, :687-689).
- **DETERMINISM:** the target list is `sort.Slice`d by (Name, TargetID) because "the API answer must not depend on map iteration order" (:778-785). `canonicalTargetID` lowercases the id for the wallet-keyed kinds (`theme`, `bot`) so `"0xABC"` and `"0xabc"` are ONE target — the fix for the live defect where a client was told it did not own its own theme (:346-357).
- **HTTP SURFACE (:808-949):** `bondedTargetRequest{asset_id, target_kind, target_id}`; bind/unbind answer with the resulting branding; `handleBondedTargets` **explicitly REFUSES a card `kind` filter** rather than silently returning nothing, and a wallet-less visitor still gets **`policy` + `wallet_required:true`** ("the policy is public; only the entity LIST is wallet-scoped"); `handleBondedBranding` is public, accepts `kind`/`target_kind` and `target_id`/`target-id`, and answers a card kind with the rule (:876-949).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none open. One honest, documented boundary: `Certified` is not retro-applied, so "an asset minted before the flag existed is not retro-refused, but an adopted one never brands" (:486-491).
- **VERDICT:** owner — the sole home of the card exclusion, the media policy and the creation cap.

### `bonded_asset_registry.go` — 960 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the STORAGE owner of bonded assets, theme/slot bindings, the viewer-scoped card views, the viewer-scoped slide themes and the market listings — the ONE struct whose `Save`/`Load` is the snapshot (:101-128).
- **THE PERSISTENCE LESSON IS WRITTEN INTO THE STRUCT:** `Assets`/`Bindings` are EXPORTED with json tags, and each NEW map carries a comment saying why — "unexported fields silently vanish from the snapshot, which is exactly how the pre-existing assets/bindings persistence bug happened" (:107-127). `CardViews`, `SlideThemes` and `Listings` therefore arrived exported.
- **`bondedAssetsFileMu` is the FILE's mutex, not an instance's** (:130-138): `Save` writes `<file>.tmp` then renames, so two concurrent saves could interleave on one temp path; the 15-minute worker was the only writer until the market began writing through after every list/cancel/sale. The comment states the ownership reason explicitly.
- **`MintBondedAssetWithMedia`** (:156-196) refuses a card asset type FIRST (`IsCardAssetType`), then an out-of-range type, an empty name, a mood outside 0..2, a royalty outside [0,1000] and an empty owner/holder; it stamps **`Certified: true`** because "the own-wallet mint path IS the legitimate spawn path" (§27.7.3); it explicitly does **NOT** re-validate media — "the storage layer stays policy-free", `ValidateBondedMedia` owns that. `MintCatalogueAsset` (:198-212) stamps `Sku`/`Source` so `EnsureStarterAssets` is idempotent and a house frame is distinguishable from a player creation.
- **`walletMatches` is THE ONE case rule** (:214-228): a resolved request wallet is lowercased while a stored spelling is whatever the engine was handed, so a case-sensitive compare "makes a wallet invisible to itself" — the SAME defect already fixed three times in this subsystem, "so the rule lives here once and every reader uses it". An empty resolved wallet matches NOTHING (an anonymous read is never the owner of an empty-wallet asset).
- **§27.8 guard** `guardOwnerHolder` requires BOTH `OwnerWallet` and `HolderWallet` to equal the caller, and it is the single canonical guard used by `TransferOwnership` (:406), `ModifyBondedAsset` (:428), `Burn` (:457) and `SetBondedAssetMedia` (:280). `TransferOwnership` also **cancels the asset's active listings** so a listing cannot outlive the ownership it was written under (:418-421). `Burn` additionally drops the asset's bindings, its CardViews and its SlideThemes, and cancels its listings — a burned asset "must not survive as a viewer's card skin" or as themed art (:460-487).
- **THE ONE-KEY-SPACE RULE IS ENFORCED AT THE DOOR** (:494-505): a theme-slot binding is keyed `<asset>:<slot>` (TWO parts) while an entity target binding is `<asset>:<kind>:<target_id>` (THREE), in the SAME map — so `BindThemeAsset` REFUSES a slot containing `:`, "instead of being left to luck", because a slot spelled `"item:someid"` would address a target binding's key and a later lock would mutate the WRONG record.
- **DETERMINISM:** `sortThemeBindings` (:336-350) and `sortBondedAssets` (:757-769) exist because a renderer receiving map-iteration order "makes the UI look like it changed when nothing did"; both state the tie-break (target kind → target id → asset id; and newest-first → asset id).
- **Save/Load** (:593-698): the snapshot is built as private COPIES of all five maps under `RLock`, **released before** the file write; then **`l.saveBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked(NotePrefixBondedAssetSnapshot, snapshot)`** dispatches the chain record — "THE RECORD IS A TRANSPORT MIRROR of the same payload the file write produces: the snapshot above was taken under the REGISTRY OWN lock and released, so the record never sees a live map" (:630-632) — and only then the file lock is taken for the temp-write + rename. `Load` reports a fresh start honestly and restores each map with a non-nil default.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **AN AUTHORIZATION GAP ON THE ONE MUTATING DOOR.** `BindThemeAsset(assetID, slot)` (:490-511) — and its handler `handleBindThemeAsset` (:845-874) — take **NO caller wallet and never call `guardOwnerHolder`**, unlike `LockThemeAsset`, `ModifyBondedAsset`, `Burn` and `SetBondedAssetMedia`. Any wallet that knows an asset id can create/overwrite its `<asset>:<slot>` binding, and because the key is the same for every caller, a third party can also OVERWRITE it. It is not cosmetic: `MoodTagForWallet` excludes any asset that has a `Locked` binding, so a foreign locked binding **removes the TRUE OWNER's asset from their own theme mood** (§27.6's "removed from play").
  2. **`Save` AND `Load` DO NOT GUARD A NIL MAP ENTRY.** `cp := *v` in all five loops (:606-625) dereferences a possibly-nil pointer, while every reader DOES guard (`OwnedSku`, `CountOwnedBy` check `a == nil`). `Load` assigns the unmarshalled maps straight through (:673-693), so a snapshot containing `null` entries (`{"assets":{"BA-1":null}}`) loads cleanly and **panics the persistence worker on the next Save**.
  3. **RAW PREFIX MATCHES ON A COMPOSITE KEY.** `Burn` (:465-469) and `MoodTagForWallet` (:562-567) both test `k[:len(assetID)] == assetID` — a raw prefix compare on `<assetID>:<kind>:<targetID>`. It is correct only because ids are `"BA-" + uuid` and therefore no asset id is a prefix of another; that requirement is stated NOWHERE, and a shorter-id scheme would let one asset's burn/mood-check affect another's bindings.
  4. **SECTION-HEADER MOJIBAKE PRESENT IN THIS FILE.** It is one of the ten damaged files (L1 U+00E2 = 57, L2 U+00C3 = 24, U+00C2 = 39 per the ledger census): comments render as `Ã‚Â§23.5`, `â”€â”€` etc. Code is unaffected; the damage is in comments and section rules.
- **VERDICT:** owner of the asset/binding/view/listing storage — and the file with the sweep's clearest authorization gap so far.


### `bonded_market_service.go` — 718 lines
- **PURPOSE:** §10.8 — the PLAYER-TO-PLAYER bonded-asset market. Before it, acquisition was CREATION (capped by §23.5.1) or a GIFT (`POST /api/assets/transfer`, which takes no money), so a player could never SELL art they had made (:6-11).
- **THE FIVE HONESTY RULES IT STATES (:13-29):** the money is EXACT and integer (buyer pays `price`; seller receives `price − fee`; `fee = price × 250 / 10_000`, floor in uint64, routed through the ONE bonded-asset sink door) and **the debit equals the credit plus the fee, ASSERTED before any write** · the price is the LISTING's (`DisallowUnknownFields`) · **§27.8 is re-proved on every read AND every write**, a stale listing is served with its reason and cancelled on the buyer's attempt, and `TransferOwnership` itself cancels the asset's active listings · **buying is an ACQUISITION, so §23.5.1's creation cap does not gate it** (a wallet with no cards may still buy) · a wallet cannot buy its own listing, and only an ACTIVE listing may be bought.
- **THE LOCK ORDER IS PART OF THE CONTRACT (:31-33):** storage lives on `BondedAssetRegistry` and **every listing accessor is `...Locked` on purpose** (it assumes `r.mu` is held); the authority takes `l.mutex` FIRST and the registry lock second — "the same nesting the placeholder shop already uses". The file then honours it explicitly: `CancelBondedListing` audits **after** releasing `r.mu` "so the lock order here is never r.mu -> l.mutex (the trade path takes l.mutex -> r.mu, and mixing the two orders deadlocks)" (:324-326), and the trade's own audit uses `logAdminAuditLocked` because `l.mutex` is already held (:413).
- **`BondedMarketFeeBps = 250`**, `MinBondedListingMicro = 1`, `MaxBondedListingMicro = 1,000,000 $VBV` ("bound a price so the integer fee arithmetic cannot overflow") (:47-61). The comment on the floor division states the direction of the remainder: **the remainder stays with the seller**, so nothing is lost to rounding (:82-86).
- **THE TRADE'S ORDERING IS SPELLED OUT** (:331-344): (1) re-read and refuse unless still ACTIVE → (2) re-prove §27.8, cancelling + reporting a stale listing → (3) verify the buyer is funded via `balanceKeyLocked` (the case rule) → (4) **move OWNERSHIP first**, atomically with marking the listing SOLD, "so the ownership-cleanup rule cannot cancel the sale it is completing" → (5) move the MONEY (debit, credit net, route fee) → (6) record the fee and net ACTUALLY moved. The reason ownership precedes money: "if the guarded transfer refused, nothing has been spent yet, so there is no rollback path to get wrong" (:342-344).
- **`CompleteSale` is the ONE atomic registry step** (:426-474): it re-proves §27.8 for the seller, moves owner+holder, marks THIS listing sold with buyer/time/fee/net, and cancels any OTHER active listing for the asset — it exists precisely because the plain `TransferOwnership` cancels active listings and would otherwise cancel the sale it is completing.
- **WHAT THE SERVED VIEW ADMITS** (`BondedMarketView`, :501-576): every listing with its state, `stale_reason` joined per row so "a buyer is never offered something that cannot be delivered", the caller's own rows marked `mine`, counts by state, `fee_bps`, the price bounds, the wallet's capacity, the **`acquisition_rule`** text (explicitly saying the cap limits CREATION, not a purchase) and a `rule` sentence covering cancellation, staleness, the price source and the fee split.
- **PERSISTENCE HONESTY (the file's most valuable paragraph, :206-228):** `persistBondedRegistryAfterMarket` writes the registry after a list/cancel/sale because otherwise a restart inside the 15-minute window "resurrected the SELLER as the owner of art a buyer had already bought". It then states the LIMIT: the ownership side becomes durable when the call returns, but **the ledger has its own independent snapshot and there is no cross-file atomic commit**, so a hard kill between the two writes can leave the money unsaved while the art moved — "that tear is AUDITABLE (BONDED_ASSET_SOLD carries buyer, price, fee, net)" and **closing it properly needs ONE atomic snapshot covering the ledger and the registry together, which is a persistence-architecture change recorded rather than claimed**. It is SYNCHRONOUS on purpose, and **a failed write is reported and never rolls the trade back** — "rolling back would be the worse lie".
- **HTTP (:578-718):** `handleBondedMarket` is a public GET; list/cancel/buy are POST-only with **4 KiB capped bodies and `DisallowUnknownFields`**, and each refusal message names why ("a client never names a seller, a status or a fee" / "the price is the listing's, never the buyer's"). Every refusal response carries `fee_bps` and the wallet's `capacity` so a refusal is actionable.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured; one is documented by the file itself):**
  1. **THE DOCUMENTED MONEY/OWNERSHIP TEAR** (:212-219) — an honest, unfixed architectural limit, cross-referenced in `Problems.md` §14.
  2. **THE VIEW IS ASSEMBLED FROM N SEPARATE SNAPSHOTS.** `BondedMarketView` takes ONE `ListingsSnapshot()`, then calls `Get(...)` (and `bondMarketStaleReason`, which calls `Get` again) **per active listing**, each taking and releasing `r.mu.RLock` independently — so a listing can change state mid-view. Each copy is released immediately, so this is a consistency-of-view observation rather than a corruption, but it is not one atomic read.
  3. **`CompleteSale` does not itself refuse a buyer == seller**; that guard lives in `BuyBondedListing` (:367-369). A second caller of `CompleteSale` would bypass it.
- **VERDICT:** owner — the market's arithmetic, its ordering, and its own stated durability limit.

### `black_market_service.go` — 1132 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the underworld economy — selling a card to the Black Market, the Dutch-auction seizure list, the 28-entry underworld contract catalogue + accept/abort, and the P2-B3 fenced-goods marketplace (list/buy/expire) (:14-16).
- **OWNS (money paths):** `HandleSellToBlackMarket` — price = **half** the card's power-derived estimate, then a fence fee (10 %, or 5 % for the `Fence` role) routed through `tokenSinkRouter.RouteCriminalTax("BLACK_MARKET_FENCE_FEE", …, {FaucetShare:1.0})` (:75-106, :220-224); `HandleBuyBlackMarket` — debit buyer, **credit `l.faucetBalanceMicro` directly**, `WantedLevel += 5`, inventory++, +50 XP via `trackCareerXPLocked` (:586-602); the fenced market — buyer pays `price + commission`, seller receives the price, the commission is routed to the sink, and the card moves between inventories (:1013-1060).
- **OWNS (career XP, already de-floated):** the Fence award is **integer permille end to end** — `loyaltyPermille` (+10/level, cap 500), `famePermille` (+100/level, cap 600), `GetVBVGatingPermille`, `ComputeXPWithBonusesPermille`, and the ally bonus is `ResolveRivalXPAward(...).Bonus` with the comment stating WHY: `rivalXP` "was the whole ATTACKER award and was therefore being paid a SECOND time" (:168-212). A duplicated `$VBV-gate` log block was deleted with the reason recorded (:214-215).
- **OWNS (contract state machine):** accept requires a **role gate per contract** (024 Fence, 025 Kidnapper, 026 Saboteur, 027 Smuggler, 028 Launderer), refuses while another contract is active, validates the id against a hand-written `switch`, then sets `ActiveUnderworldContractID`, bumps `WantedLevel`, audits and broadcasts (:617-775). Abort clears the contract and applies a **−50 reputation** penalty clamped at 0 (:779-809).
- **OWNS (Dutch auction):** the price starts at 75 % of the loan's repayment and **decays 5 % per hour in market, capped at 70 %** (≈5 % floor) — the same formula is written TWICE, once for the list and once for the buy (:505-527, :570-579).
- **HONEST SELF-STATEMENTS IN THE FILE:** the card is not really stored as a purchasable asset — "For now, we just 'burn' it into the black market, but a future feature could make it purchasable", which is why `HandleGetBlackMarket` lists `l.blackMarket` LOANS and not cards (:226-227, :513); the contract list is static and "In production, this would query a dynamic registry or database" (:251-252).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **A NIL-MAP WRITE PANIC ON A LIVE PATH.** `HandleBuyBlackMarket` reads `stats := l.leaderboard[wallet]` and immediately writes `stats.Inventory[...]++` (:590-591) with **NO existence check and NO `ensurePlayerStatsMapsInitialized`** — unlike `HandleAcceptUnderworldContract` (:626) and `HandleListFenceGoods` (:862-866). A wallet with a balance but no `PlayerStats` row panics here.
  2. **A SLICE-BOUNDS PANIC ON A WALLET SHORTER THAN 8 CHARACTERS.** The fenced sale's log AND its audit line both do `listing.SellerWallet[:8]` / `wallet[:8]` (:1050-1051), so a short wallet id (a test wallet, a custom id, `0xai…`-style bot wallet) panics at the line that exists to record the sale.
  3. **A CARD CAN BE SILENTLY LOST ON EXPIRY.** `cleanupExpiredFencedListings` deletes the listing **regardless** and returns the card only `if ok` (the seller's leaderboard row still exists) (:1086-1097) — so a seller whose stats row was cleared loses the card with no record and no compensation.
  4. **BARE ARRAYS AND `null` SLICES (the class the auction path was fixed for, NOT applied here).** `HandleGetUnderworldContracts` encodes a bare array (:481) and `HandleGetBlackMarket` encodes `var listings []blackMarketListing` **never `make`d** → `null` (:510, :536); `HandleGetFencedGoods` (:933, :942) and `HandleGetSellerListings` (:1122, :1131) do the same. Only the two `fencedListings == nil` early branches emit `[]` — the same endpoint answers `[]` or `null` depending on which branch runs.
  5. **THREE FLOATS ON MONEY/PRICE PATHS.** (a) the fence discount is `var fenceDiscount float64 = 1.0`, composed as `uint64(float64(fenceFeeMicro) * fenceDiscount)` (:85, :98-105) — float arithmetic on a fee that moves real money to the player; (b) the Dutch-auction price derives from `now.Sub(item.DueAt).Hours()` (float64) before `uint64(hoursInMarket)*5` (:516-527, :570-579); (c) the CONTRACT-024 gate compares `card.Rarity >= 1.5` (a float) to release a 30,000 $VBV payout (:150).
  6. **`CalculateReputation` IS CALLED WHILE HOLDING THE WRITE LOCK** at three sites in this file (:144, :593, :761) — a member of the recorded five-site family flagged as a self-lock candidate; the same pattern as the fixed `isJusticeAligned`/`TrackCareerXP` cases.
  7. **THE BLACK-MARKET BUY BYPASSES THE SINK ROUTER.** It credits `l.faucetBalanceMicro` directly (:587) instead of routing through `tokenSinkRouter`, and — unlike the sell path — does **not** update the `faucetBalance` float mirror, so the two representations of the faucet disagree after a purchase.
  8. **A CONTRACT PAYOUT IS CREDITED FROM NOWHERE.** `l.playerBalances[wallet] += 30000*1_000_000` for CONTRACT-024 (:151-157) with no counterparty debit (the following `applyDynamicScalingLocked()` re-scales rewards, it does not fund the payment) — the "no silent minting" rule of the Industrial Loop is not satisfied by this path.
  9. **THE CONTRACT TABLE AND THE VALIDATION `switch` ARE TWO HAND-MAINTAINED COPIES** of the same 28 ids — the source of the drift risk the file itself names (:251-252, :660-754).
- **VERDICT:** owner of the underworld economy, and the densest concentration of recorded defects the sweep has found: two crash paths, one silent asset loss, four `null`/bare-array answers, three floats, a sink bypass and a mint with no counterparty.

### `ai_citizen_engine.go` — 1731 lines
- **PURPOSE:** PILLAR 7-D — the AI citizens as first-class economic participants: a pool of autonomous citizens with careers, treasuries, pathways, relational lineage (BondGraph), region caps, a 1-minute behavioural tick, and their own persistence (:19-23).
- **OWNS:** `AICitizen` (wallet, career, tier, treasury, savings rate, investment threshold, learning XP, reputation, pathway, status, OriginWallet/OwnerWallet, attachment tier, challenge bond, region, BondGraph, §27.7.3 certified/black-market flags, §30 `Stats`/`BotLevel`/opinions, §32 faith fields) (:26-65) · `BondGraph` (:70-75) · the **12 §15-v3 pathways** with `aiPathwayByCareer` (:79-115) · `AICitizenEngine` with `citizens`/`lobby`/`mu`/`tickInterval`/`regionCounts` (:117-125) · the career action set (heist planning, laundering, fencing, smuggling, bounty hunting, recruitment, evidence analysis, cyber surveillance, generic) · `SpawnAI`/`SpawnBusiness`/`SaveCitizens`/`LoadCitizens` · the HTTP wrappers (`handleSpawnAI`, `handleGetAIStats`, `handleSpawnAIBusiness`, `handleMarryAI`, `handleBreedAI`, `handleAdoptPetAI`, `handleAIProgression`, `handleGetCitizens`).
- **THE LOCK DISCIPLINE IT NOW STATES, AND WHY (measured, not assumed):** `BehavioralTick` holds `ace.mu` for its whole body, so it MUST NOT acquire `lobby.mutex` — the Zen-Garden doors and `runAutonomousTournament` take the lobby lock and then reach INTO the engine, so a lobby lock taken in the tick would close the ABBA cycle, and "because request paths hold the lobby WRITE lock across their bodies, a queued writer is the NORMAL state here, so neither side would ever return" (:787-794). The fix is **snapshot → release → compute → apply**: `lobbySnapshotForTick` takes the lobby lock **ALONE** and copies `clubNameByOwner` + `wantedByWallet` as VALUES (:813-845), `behavioralTickLocked` runs under `ace.mu` and **RETURNS** the work (faith rituals, captured outlaws) (:885-1021), and `applyFaithRituals` / `applyCapturedOutlaws` take the lobby WRITE lock afterwards (:847-883). `executeEmployment` and `executeJusticeEnforcement` were converted for exactly this reason — and the file records that **the behavioural watchdog found them where the static gate could not**, because they take ANOTHER object's mutex (`ace.lobby.mutex`), "not one of its own receiver's fields" (:279-282, :319-326, :805-811).
- **THE `...Locked` PAIRS AND THEIR CALLER CONTRACTS are explicit:** `executeMarriageLocked` (:127-174), `executeBreeding` (self-locking door that DELAYS to `executeBreedingLocked`, :194-207) — each carrying a comment that taking the lock again "on a non-re-entrant `sync.RWMutex` … never returns and freezes the engine". `AddRitual`/`Meditate`/`AdjustReputation` **own their lock**, so a door never has to hold one (:1023-1075).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **THE ABBA PATH THE FILE'S OWN CONTRACT FORBIDS IS STILL THERE — MEASURED.** `triggerEntityInvestment` (:1248-1271) calls **`ace.lobby.handleInvestEntity(...)`**, and `handleInvestEntity` takes `l.mutex.Lock()` (`entity_investment_service.go:115`). It is reached from inside the tick through three career actions — `executeMarketActivity` (:1003-1005), `executeSmugglingRoute` (:1155-1157) and `executeCyberSurveillance` (:1228-1230) — so a tick acquires **`ace.mu` → `lobby.mutex`**, the exact order `BehavioralTick`'s own comment says must never happen. The lock-order gate cannot see it: the mutex belongs to ANOTHER object (`ace.lobby.mutex`), the same blindness the file itself documents for the two helpers that were fixed.
  2. **A LOBBY-OWNED MAP WRITE UNDER THE ENGINE LOCK.** `ace.lobby.playerBalances[citizen.Wallet] = citizen.Treasury` (:1264) runs with **only `ace.mu` held** — a write to a lobby-owned map that the persistence worker, every balance reader and every money door touch under `lobby.mutex` (the concurrent-map-access class, not a deadlock). It is also an **ASSIGNMENT, not a mirror**: whatever real balance that wallet held is DISCARDED in favour of the AI's internal treasury.
  3. **NON-DETERMINISM ACROSS THE WHOLE SIMULATION.** `math/rand` decides the career and the citizen's name (:589, :598), and the REWARD-BEARING outcomes: combat matchmaking (`rand.Float64() < prob`, :948), marriage 1-in-10 (:964), breeding 1-in-20 (:969), pet adoption 1-in-12 (:974), justice capture 1-in-15 (:982), rivalry 1-in-25 (:996), market activity 1-in-10 (:1003), career progression 1-in-30 (:1009), event hosting 1-in-40 (:456) — plus every INCOME (`rand.Intn(...)` in eight handlers) and the reputation jitter (`rand.Intn(5)-2`, `rand.Intn(11)-3`). The determinism mandate requires identical results across Go/WASM/browsers.
  4. **FLOATS ON LEDGER MONEY AND XP.** `uint64(float64(citizen.Treasury) * 0.1)` (:423), `* 0.5` (:1253), `* 0.25` (:1136), `* citizen.SavingsRate` (:1173), and `uint64(float64(contract.Template.XPBase) * citizen.SavingsRate)` for XP (:1099); `SavingsRate float64` is a STORED per-citizen field (:32) fed by `AICareerSavingsRates map[string]float64` (:486-500).
  5. **TREASURY IS MINTED WITH NO COUNTERPARTY.** Every career action ends in `citizen.Treasury += income` (heist :1102, fencing :1133, smuggling :1152, bounty :1170, recruitment :1196, evidence :1209, cyber :1225, generic :1239) and employment mints a salary with no club debit (:335-336). Nothing is debited anywhere; the "no silent minting" rule of the Industrial Loop is not met, and once such a treasury is mirrored into `playerBalances` the minted value becomes spendable lobby money.
  6. **A FLOAT SERVED IN A PAYLOAD.** `GetAIStats` computes `avg_reputation` as `float64` and returns it (:1435-1443).
  7. **AN XP GRANT FOR INTENT, AND A FLOAT IN THE ENVELOPE.** `matchAIWithHuman` awards `LearningXP += 15` for merely QUEUEING a match (:1483) and sends `{"priority":0.3}` — a float in a payload (:1470).
  8. **THE SPAWN DOOR READS THE BODY TWICE AND WRITES THE CITIZEN OUTSIDE THE LOCK.** `handleSpawnAI` decodes the JSON body once for the wallet and again for the options (:1503-1521), then applies `Career`/`Pathway` overrides directly to the live `citizen` pointer with **no engine lock held** (:1544-1553) — a data race against the tick that reads the same fields.
  9. **NOT VERIFIED (recorded as an open question):** whether `RemoveAI` decrements `regionCounts`, which `SpawnAI` and `executeBreedingLocked` increment (:247) — if it does not, a region's cap leaks permanently.
### `battle_service.go` — 2330 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the SERVER-AUTHORITY half of battle resolution — the power twin of the WASM engine, the capture/rule engine, the board hash, winner verification, the whole career-XP award ladder, wagering settlement, jailing, fatigue and item buffs (:1-14).
- **OWNS `getEffectiveServerPower`** (:16-130) — a deliberate line-for-line twin of the client's `getEffectivePower` so preview and authority agree: `Power[side] + Artifact`, then `Fallen −50`, `mood_catalyst +50`, coalition **+10 %** / regional **+5 %**, the **§26.4.3/§25.6.2 bonded-deck percentage** ("the percentage was snapshotted by initiatePairedMatch, so the WASM engine applies the very same integer to the very same base"), the **Wanted penalty mitigated by Cunning** (1 cunning = 2 points, clamped at the penalty), **fatigue above 50 reduced by Nurturing**, **loyalty ≥ 100 → +25**, the §32.3 faith delta, and `Elemental_sync` tile-mood ±50 with a four-cycle weakness table and a `grounded_shield` immunity.
- **OWNS `applyReligiousModifiers`** (:132-185) — `RivalFaithBlessing = 40`, `SameFaithWeaken = 30`, a non-leader faithful card +10 when its faith dominates the field, clamped to the same band; the comment states the rule: "Pure integer math — no float".
- **OWNS `ComputeBoardHash`** (:187-221) — sha256 over fixed-width **big-endian** id/owner/artifact per slot plus the acting player, explicitly "to ensure cross-platform parity between 64-bit Server and 32-bit WASM".
- **OWNS `serverCheckCaptures`** (:223-473) — direct comparison, `Power_copy` same-groups, `Power_up`/`Force_Plus_Trigger` plus-groups, a **COMBO chain-reaction queue** with de-duplication so a card is not double-counted for jailing, `Fallen_penalty` scars, the factional 110 % boosts (JUSTICE vs Fallen or Wanted ≥ 15; UNDERWORLD vs JUSTICE or clean ≤ 2) and the cumulative justice power bonus — returning the settled board hash.
- **OWNS `applyCapturePenalty`** (:475-498) — persists ONLY the Artifact scar into `l.inventory` + `persistentCardCache`, stripping transient `EquippedItems`, because "territory-based traps must not leak into the global inventory".
- **OWNS `verifyWinner`** (:499-1562) — scores board + unplayed hand cards; **sudden death on a 5-5 draw** (fatigue, pre-tie-break jail amnesty, redistribute); a tournament draw resolved by Reputation; draw history + recovery-streak reset; the justice missions (MISSION-001 ≥15 wanted / MISSION-004 club-affiliated loser), the AI challenge-bond settlement, the entire career-XP ladder, the wagering settlement, fatigue, buff expiry, both jailing rules, and deletion of the match.
- **THE CAREER-XP LADDER IT OWNS** (sixteen blocks): Heist Planner, Hostage Host, Lawyer-Commissioner, Arc-Net Operative, Smuggler, Launderer, TaxAuditor, Warden, AOS Leader, ForensicAnalyst, Sector Peacekeeper, BountyHunter, JusticeRecruiter, IntelAgent, Compliance Auditor, EthicsOverseer — each `base + captures × N`, `ComputeScaledXP`, rival/ally hooks, a promotion loop on `CareerLevel[...]` and a client notification.
- **OWNS the jailing rules** — `processPrisonerRuleLocked` (:1897-1981, the loser's RAREST card, gated on the winner being in the territory's club and the loser not) and `processFallenPenaltyJailLocked` (:1983-2100+, every captured card, with a `round-grid` dedup key and a **card-presence check** that "prevents attempting to jail board-only captures or causing negative inventory counts"), plus mojo gain, achievements and a CONTRACT-003 payout.
- **OWNS `initiateSuddenDeath`** (:1583-1670) — frequency-map redistribution of board cards, unplayed cards returned to their starters, fatigue reset persisted to inventory/cache, and a `handMetadata` sync so clients receive scars.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **A TEAM-SYNERGY AWARD IS BROADCAST TO EVERY KIDNAPPER ON THE SERVER.** Inside the Heist Planner block the comment says "if both in same organization", but the loop ranges **`l.leaderboard`** and pays `synergyXP` to ANY wallet holding the Kidnapper role (:866-876) — the only condition is that the winner's club id is non-empty. It also calls `ensurePlayerStatsMapsInitialized(otherWallet)` and writes `l.leaderboard[otherWallet] = otherStats` **while ranging the same map** (a write-during-iteration; Go may or may not visit an entry created mid-range).
  2. **TWO UNDERFLOW-SHAPED RIVAL AWARDS REMAIN.** `rivalBonus := uint64(int(rivalXP) - int(scaledXP))` followed by `if rivalBonus > 0` (:808-809) — the guard cannot protect, because a negative difference wraps to a huge uint64 that IS `> 0`; and `ComputeScaledXP(rivalXP-hpXP, …)` (:859) is passed an **unguarded** subtraction (contrast :1158 and :1203, which DO compare first). This is the class the rival-XP session repaired elsewhere.
  3. **FLOATS ON XP THROUGHOUT THE AWARD PATH.** `scaledXP := uint64(float64(baseXP) * vbvMultiplier)` where the multiplier is the FLOAT `GetVBVGatingMultiplier()` (:772-776); `uint64(float64(hpXP) * 0.15)` (:869); `uint64(float64(len(match.CapturedCards)) * 20.0)` (:1098); `faXP = uint64(float64(faXP) * evidenceAccuracy)` (:1126); `recruitBonus := uint64(float64(scaledJRP) * GetRecruitmentBonus())` (:1254); `visionBonus := uint64(float64(scaledIAP) * (decryptMult-1.0)/2.0)` (:1288); `wagerCleanXP := uint64(float64(match.WagersMicro) * 0.001 / 1000000.0)` (:1015) — and the INTEGER owner `ComputeScaledXPPermille` is **not used in this file at all** (~20 sites call the float-returning `ComputeScaledXP`). Floats also decide control flow: `if vbvMultiplier > 1.0` (:773), `if detentionBonus > 1.0` (:1084), `if evidenceAccuracy > 1.0` (:1125), `if decryptMult > 1.0` (:1287).
  4. **THE MATCH AUTHORITY IS NON-DETERMINISTIC AT THE ONE MOMENT THE BOARD IS REBUILT.** `rand.Shuffle` redistributes the sudden-death decks (:1633-1634), so the tie-breaker cannot be reproduced from the same inputs and cannot be mirrored by the WASM client — against the determinism mandate ("identical results across Go, WASM, Browsers"). `math/rand` is imported for this and nothing else.
  5. **A FLOAT MIRROR WRITTEN AS LOBBY STATE.** `l.faucetBalance = float64(l.faucetBalanceMicro) / 1000000.0` (:1536) — the same family as `auction_service.go`.
  6. **`CalculateReputation` UNDER THE WRITE LOCK** at :748 and :1893 (members of the recorded five-site family), and at :748 the result is **ADDED to the existing reputation** (`hStats.Reputation += l.CalculateReputation(hStats)`) — a doubling on a solo capture, not a recomputation.
  7. **PAYOUTS CREDITED WITH NO COUNTERPARTY.** `playerBalances[wallet] += 1200e6` (MISSION-001, :674), `+= 1000e6` (MISSION-004, :701), `+= 15000e6` (CONTRACT-028, :1750), `+= 1000e6` (CONTRACT-003, :1969) — each followed by `applyDynamicScalingLocked()`, which re-scales rewards rather than funding them.
  8. **THE SPECTATOR PAYOUT COMES FROM A MATCH FIELD, NOT AN ESCROW.** `netPayoutMicro := match.WagersMicro - siphonAmtMicro` is credited to the winner (:1527-1528) with no read of what was actually collected; on a draw only the 2 % siphon is routed (:1533). A divergence between `match.WagersMicro` and the collected pool would be invisible.
  9. **MISSPELLED SERVED AUDIT KEYS.** `CAREER_BOunTY_HUNTER_TRACKING_BONUS` (:798) and `RIVAL_INTEL_AGENT_ARCNET_OPERANTE` (:965) — a log filter keyed on the correct spelling will never match these lines.
  10. **DEAD BUT DOCUMENTED BRANCH:** `if false { _ = winnerWalletForSample }` (:1507-1509) — the superseded per-wallet liquidity sampling, left with its reason rather than deleted.
### `backend_types.go` — 750 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the server-side type home — network/session resilience types, the economic router and AMM types, verification/nonce/watchdog records, the §30 stat vector and the bonded-asset NFT records, the §25.10 rivalry calibration, and **`type Lobby struct` itself** (the field census the map gate, the self-lock gate and the record families all derive from). Two helpers live here too: `GetLevelLabelForDisplay` and `processMojoDecayLocked` (:640-750).
- **OWNS the session/network shapes:** `SessionState` (`CONNECTED` / `PENDING_DISCONNECT`), `PlayerSession` (+ a `context.CancelFunc` to halt eviction), `GracePeriodMatrix` (its own mutex + an `EvictionWorker` hook), `FrameDelta`/`SyncHandshaker` (sequence id, historical frames, last verified hash), `VerificationHook` (`ActiveNonces` + `ConsumedNonces` = replay protection), `SessionWatchdog`, `HostageSituation`, `VictimRegistry` (`ActiveKidnaps`: victim → attacker → situation, "eliminates the Immunity Exploit by allowing multiple attackers per victim"), `CyberInterceptEvent`, `RaidEvidence` (:16-160).
- **OWNS the economic types:** `RegionalGovernanceMetric`, `ClubTreasuryNode`, **`TokenSinkRouter`** (a `*uint64` reference to the faucet pool, the admin maintenance pool, `Ledger interface{}`, `ActiveClubs`, **`MarketNodes`**, `RegionalDistricts`, an audit reporter and a siphon notifier) and **`EntityMarketNode`** — the AMM: shares issued, reserve balance, cumulative yield per share, a dividend-freeze flag (:56-97).
- **OWNS the §25.10 calibration as NAMED CONSTANTS** (:399-424): `RIVAL_COLLISION_THRESHOLD = 40000` ("40 % signature overlap triggers auto-rivalry"), `RIVAL_SHOWCASE_BUFF_BPS = 5000`, `RIVAL_CACHE_BASE_MICRO = 50 $VBV`, `RIVAL_GOV_REP = 1000`, `RIVAL_WORKER_BONUS_CAP`, `RIVAL_CREATOR_MULT = 1200`, plus the eight asset-class weights `W_*` (bps×1000) — with `AssetSignature`, `RegionRivalry` and `RivalryEngine` (its own mutex + `Signatures` + `Rivalries`).
- **OWNS the §30/§26.4/§25.6/§25.7 asset records:** `EntityStats` with `StatSum()` and `DominantStat()` (pure integer 1..100, "never float (constitutional ledger rule)"), `PetNFT` (lineage + a grooming ladder keyed by stat axis), `VehicleNFT` (the part ladder, a break-in window, §27.7.3 provenance), `WorldContentNFT`, `RegionView` (+ the power overlay).
- **OWNS THE `Lobby` FIELD CENSUS — the most load-bearing read of this file.** It is where every gate's subject comes from: the money surface (`playerBalances`, `faucetBalanceMicro` **and its `float64` mirror** `faucetBalance`, `maxFaucetCapacity`, `GlobalFaucetPool`/`AdminMaintenancePool`), `leaderboard map[string]PlayerStats` (**VALUES**), `inventory`/`persistentCardCache`, the record-relevant maps (`pets`, `vehicles`, `worldContent`, `fencedListings`, `loans`, `auctions`, `clubs`, `matchHistory`, `linkedWallets`, `onboardedWallets`, `registeredTxIDs`, `pendingTxIDs`, `rewardStack`, `rewardTokens`, `treasuryAverages`), the PER-FIELD mutexes (`mutex`, `fencedListingsMu`, `counterfeitRateLimitMu`, `envoiMutex`, `counterfeitRateLimiterMu`) and the ~40 service pointers (:491-638).
- **THE ALIASED MAP IS DECLARED HERE, PLAINLY:** `marketNodes map[string]*EntityMarketNode` (:516) sits a few lines from `tokenSinkRouter *TokenSinkRouter` (:517), whose own `MarketNodes` (:78) is **the same map** — the hazard `market_nodes_alias_gate_test.go` exists to measure.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **FLOATS ON THE LOBBY'S OWN MONEY SURFACE.** `tournamentPotBonus float64` and `pendingTournamentPayouts float64` (:498-499) exist BESIDE the integer twins `tournamentPotBonusMicro`/`pendingTournamentPayoutsMicro` (:494-495); `faucetBalance float64` (:586) is the float mirror of `faucetBalanceMicro`; `maxFaucetCapacity float64` (:596); `RewardRatio float64` (documented as "current scaling ratio for transparency" — :626); and `treasuryAverages map[string]float64` (:621).
  2. **FLOATS ON RATES THAT DECIDE ECONOMIC OUTCOMES.** `RegionalGovernanceMetric.CustomTaxRate float64` (a TAX RATE), `EntityMarketNode.ReserveRatio float64` (the AMM's pricing ratio, "e.g. 0.33"), `CyberInterceptEvent.DecryptBonus float64` (which scales a career award), `RaidEvidence.Confidence float64` (:61, :91, :146, :159).
### `justice_handlers.go` — 425 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the Justice Hegemony Dashboard's HTTP presentation layer — "Business logic remains in justice_service.go; this file owns only HTTP I/O" (:3-5).
- **OWNS:** the DTOs (`JusticeDashboardAPI` with camelCase json tags, `BountyItemJSON`, `UseTruthSerumRequest`, `CaptureBountyRequest`, `APIResponseJSON` + reconciled fields), `JusticeHandlers` (lobby + `justiceService` + `playerService` pointers), the resolver `resolveWalletToPlayerID` (lowercases, then `wallets[wallet]`, then the leaderboard key itself; `""` when neither), the non-blocking broadcast helper (a `select` with a `default` that SKIPS a slow client rather than blocking the broadcast), `getDashboardForPlayer`, and the endpoint handlers — dashboard, use-truth-serum, capture-bounty, bounty-board (an alias to the dashboard), award-card and use-rep-shield (:22-425).
- **STATED BEHAVIOURS:** the dashboard's `wallet` is **OPTIONAL** — without one it answers a global view ("e.g. the Underworld bounty board"), so the World Dashboard's wallet-less GET succeeds with 200; with an unknown wallet it answers **404 "player not found"** (:166-208). Defaults are named constants (`ReputationShieldDefaultProtection`, `TruthSerumDefaultDuration`, `JusticeOutlawBonusThreshold`).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **`handleCaptureBounty` PAYS NOTHING — and never resolves a caller.** It decodes a `targetWallet` from the BODY, computes a `reward`, answers `200 {"success":true,"reward":N}`, broadcasts `bounty_updated` and logs "Bounty captured: … (reward: N micro-VBV)" — but there is **no balance mutation, no audit entry and no state change anywhere in the handler**, and no caller identity is read at all. A success response and a log line reporting money that never moved (contrast `black_market_service.go`'s `BountyRewardMicro`, which at least records an amount).
  2. **THESE DOORS TRUST THE BODY FOR THE SUBJECT.** `handleUseTruthSerum` applies a truth serum to the `targetWallet` named in the body; `handleAwardJusticeCard` awards a justice card to the `player_id` named in the body; `handleApplyRepShield` applies a reputation shield to the `player_id` named in the body. None of the three resolves or verifies the CALLER, so any client can name any player — the only gate is the route's rate limit.
  3. **A READ LOCK TAKEN AROUND A CALL INTO THE SERVICE.** `getDashboardForPlayer` holds `h.lobby.mutex.RLock()` and then calls `justiceService.GetJusticeFaction` / `GetRevealedBuffs` / `GetShieldRemaining` (:150-160); `handleCaptureBounty` does the same around `GetJusticeFaction` (:285-300). If those service methods take the lobby lock themselves this is the **recursive-read** class the self-lock gate now FAILS on — worth confirming against `justice_service.go`.
  4. **THE BOUNTY NUMBER IS DERIVED TWICE, DIFFERENTLY.** The dashboard builds its list from `len(stats.Wanted)` and a reward of `wantedLevel * 100_000` micro (:192-205), while `handleCaptureBounty` pays `faction.BountyRank * 500_000` with a 1.5 $VBV default (:305-310) — two independent derivations of "the bounty" for one target.
  5. **A FIXED-SCALE PERCENTAGE.** `PowerBonus = int(totalPower / 10000)` with the comment "Convert micro-units to percentage points (simplified)" (:155-157).
  6. **THE ENVELOPE TYPE CARRIES NO EVENT NAME.** `broadcastJusticeEvent` sets `Envelope.Type = "justice_event"` and puts the real type INSIDE the payload (:96-120), so a client must parse the payload to route (every other family uses the envelope type).
- **VERDICT:** owner of the justice HTTP surface — carrying one endpoint that reports a reward it never pays, three endpoints that act on a body-named subject with no caller check, and two recursive-read candidates.

### `ethereum_client.go` — 450 lines
- **PURPOSE:** PILLAR-A's multi-chain trust anchor — a load-balanced Ethereum **node pool** with health monitoring, failover, blacklisting and vault-key signing (:22-23).
- **OWNS:** `EthereumClient` (a token slice, an `activeIdx`, a per-index blacklist map with expiry, a retry backoff, the vault address + an `ecdsa.PrivateKey`, and atomic health / last-block / last-error fields) · `NewEthereumClient` (dial each URL, verify connectivity with a `ChainID` call, load the vault from **ENV ONLY** `ETH_VAULT_KEY` / `ETH_VAULT_ADDRESS`, start the monitor) · `startHealthMonitor` (30 s ticker) and `checkHealth` · `GetClient` / `GetVaultAddress` / `IsHealthy` · `SendETH` (a legacy 21000-gas tx signed with EIP-155) · `SendERC20` (an inline minimal `transfer(address,uint256)` ABI, a 65000-gas bound) · `BatchTransferETH` · `GetBalance` (Wei → micro by an exact `big.Int` divide by 1e12, with an `IsUint64` overflow refusal) · `GetNonce` / `GetBlockNumber` / `GetTransactionReceipt`.
- **HONESTY OF THE SHAPE:** every read refuses when no node is healthy, validates hex addresses/hashes before use, and the vault key comes from the environment — never from a file or a request.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **A WRONG-CHAIN NODE STAYS IN THE POOL.** `NewEthereumClient` appends the client to `ec.tokens` at :63 **before** the chain-id comparison at :67, so a node that answers a non-mainnet chain id is appended and then merely LOGGED as "skipping" — it remains eligible for `GetClient()` and therefore for real settlement. The log says the node was skipped; the code kept it.
  2. **`activeIdx` IS AN INDEX INTO THE OLD SLICE.** `checkHealth` compacts `ec.tokens` into `newHealthyTokens` while recording `newActiveIdx = uint64(i)` from the ORIGINAL loop index, then assigns both — so after any heal pass that drops a node, the selected client can be a DIFFERENT node than the one measured as best (and `GetClient` silently clamps an out-of-range index to 0). The two fields are indexed against different arrays.
  3. **A COMMENT THAT CONTRADICTS ITS CODE.** `BatchTransferETH`'s doc says "multiple ETH transfers from a single transaction using a custom contract" and "Returns the gas cost in micro-units for economic reconciliation" — the body loops one full `SendETH` per recipient (separate transactions, separate nonces) and returns no gas cost.
  4. **DORMANT OUTBOUND RAIL.** `SendETH` / `SendERC20` / `BatchTransferETH` have no caller in any core flow (measured earlier in this repository, and the Algorand outbound twin was deliberately removed), so this is a live-looking surface with no live path.
  5. Floats appear only in log formatting (`float64(amountMicro)/1e6`), not in the amounts.
- **VERDICT:** owner of the Ethereum node pool — carrying a wrong-chain node that survives its own check and an `activeIdx` measured against a slice that no longer exists in that shape.


### `rate_limiter.go` — 457 lines
- **PURPOSE:** PILLAR 1-C — rate limiting and DDoS mitigation: a token bucket **plus** a sliding window, per-tier, per-wallet and per-IP (:18-21).
- **OWNS:** `RateLimitTier` (MaxTokens/RefillRate/WindowDuration/MaxRequests — floats are correct here: this is a limiter, not the ledger) · `WalletQuota` (+ `IsAdmin` and an IP fallback) · `RateLimiterService` (`Tiers`, `WalletQuotas`, `AdminWallets`, a `sync.Map` of client IPs, and a Prometheus counter) · the eleven default tiers (`auth` 5 burst / 1-per-12 s; `economy`; `economy-tight` 3; `core-economy`; `standard`; `wallet-default` 30 burst / 1 s refill; `achievement`; `underworld`; `admin` 2 burst / 1-per-30 s; `default` 20) · `NewRateLimiterService` / `SetWalletQuota` / `SetClientIP` · `getClientIP` (X-Forwarded-For → X-Real-IP → RemoteAddr host) and `getWalletFromRequest` (header → `?wallet=`) · `resolveKey` / `resolveTier` / `Allow` · `Middleware` and `WithRateLimit` · `formatRetryAfter` · `CleanupStaleEntries` + `cleanupLocked` · `GetStatus`.
- **THE ALGORITHM IS HONEST AND EXPLAINED:** the sliding window is checked FIRST ("prevents burst overflow within window"), then the token bucket, with an elapsed-time leak refilled to `MaxTokens`; a refusal returns a computed `retryAfter` (`deficit / RefillRate`).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **`WithRateLimit`'s TIER ARGUMENT IS DECORATIVE.** It builds `tierKey := "tier:" + tierName` but then calls **`rls.Allow(r)`**, which re-derives the key from the REQUEST via `resolveKey` — so the tierName never reaches the decision and is used only to label the refusal (`X-RateLimit-Limit`). A route wrapped as `WithRateLimit(h, "economy-tight")` is actually limited by the wallet quota or, absent one, by the **path fallback**: `resolveKey` maps ANY `/api/…` path to `tier:economy` (:245-248). Every per-route tier name in the route tables is therefore unenforced, and `economy-tight`/`underworld`/`achievement`/`admin` differ from `economy` only if a wallet quota says so.
  2. **A LOBBY-OWNED MAP IS WRITTEN UNDER THE SERVICE'S MUTEX.** `Allow` and `cleanupLocked` mutate **`rls.Lobby.httpRateLimits`** (a `Lobby` field) while holding `rls.Mu` — one map, a second mutex candidate: the same shape as the aliased market map, and the reason `httpRateLimits` needs a stated owner.
  3. **A HEADER/TAG MISMATCH ON REFUSAL.** Both middleware paths set `Content-Type: application/json` and then call `http.Error`, which overwrites it with `text/plain` — the JSON body is served with the wrong content type.
  4. `resolveKey`'s admin branch returns `skip = true` (unlimited), which is the documented intent ("Task 3105: infinite quota for admin wallets") but also means an admin wallet's requests are never counted anywhere.
- **VERDICT:** owner of the rate-limiting algorithm — carrying a real enforcement gap (the declared per-route tier is not applied) and an undeclared second owner for a lobby map.



### `religion_governance.go` — 458 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the religion-governorship system: a **faucet-owned chain store capped at 24 religions server-wide**, where users buy a governorship, a buyout is a TOTAL LOSS for the previous owner (no commission, no safety net), a converted user can only return by buying out another religion, and on a buyout the buyer chooses to restore the old religion or create a new one (:15-20).
- **OWNS:** `MaxReligionsPerServer = 24`, `DefaultReligionPrice = 5 $VBV` · `Religion` (id, name, dogma, governor, last price paid, members, rivalries, faith power, coherence, ritual count, region, created/last-buyout times) · `ReligionGovernance` (`mu` + a religions map) · `InitializeFaithReligions` (24 named, dogma-cycled, faucet-owned) · `AddRitual` / `AddRivalry` · `GetHighTierRivals` · `Save`/`Load` · `ResolveFaithWar` (higher `FaithPower` wins; the loser's members AUTO-CONVERT to the winner and the loser's ritual count transfers) · the HTTP doors (`handleFaithReligionRitual`, `handleFaithReligionRivalry`, `handleFaithHighTier`).
- **STATE OWNERSHIP — RECORDED:** the religions live in a **package-level global** `var religionGov = &ReligionGovernance{…}` (:50-52), not on the `Lobby` — so it is one of the states outside the Lobby struct that the record set has to reach deliberately rather than by carrying a Lobby field. Whether the `faith` record family actually carries it must be read in `faith_church.go`'s entry (the family's writer is `FaithChurchEngine.Save`); the family's `Carries` text says "religions", so this is the file to verify it against when that entry is written.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **A MONEY DOOR THAT TAKES NO LOBBY LOCK.** `handleFaithReligionRitual` reads and writes the lobby's balance maps **without `l.mutex`** (`if l.playerBalances[wallet] < fee` → `-=` → `l.faucetBalanceMicro += fee`, :363-370) — an unfenced lobby map read+write from an HTTP door, the exact class `map_race_gate_test.go` exists to measure, and a torn read/write against every other money path.
  2. **THE FEE IS CHARGED BEFORE THE SERVICE CAN REFUSE, AND IS NOT REFUNDED.** The debit and the faucet credit happen FIRST; `religionGov.AddRitual` is called afterwards and, when it fails, the handler answers an error while the money has already moved (:371-374) — a taken fee with no service delivered and no reversal.
  3. **`Save` IS NOT ATOMIC AND HAS NO CHAIN MIRROR.** `religions.json` is written with a plain `os.WriteFile` while holding only `rg.mu.RLock()` (:400-408) — no temp-file swap (the registries all use tmp + rename), no file mutex, and **no `saveBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked` dispatch**, so religion state has no transport mirror even though the `faith` family claims to carry "religions".
  4. **`Load` SWALLOWS EVERY ERROR.** A missing file and a corrupt file both return `0` with no log (:410-419), so a truncated registry is indistinguishable from a fresh install.
  5. **A RIVALRY CAN BE DECLARED BY ANYONE.** `handleFaithReligionRivalry` takes both ids from the body and never resolves the caller, so any client can declare one religion a rival of another.
- **VERDICT:** owner of the 24-slot religion store — carrying an unsynchronised money door, a fee taken before its service can refuse, a non-atomic unmirrored save, and a silent load failure.


### `creator_store_service.go` — 470 lines
- **PURPOSE:** PILLAR 7-C — the creator storefront: product registration, listing, a primary purchase, a secondary sale with a creator royalty, ratings and a bounded royalty ledger (:12-59).
- **OWNS:** `CreatorStoreProduct` (id, creator, name, description, price in **micro-VBV**, DLC links, category, tags, sales count) · `CreatorProfile` (bio, display name, average rating, rating count, lifetime revenue, product count) · `RoyaltyTransaction` (product, buyer, seller, creator, rate, amount, royalty paid) · `CreatorStore` (`products`, `creatorProfiles`, a bounded `royaltyTxns` slice, a history cap) · the constants (`DefaultRoyaltyRate 0.10`, `MaxProductTags 8`, name/description/bio caps, `RatingMultiplier 1000.0`, `MaxRoyaltyHistoryEntries 1000`, four categories, `MinRoyaltyRate`/`MaxRoyaltyRate`/`DefaultRoyaltyPct`, `PlatformFeeRate = 0.10`) · `CreateProduct` / `ListProducts` / `GetProduct` / `PurchaseProduct` / `ProcessSecondarySale` / `GetCreatorProfile` / `GetRoyaltyHistory` / `RateProduct` / `DeactivateProduct` / `ReactivateProduct` · the helpers `sanitizeString`, `clampAndDedupTags`, `clampUint64`, `pruneRoyaltyHistory`, `min`.
- **THE DECLARED INTENT IS INTEGER:** `PriceMicroVBV uint64` ("uint64 precision"), `TotalRevenueMicroVBV uint64`, and the constants block's own comment — "Royalty rate bounds (float64 for UI only; uint64 enforced server-side)" (:77-80).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **THE MONEY NEVER MOVES — THE ENTIRE CREATOR ECONOMY IS ACCOUNTING ONLY.** `PurchaseProduct` computes `platformFee` and `creatorRevenue` and then updates a PROFILE COUNTER (`TotalRevenueMicroVBV += creatorRevenue`), bumps `SalesCount` and appends a record — there is **no `playerBalances` debit, no credit, no sink routing, no faucet credit and no balance check on the buyer anywhere** (:217-268). `ProcessSecondarySale` is identical, and it makes the discard EXPLICIT: `sellerNetRevenue := salePriceMicroVBV - royaltyAmount` followed by **`_ = sellerNetRevenue`** (:282-284). A buyer is never charged and a seller is never paid; only a counter moves.
  2. **A `Lock`/`RUnlock` MISMATCH THAT PANICS.** `RateProduct` takes `cs.mu.Lock()` and defers **`cs.mu.RUnlock()`** (:371-372) — on a `sync.RWMutex` the deferred release is a write-unlock of a write-held mutex, so Go panics ("sync: RUnlock of unlocked RWMutex") on every rating.
  3. **THE RUNNING AVERAGE DRIFTS.** `oldRatingFloat := profile.AvgRating * RatingMultiplier`, then `uint64(oldRatingFloat)` TRUNCATES before the new total is formed, and the new average is a float division (:387-392) — so each rating shaves the accumulated total toward zero, while the struct comment claims "uint64 precision".
  4. **FLOATS ON MONEY, EXACTLY WHERE THE COMMENT SAYS THEY ARE NOT.** `uint64(float64(price) * PlatformFeeRate)` (:229) and `uint64(float64(salePriceMicroVBV) * royaltyRate)` (:281) — float multiplication of a ledger amount, with `RoyaltyRate float64` persisted on the transaction record.
  5. **THREE `wallet[:8]` PANIC SITES.** The default display name is built as `fmt.Sprintf("Creator_%s", creatorWallet[:8])` in `CreateProduct`, `PurchaseProduct` and `ProcessSecondarySale` — a wallet shorter than 8 characters panics at each (the same defect recorded in `black_market_service.go`).
  6. **READS HAND OUT LIVE POINTERS.** `GetProduct` and `ListProducts` return the registry's own `*CreatorStoreProduct` under an `RLock` that is then released, so a caller can mutate stored state with no lock — the opposite of the copy-on-read discipline the bonded-asset registry adopted for exactly this reason.
  7. `nextProductID` is declared (:85) and never used (`CreateProduct` takes a caller-supplied id), and a local `min()` shadows the Go 1.21 builtin (:465-470).
- **VERDICT:** owner of the creator store — a surface whose every money path is a counter, whose rating path panics, and whose own comment forbids the float arithmetic it performs.
### `resilience_utils.go` — 472 lines
- **PURPOSE:** PILLAR 4 — network resilience: a load-balanced node cluster with health tracking, blacklisting/cooldown, rate-limit counting and a health report; plus the multi-chain router whose OUTBOUND rail is now deliberately one-way (:22-45, :118-145).
- **OWNS:** `ManagedNode` (URL, client, latency, last block, blacklist flag + last-error time, a rate-limit counter and window, `MaxRateLimits`, a block-sync-lag threshold, an HTTP-status histogram) · `LoadBalancedLedgerClient` (nodes, `ProductionMode`, `RateLimitCooldown`, a success counter) · `NodeHealthReport` · `NewLoadBalancedClient` / `SetProductionMode` (stricter thresholds in production: 3 rate limits, 10 s sync lag) · `GetHealthReport` · `MultiChainRouter` (Voi, Algorand and an ETH client, the asset/app ids, and the vault addresses/mnemonics) · **`TransferToChain` / `selectChain` / `transferToAlgorandMainnet` / `transferToVoi`** · `ReportHTTPStatus` / `RecordSuccess` · `copyHTTPCodes` / `formatLastError` · `GetBestClient` / `GetBestNode` · `UnmarkNode` / `MarkNodeFailure` · `IsSemantic404Error` · `RunHealthMonitor` / `performSyncCheck`.
- **THE OPERATOR ADJUDICATION IS IMPLEMENTED EXACTLY AS RECORDED (2026-09-19, plan §1), and the comments say why:** `TransferToChain` REFUSES a non-Voi hint ("rail %q is not $VBV on Voi; there is no other outbound rail"), `selectChain` **takes no hint by design** and answers `"voi"` only when a Voi client with nodes exists, and `transferToAlgorandMainnet` is a kept REFUSAL — "kept rather than deleted so a future caller receives a STATED REFUSAL instead of a compile error that invites someone to re-implement the rail" — while the Algorand CLIENT stays configured for INBOUND verification and native VOI may leave only as the onboarding gas stipend (:146-215).
- **`IsSemantic404Error`** classifies a node answer as authoritative by `strings.HasPrefix(msg, "HTTP 404")` — a STRING match on an error message, documented as such, so a caller can treat "the datum does not exist" as healthy rather than blacklisting the node (:416-424).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **A HEALTH REPORT THAT CALLS A CLEAN NODE UNHEALTHY.** `GetHealthReport` computes `Healthy: !node.IsBlacklisted && time.Since(node.LastErrorTime) < time.Hour` (:113-121) while `NewLoadBalancedClient` never initialises `LastErrorTime` — so for a node that has NEVER failed, `time.Since(zeroTime)` is ~2000 years and the second clause is false, i.e. **`healthy:false` is reported for a perfectly healthy node** until its first error. (`performSyncCheck` uses the opposite, correct convention: no error ⇒ clear the flag.)
  2. **`GetBestNode` CAN PANIC, HAS A DEAD CHECK, AND CAN RETURN A BLACKLISTED NODE.** `best := lb.Nodes[0]` panics on an empty pool; `if best == nil || …` is dead (the index already assigned a value); and when EVERY node is blacklisted the loop `continue`s for all of them, so `best` remains `lb.Nodes[0]` — a blacklisted node returned as "best". Its sibling `GetBestClient` handles that case explicitly (a degraded-mode fallback and an error when no node qualifies), so the two selectors do not agree, and `transferToVoi`'s `if bestNode == nil` check can never fire (:370-386, :340-366).
  3. **EVERY VOI PAYOUT IS LABELLED A DIVIDEND.** `transferToVoi` attaches **`NotePrefixVoiDividend`** as the note for EVERY transfer through this rail (:254) — and the vocabulary's own catalogue entry for it is `{Key: "voi_dividend", Prefix: "VBT_VOI_DIV:", Scope: audit_log, Description: "Voi-side dividend payout record from the multi-chain router"}` (`note_vocabulary.go:135/254`). Since `TransferToChain` refuses every other rail, this ONE label is the on-chain purpose for a dividend, a bounty, a tournament payout and anything else that pays out — the note vocabulary cannot distinguish them.
  4. `selectChain` decides on `len(Nodes) > 0` only — it never consults health or the blacklist, so "no healthy Voi node" is discovered later by `GetBestNode`/dispatch rather than at the rail decision (:190-196).
  5. `NewLoadBalancedClient` silently `continue`s when an endpoint cannot be constructed, so a dropped URL is never reported.
  6. `RunHealthMonitor` and `UnmarkNode` have no caller (measured earlier in this repository) — the 5-minute/`RateLimitCooldown` expiry inside the selectors is what actually recovers a node.
- **VERDICT:** owner of node resilience and of the (now single) outbound rail — carrying a health report that inverts its own predicate for a clean node, a selector that can panic or hand back a blacklisted node, and one note label for every payout.
### `faith_church.go` — 487 lines
- **PURPOSE:** the faith-church storefront: a player opens a church (24-cap, buyouts to the faucet, total loss for the old owner), sells items, performs rituals and is ranked by faith power (:15-17).
- **OWNS:** `Church` (owner, religion id, dogma, faith power, members, opening cost, ritual fees, tournament pot, region, active) · `ChurchItem` (price, category, effect) · `ChurchRitual` (performer, type, cost, effect) · `FaithChurchEngine` (`mu` + churches/items/rituals maps) · `OpenChurch` / `AddMember` / `RemoveMember` / `AddItem` / `GetItems` / `PerformRitual` / `GetRituals` / `GetLeaderboard` / `GetChurch` / `GetChurchesByOwner` / `GetChurchesByRegion` · `Save` / `Load` · the HTTP doors (`handleChurchOpen`, `handleChurchGet`, `handleChurchesByOwner`, `handleChurchesByRegion`, `handleChurchAddMember`, `handleChurchRemoveMember`, `handleChurchAddItem`, `handleChurchItems`, `handleChurchRitual`, `handleChurchRituals`, `handleChurchLeaderboard`).
- **STATE:** the engine is a **package-level global** (`var faithChurchEngine = &FaithChurchEngine{…}`), and beside it sits a SECOND global — **`globalLobbyRef`** with `SetGlobalLobbyRef`/`getGlobalLobby` — used so a method that does NOT receive the lobby can still reach `l.clubs` (:69-79).
- **`Save` DISPATCHES THE CHAIN MIRROR FROM INSIDE ITS OWN READ LOCK, and says so:** the payload is `{churches, items, rituals}`, written to `faith_churches.json`, then `l.saveBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked(NotePrefixFaithSnapshot, data)` — "the faith RLock is STILL HELD here (a deferred release), so the mirror is marshalled under the SAME lock that guards these maps" (:82-101). (Contrast the bonded registry, which RELEASES before dispatching.)
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **`PerformRitual` TAKES NO MONEY AND RAISES FAITH POWER FROM A CLIENT-DECLARED NUMBER.** `church.FaithPower += cost / 10` where `cost` is the `Cost` field of the request body (:458-476, :295-317) — nothing is debited, nothing is validated, so a POST of `{"cost": 999999999}` grants 99,999,999 faith power for free. Faith power feeds the church leaderboard, which feeds the theme/region signals.
  2. **A CHURCH'S `opening_cost` IS ALSO CLIENT-DECLARED AND NEVER CHARGED.** `handleChurchOpen` passes the body's `opening_cost` straight into `OpenChurch`, which stores it as `OpeningCost` with no debit anywhere.
  3. **A LOCK-ORDER EDGE: `faithChurchEngine.mu → Lobby.mutex`.** `OpenChurch` holds `fce.mu.Lock()` for its whole body and then takes `l.mutex.Lock()` to create the mirror `Club{Type:"Faith"}` (:159-178) — a cross-mutex edge the order gate should be asked about (the reverse direction exists elsewhere, e.g. `bonded_branding.go`'s church case reads this engine under its own lock).
  4. **THE `faith` RECORD FAMILY'S "religions" CLAIM IS NOT SATISFIED BY THIS WRITER.** The snapshot is `{churches, items, rituals}` — **`religionGov.Religions` (religion_governance.go's global) is not in it** — so religion state has a `religions.json` file and NO transport mirror while the family's `Carries` text names "religions". Recorded as a measured record-set gap.
  5. **`Save` IS NOT ATOMIC** (`os.WriteFile`, no temp swap, no file mutex) and `Load` swallows every error; `AddItem` and `PerformRitual` never check that the church exists (an item can be added to a nonexistent church; a ritual against one is still recorded as a success), and `GetChurch`/`GetItems`/`GetRituals`/`GetLeaderboard` hand out **live pointers** after releasing the lock.
  6. **BODY-NAMED SUBJECTS WITH NO CALLER CHECK:** anyone can add ANY wallet to ANY church (`handleChurchAddMember`), remove one, or add an item with a body-supplied price to any church.
- **VERDICT:** owner of churches and rituals — carrying a free faith-power mint from a body field, an unchallenged client-declared opening cost, a cross-lock edge, and a record-set claim its writer does not satisfy.
### `onboarding_service.go` — 475 lines
- **PURPOSE:** PILLAR 5/4 — new-player onboarding and the session watchdog: the Voi "starter pack" bridge (1 VOI + 1 $VBV), the console-voucher conversion funnel, an identity refresh fee, and a continuous session auditor that evicts on age or liquidity (:76-100, :129-133).
- **OWNS the WATCHDOG:** `NewSessionWatchdog` / `StartWatchdogEngine` (a 10-minute audit ticker) · `TrackSession` / `UntrackSession` (lowercased keys) · `AuditActivePlayerSessions` — it copies the tracked wallets under the lock, then per player checks a **24-hour session limit** and runs a **liquidity audit in a goroutine** that fans out across the configured Voi nodes and calls `DisconnectClient(…, "INSUFFICIENT_LIQUIDITY")` when the native-VOI balance is **below 100000 micro-VOI** (:95-125).
- **OWNS the BRIDGE (`HandleVoiOnboarding`), implemented exactly as the operator adjudication requires and NAMED as such:** the dispatch is ONE ATOMIC GROUP of two transactions from the faucet/vault account — a native-VOI payment carrying `NotePrefixOnboard+"GAS"` and an ARC-200 `transfer` of 1 $VBV carrying `NotePrefixOnboard+"TOKEN"` — with a shared group id; the comment states "THE ONE PERMITTED NATIVE-VOI DEPARTURE (operator adjudication 2026-09-19, plan §1) … Every other value payout is $VBV on Voi, and the multi-chain router REFUSES a non-Voi rail" (:320-340). The Sybil/duplicate machinery around it is explicit: a `SybilSyncComplete` guard, a per-wallet in-flight claim in `processingOnboarding`, a **global `onboardingSemaphore` with a 10-second acquisition timeout**, an `onboardedWallets` historical check, an on-chain native-VOI pre-check (≥ 1 VOI ⇒ `204 No Content`, no pack), a **vault reservation taken atomically and REFUNDED on failure**, and `onboardedWallets[...] = true` set only AFTER a txid is returned (:134-250, :305-310).
- **OWNS the funnel:** `HandleVoucherConversion` harvests `ArenaVouchers` only from links whose `Verified` flag is set, ZEROES each source as it harvests ("Integer Supremacy: clear the source balance to prevent double-claiming"), then credits `playerBalances` 1:1 and re-checks solvency via `applyDynamicScalingLocked` (:378-437). `HandleIdentityRefresh` charges a flat **100 $VBV** and routes 90 % faucet / 10 % club through the token-sink router (:443-475).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed):**
  1. **SIGNING ERRORS ON THE ONBOARDING MONEY PATH ARE DISCARDED.** `_, stx1, _ := crypto.SignTransaction(...)` and the same for `stx2`, then `client.SendRawTransaction(append(stx1, stx2...))` (:347-350) — a signing failure leaves zero-value transaction bytes that are still submitted, and the error is never surfaced.
  2. **A BALANCE THRESHOLD GATES SESSION CONTINUITY.** The watchdog evicts a live session purely on a native-VOI balance below 0.1 VOI (:117-122), i.e. a player holding under the gas floor is disconnected mid-session — an economic gate on ACCESS (not on a feature), delivered with no in-game notice beyond the eviction payload.
  3. **A PARSE ERROR IS DISCARDED ON A FEE ROUTE.** `HandleIdentityRefresh` derives the club id with `ParseUint(strings.TrimPrefix(stats.EmployerClubID, "CLUB-"), 10, 64)` and ignores the error (:466), so any employer id not shaped `CLUB-<n>` silently yields club id 0 and the 10 % club share is routed to club zero.
  4. **FLOAT MIRRORS ARE WRITTEN ON EVERY MONEY STEP HERE TOO** (`l.faucetBalance = float64(l.faucetBalanceMicro) / 1000000.0` at the reservation, at the refund and at the identity refresh) — the same float-on-the-lobby family recorded for `auction_service.go` and `battle_service.go`.
  5. `AuditActivePlayerSessions` spawns an unbounded goroutine per tracked wallet per audit; each takes its own 10-second timeout context.
- **VERDICT:** owner of onboarding and the session watchdog — the one place in the tree where the single permitted native-VOI departure is implemented and NAMED, carrying two ignored errors on money paths and a balance-gated session rule.








  3. **`processMojoDecayLocked` IS A FLOAT COMPUTATION ON CLUB PRESTIGE** (:654-750): `decayRate := 0.02` / `0.05` for a region, `decayRate += float64(len(club.Members)) * 0.002`, `decayRate *= 0.50` for a stabiliser, and `decayAmount := int(float64(club.Mojo)*decayRate + 0.5)` — club mojo gates club standing, so the integer rule is not met on this path.
  4. **`CalculateReputation` IS CALLED UNDER THE WRITE LOCK WHILE RANGING `l.leaderboard`** (:738-742), and the loop writes `l.leaderboard[wallet] = stats` back into the map it is iterating — the recorded five-site family, plus a write-during-iteration.
  5. **`TokenSinkRouter.Ledger interface{}`** (:76) — an untyped back-reference to the Lobby ("Reconciled: back-reference"), so every use needs an assertion where a typed pointer would be checked by the compiler.
- **VERDICT:** owner of every server-side type and of the `Lobby` field census — and the file that shows the float-on-money surface in one place: six float fields beside their integer counterparts, four float rates on outcome-deciding paths, and a float mojo-decay computation.

- **VERDICT:** owner of match authority and of sixteen career-XP hooks — carrying a broadcast XP award to unrelated players, two live underflow-shaped rival awards, a float-based XP path the integer owner is missing from, and a non-deterministic sudden-death reshuffle.

- **MOJIBAKE:** one of the ten damaged files (L1 U+00E2 = 30, U+00C2 = 47) — visible as `â€”`, `Â§` in comments.
- **VERDICT:** owner of the AI civilization — carrying the sweep's most significant unfixed lock finding so far (a measured `ace.mu → lobby.mutex` edge the file's own contract forbids), a lobby-owned map write under the engine lock, and a non-deterministic, counter-party-free minting economy.
