### `card_view_skins_test.go` — 496 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-496)
- **PURPOSE:** the test file for the viewer-scoped card display layer — it pins three load-bearing properties with POSITIVE AND NEGATIVE controls (:5-11): the layer's own vocabulary never names a card, a card-shaped request fails closed BEFORE anything is written, and the layer CANNOT write branding state (so the §10.1 card exclusion stays structural rather than conventional).
- **OWNS:** the fixtures only — `cardViewOwner = "cv-owner"` / `cardViewRival = "cv-rival"` (:22-25, lowercase-canonical as the engine requires) and a shared lobby builder `newBrandingTestLobby` (defined in the branding test file, not here). No production state.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `CardViewPolicy()` :33 · `IsCardTargetKind` :50 · `isCardViewMode`/`normalizeCardViewMode` :53 · `cardViewRefusesCardAlias` :61-73 · `l.SetCardViewForWallet` :90ff · `l.bondedAssets.MintBondedAssetWithMedia` :139 · `l.BondedBrandingForTarget` :182 · `l.BondedTargetsForWallet` :185 · `l.BindBondedAssetToTarget` :188 · `l.CardViewSurfaceForWallet` :205ff · `l.ClearCardViewForWallet` :255-258 · `l.bondedAssets.Save`/`Load` :281-288 · `Burn` :331 · `SetCardView` (legacy/dangling records) :350, :356 · `TransferOwnership` :372 · `l.handleCardView` :397, :434, :453 · `l.handleClearCardView` :474, :480. Every assertion drives the PRODUCTION entry point, never a re-implementation.
- **CALLS OUT:** `httptest.NewRecorder` (the REAL HTTP handlers) · `json.Unmarshal` · `strings.Contains` on the refusal text — i.e. these tests assert the SERVED REFUSAL REASON, not merely a boolean.
- **CALLED BY:** `go test .` only.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none taken by the tests (they call the production doors, which own their locks); the file asserts the WRITE side (bindings written, records created) rather than mutating shared state directly.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **WHAT IT PINS (measured, the file's real value):**
  1. **The vocabulary is card-free AND the alias trap does not misfire on the audience vocabulary** (:32-74). The disjunction is the point: the scope vocabulary legitimately says "cards" (`foreign_cards`/`own_cards`/`all_cards`) to describe WHICH cards are in view, so a naive substring scan would refuse the layer's own valid scopes. Both halves are pinned — `cardViewRefusesCardAlias` must be FALSE for all three scopes (:66) and TRUE for eight entity spellings including `Deck-Card` and `deck` (:69) — plus `len(cardViewModes) != 2` (:43), so the count itself says engine+asset only.
  2. **Fail-closed ordering** (:78-127): five card-shaped requests (two modes and two as an `asset_id` VALUE) are refused with `"cards are excluded"` and `CardViewCount() == 0` after; then three NON-card refusals are pinned as DIFFERENT reasons — `"unknown card-view mode"` for `hologram` AND for the retired `placeholder` mode (:112), `"unknown card-view scope"` for `everyone`, and `"a bonded asset is required"` for asset mode with no asset (:116) — because answering an unknown mode with the card reason would itself be an untrue refusal.
  3. **Ownership/legitimacy gates** (:131-176): wearing your own asset succeeds and joins media + name, the scope DEFAULTS to `foreign_cards` (:150), a RIVAL's asset is refused with the §27.8 `"owner/holder"` reason, and an asset flagged `BlackMarketAdopted` is refused with the §27.7.3 `"black-market"` reason.
  4. **THE STRUCTURAL GUARANTEE, pinned against the WRITER (:178-197):** after an asset-mode card view, `len(l.bondedAssets.Bindings) == 0`, `BindingsForAsset` is empty, and for NINE card kinds `BondedBrandingForTarget`, `BondedTargetsForWallet` and `BindBondedAssetToTarget` all answer empty/refused — the last one with `"cards are excluded"`. So the feature cannot write branding even BY ACCIDENT, and the exclusion is re-proved from the branding side at the same moment.
  5. **Viewer scoping** (:199-269): a second viewer reads engine/foreign (never the first viewer's record); a second viewer's write does not change the first's; `CardViewCount()` is 2 (one per viewer); and an UPPERCASE spelling of the same wallet creates NO duplicate record (:250-260) — the case-canonicalisation rule.
  6. **Clear** (:255-268): a real removal, then a SECOND clear reports `cleared:false` (idempotent), then the surface reads inactive.
  7. **Persistence + the off switch** (:271-313): `Save` → **fresh registry** → `Load` restores asset/scope AND resolves the media; then `engine` mode clears the record entirely (`CardViewCount() == 0`, `GetCardView` not-ok).
  8. **Burn drops a dangling view** (:315-335) and **legacy/dangling records read as OFF** (:337-379): a retired-`placeholder` record, an asset id that no longer resolves, and — the important one — a viewer who SOLD their art via `TransferOwnership` stops wearing it, proving §27.8 is re-proved on EVERY read rather than checked once at set.
  9. **The HTTP boundary** (:381-491): six bodies are refused AT THE DECODER (`target_kind`/`target_id`, `card_id`, two card modes, the retired mode, asset-with-no-asset) and nothing is written; a legitimate request succeeds and its response carries the `capacity` budget (:420) and NO card field (:423); GET is viewer-scoped and an ANONYMOUS GET still serves the POLICY with `wallet_required:true` (:450-463) rather than an empty answer; `DELETE` on the route and `GET` on `clear` are both method refusals (:465-477); then the clear route empties `CardViewCount()`.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none in production code (this file is the pin). Two honest notes about the TEST as an instrument: (a) it asserts on refusal SUBSTRINGS, so a rewording of an error message breaks the test without a behaviour change — acceptable here because those messages are the contract a client reads; (b) every test constructs a FRESH lobby via `newBrandingTestLobby`, so the file proves single-object behaviour and cannot see cross-lobby/aliasing hazards (the §35 one-map-two-mutex class).
- **VERDICT:** test (owner of the §10.5/§23.5.5 evidence chain) — it is the reason "the card-view layer cannot brand a card" is a MECHANICAL claim rather than a reading, because it drives the §10.1 writer directly from the §10.5 feature and requires the writer to refuse.


### `career_role_vocabulary_test.go` — 501 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-501)
- **PURPOSE:** the test file for the career ROLE vocabulary and the Intel-Agent cyber-intercept door, pinning THREE defects found by reading the door against the engine (:14-34): (1) one career reaching the engine under two spellings, so `GetRivalPairName`'s `==` answered `""` for half the pairings — a silent skip with no error, no log and no XP; (2) the `handleCyberIntercept` SELF-DEADLOCK (WRITE lock held → self-locking `l.isJusticeAligned`, fatal only when an Arc-Net Operative was on the leaderboard); (3) the door's `X-Client-ID` requirement that no module in `Public/**` sets, so it could only answer 401.

- **ENTRY POINTS:** `cyberCall` (:76-96) runs the door ON ITS OWN GOROUTINE and fails by TIMEOUT at 5 s (“a deadlock raises no error at all”). Production entries driven: `RoleKey` :111 · `GetRivalPairName` :150 · `roleXP`/`GetCareerTier`/`getTierFor`/`HasCareer` :170-178 · `IsJusticeAligned` :194 · `l.isJusticeAligned` / `l.isJusticeAlignedLocked` :205-238 · `GetVBVGatingPermille` / `GetVBVGatingMultiplier` :246-262 · `l.handleCyberIntercept` · `GetRivalXPDelta` :491 · `rivalPairTable` :467.

Two watchdog patterns act as the instrument: a handshake goroutine proving the lock is FREE after `isJusticeAligned` returns (:225-232), and a 5 s timeout proving `isJusticeAlignedLocked` returns while the WRITE lock is HELD (:233-250).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1


- **WHAT IT PINS (measured):**
  1. **The fold, with a NEGATIVE CONTROL** (:111-140): ELEVEN same-career spellings must fold to one key — including `Mutation Log Auditor`/`MutationAuditor` (the declared abbreviation), `IntelAgent`/`Int.Agent` (handlers_criminality.go's spelling) and the case/separator variants — and FIVE pairs of unrelated careers must NOT collapse. The negative control is what keeps the fold honest: a `RoleKey` that stripped too much would pass the first half alone.
  2. **Pair resolution across spellings** (:142-176): nine cases that ALL answered `""` before, including BOTH argument orders (`Gossip, Forensic Analyst` and its reverse) answering the same canonical name — the one-row-per-unordered-pair property — plus a negative control (`Warden, Fence` → `""`) proving the hooks around it still answer "not a rival" instead of paying a bonus.
  3. **`roleXP` reads the writer's key** (:158-186): XP stored as `Int.Agent` is visible to `roleXP(cxp,"IntelAgent")`, `GetCareerTier` and `getTierFor`, and `HasCareer(cxp,"Intel-Agent")` is true; with TWO keys for one role the answer is the HIGHEST (2500), never the sum — "a sum would invent XP that was never awarded" (:183-185).
  4. **`IsJusticeAligned` across spellings** (:188-200) with both polarities, and **the lock-held pair is pinned in BOTH directions** (:202-252) — the unlocked face takes/releases the lock, the locked face returns while the write lock is held.
  5. **The $VBV gate is INTEGER and EXACT** (:242-268): all six tiers checked in permille AND the float display wrapper compared against the integer owner, then the door's own arithmetic (`base × permille / 1000`) pinned at 50 ungated and 1600 at Boss.
  6. **The door end to end** (:304-350): a LOWER-CASE request for a MIXED-CASE stored wallet resolves; `xp_awarded = 50`, `cost_micro = 500 $VBV`, `decrypt_bonus_permille = 1000`, and the response echoes the STORED spelling; the balance drops 2,000 → 1,500 $VBV, the FAUCET receives exactly the fee (the sink reconciles), the XP lands on a NEW canonical key while the PRE-EXISTING key is left undisturbed (:340-345 — the anti-clobber assertion), and exactly ONE evidence record exists.
  7. **The integer gate reaches the award** (:363-378): the same base pays 1600 (32×) at the Boss tier.
  8. **EVERY REFUSAL MOVES NOTHING** (:380-434): a four-value before/after tuple (balance, faucet, XP, record count) asserted unchanged for 401 / 404 / 403 / malformed-JSON, and a 402 that must name BOTH the price and the balance.
  9. **THE ALLY-SHARING REGRESSION IS TWO DEFECTS AT ONCE** (:436-432): the test's own comment states the double unreachability (the comparison read the DEFENDER's award, and the branch called the self-locking `isJusticeAligned` while the write lock was held), and the `cyberLobby` fixture is built with an Arc-Net Operative ON the leaderboard — exactly the state that fired the deadlock. It then asserts the exact integer arithmetic (71 attacker XP from `50 × 1.1 × 1.3`; ally share 700 → **721**) and that an unrelated wallet is untouched (100).
  10. **Re-intercept of a known event is FREE** (:434-458): `cost_micro = 0`, the balance is NOT debited, and the existing record is marked `Intercepted`.
  11. **The pair table's two invariants** (:460-501): one row per UNORDERED pair (a duplicate can never be reached, because the scan is order-independent and returns on the first match — the defect that let one pair be an ally AND an enemy), and every declared pair has a PRICE except the two NAMED zero-delta pairs (`JusticeRecruiter↔MutationLogAuditor` P2-D8, `MutationLogAuditor↔Kidnapper` P2-D10) — and the test fails in BOTH directions, so pricing a declared-zero pair must come with deleting it from that list.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none in production code (this file is the pin). The `rivalPairTable` invariants here REMAIN the live record of two operator-facing balance calls (P2-D8 and P2-D10 are still unpriced), and the `×1.1`/`×1.3` pair arithmetic documents the path repaired in session (j).
- **VERDICT:** test (owner of the role-vocabulary and cyber-intercept evidence chain) — it converts two silent failures and one process freeze into reproducible assertions, and its two negative controls (the fold's `differ` list, the pair table's price list) are what stop the fix from over-reaching.

### `note_vocabulary_test.go` — 506 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-506)
- **PURPOSE:** the test file that makes the note-vocabulary ownership rule MECHANICAL rather than conventional — it proves the prefix set is well-formed, unique, unambiguous and exact-match-only; that the VOCABULARY is used and never re-declared (server AND client); that a money door cannot be called with an empty purpose; and that the served route is registered in BOTH servers.
- **OWNS:** the helpers only — `firstPartyGoSources` (:405-430, reads every `.go` in the repo ROOT and FAILS if zero were found, so a clean scan can never be a scan of nothing), `splitTopLevelArgs` (:434-455, splits on commas NOT inside brackets so `uint64(5000*divisor)` stays one argument), `notePrefixConstRe` + `declaredNotePrefixConstNames` (:457-479, reads the constant names/values FROM `note_vocabulary.go` so "the constant exists" is proven from source rather than asserted from a list), and `fileBuildsPrefixFromVocabulary` (:484-505).
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `assertNoteVocabularyPolicy` :20 · `noteVocabulary` :21, :258 · `NotePrefixes` :30 · `AmbiguousNotePrefixPairs` + `notePrefixAmbiguitiesAcknowledged` :44-56 · `IsDeclaredNotePrefix` :67-79 · the five bound builders + `notePrefixBuild` :87-131 · `IsMoneyDoorPurpose` :98 · `VerifyBuyInTransaction` :166ff, :344ff · `notePrefixesByScope` :227, :277 · `NoteVocabularyPayload` :265 · `handleNoteVocabulary` :330-341, :395 · `Public/js/*.js` (scanned as TEXT, :230-245).
- **CALLS OUT:** `os.ReadDir`/`os.ReadFile` on the repo root AND on `Public/js` · `httptest` · `regexp` · `sync.Once` (the constant scan is memoised once per process) · `encoding/json`.
- **CALLED BY:** `go test .` only.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none — no mutex is taken anywhere in the file (it exercises no lobby state; the `Lobby` literal at :344 exists only to satisfy the oracle's config lookup).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **WHAT IT PINS (measured):**
  1. **Form + uniqueness + exact-match-only** (:19-80): the init assertion is re-run EXPLICITLY so a failure names the vocabulary instead of appearing as "panic in init"; two purposes sharing a prefix is refused ("a payment made for one could satisfy the other"); and three proofs that `IsDeclaredNotePrefix` is an exact test — a longer note must not satisfy a shorter declaration, a prefix missing its `:` is not declared, and **the empty string must never be declared, because `HasPrefix(x, "")` is always true**.
  2. **The ambiguity set is pinned AND self-checking** (:43-61): `AmbiguousNotePrefixPairs()` must equal the acknowledged set index-for-index, and every acknowledged pair must STILL be a real prefix pair — so a stale acknowledgement (which would mask a new ambiguity behind a count that no longer matches) fails the build. Recorded as a deliberate property: the set is EMPTY today.
  3. **Bound builders bind their target** (:84-131): each carries its declared base AND a non-empty bound part, each base is registered as a money door, two different club ids / district ids produce DIFFERENT prefixes (so a payment cannot be replayed against another club, district or tournament), an EMPTY bound part returns `""` (the door fails closed at the boundary), and `notePrefixBuild` PANICS on an undeclared base — a first-party wiring error asserted as unreachable from a request.
  4. **Money-door parity across the whole tree** (:140-201): every `VerifyBuyInTransaction(` call site in every root `.go` file must pass either a constant declared in the owner file or a variable THAT FILE builds from a `Note*` name; a re-typed LITERAL fails. Two anti-staleness guards: the scan errors if a call's arguments cannot be parsed, and the whole test FAILS if fewer than EIGHT call sites are found ("is the scan stale?"), so the check cannot silently become a scan of nothing.
  5. **Single-owner literals** (:203-223): no file other than `note_vocabulary.go` may contain a quoted prefix; the test first proves it CAN see the owner's own literals before trusting a clean result.
  6. **The CLIENT carries no copy** (:225-245): every `Public/js/*.js` must be free of every money-door prefix (it must read them from the served route), with `scanned == 0` a fatal error.
  7. **Catalogue completeness BOTH ways** (:247-263): every declared constant must appear in the served catalogue AND the catalogue must hold exactly as many prefixes as there are constants — the equality catches an orphan catalogue entry as well as a missing one.
  8. **The served payload's shape and arithmetic** (:265-306): every purpose carries key/prefix/scope/direction/description; `counts.total` equals the number served; `counts.money_door` equals the declared count; **and the three scope counts must SUM to the total** (a purpose in no bucket would otherwise be invisible); the rules must state `client_may_name_money_door_only` and `empty_prefix_is_refused`.
  9. **Route parity across the TWO servers** (:308-342): both `server_main.go` and `console_server.go` must register `/api/notes/vocabulary` (the repo's repeated "route added to one server only" defect class), the HTTP boundary is exercised for real (200 + `application/json`), the served money-door count is compared with the declaration, every served scope=money_door prefix must be one the server declares, every served prefix must be declared, and `POST` must be **405** (a read-only vocabulary).
  10. **The two fail-closed guards on the money path** (:344-399): an EMPTY purpose prefix, an empty transaction id and a blank transaction id are each refused (the empty prefix is the important one — `HasPrefix(x,"")` is always true, so an empty prefix would silently drop purpose binding), and a well-formed request for an UNKNOWN network is still refused by the config lookup — which is what proves the new guards did not swallow legitimate calls.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none in production code. **One measured GAP in the instrument:** the payload-shape test asserts five fields (:272) and `assertNoteVocabularyPolicy` asserts charset/scope/direction/ambiguity — **neither checks TEXT ENCODING**, which is exactly how the mojibake (688 U+00E2 in `note_vocabulary.go`) passes the init panic, this file and all nine repo gates while the SERVED Descriptions carry the damage. The proposed SIXTH policy rule (refuse a Description containing the mojibake signature) would be asserted here and would have made that corruption a boot panic.
- **VERDICT:** test (owner of the note-vocabulary evidence chain) — the strongest parity instrument in the tree, because it derives its subject from source (constants, call sites, the client directory) and fails loudly when the derivation finds nothing; its only hole is the one the corruption came through.



### `loan_service.go` — 521 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-521)
- **PURPOSE:** the collateralized-debt service — take a loan against an escrowed `CardBundle`, repay it through the ONE txid-memoised money door, and default it when overdue (auto-pulling pre-approved tokens first, else liquidating the collateral into the Black Market) (:25-27).
- **OWNS:** the LOAN LIFECYCLE over `l.loans` (creation :127-137, repayment :231-241, default :341-357) · the loan's on-chain audit trail (`NotePrefixLoanPayback` :269, :322 · `NotePrefixLoanLiquidate` :404 · `NotePrefixLoanAutoPull` :448) · the collateral escrow rule (via `TransferBundleItems`) · the auto-pull rail (`pullApprovedTokens` :425-471).
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `HandleGetLoans` :30 (route `GET /api/loans`) · `HandleTakeLoan` :57 (`POST`) · `HandleRepayLoan` :160 · `ProcessLoans` :280 (the overdue daemon) · `pullApprovedTokens` :425 · `TransferBundleItems` :476 — documented "assumes the lobby mutex is held" :475.
- **CALLS OUT:** `l.oracleService.ResolveEnvoiName` :48, :78 · `l.oracleService.VerifyBuyInTransaction` :222 · `l.oracleService.CheckAssetApproval` :302 · `l.claimTxID`/`releaseTxID`/`commitTxID` :215, :226, :262 · `l.applyDynamicScalingLocked` :140, :246, :467 · `l.logAdminAuditLocked` · `l.updatePlayerPlaystyleTendenciesLocked` :396 · `l.getClubByTerritoryID` :345 · `l.sendToClientLocked` + `l.getClientIDFromWalletLocked` :368 · `l.sendNoteTx` :268, :322, :404 · `l.CalculateReputation` :519 · `l.blackMarket` · the algosdk (algod, crypto, msgpack, mnemonic, transaction, types) for the pull.
- **CALLED BY:** the route table (`server_main.go`) for the three HTTP doors; the liquidity/15-minute ticker for `ProcessLoans`; `TransferBundleItems` is ALSO called from the auction/black-market bundle paths — it is the shared inventory-move helper, and this file documents that contract.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `l.mutex` — **read-validate → release → verify → one write section** on the repayment door (:189-197 RLock + RUnlock, :230-260 Lock/Unlock) and on the default path (:281-287, :337-419); `HandleTakeLoan` holds the write lock for its whole body (:80-81) but performs NO network I/O inside it; `pullApprovedTokens` takes the lock TWICE, each time briefly and **released before any I/O** (:426-430, :456-468). State touched: `l.faucetBalance` (+ `faucetBalanceMicro` on the pull only) · `l.playerBalances` · `l.leaderboard` · `l.loans` · `l.nonces` · `l.blackMarket`.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code):**
  1. **FLOAT ON LEDGER MONEY, AND IT IS CLIENT-DECLARED.** `LoanAmount float64 "json:\"loan_amount\""` (:61) in whole $VBV units, converted inline with `uint64(req.LoanAmount*1000000 + 0.5)` (:124) and compared as a float against the faucet mirror (`l.faucetBalance < req.LoanAmount` :83). Every other money path in the tree takes micro-units; here the caller chooses the rounding, and a request of `0.0000004` buys a 0-micro loan while `DurationHours` still creates the liability. Interest (`(loanAmountMicro*110 + 50)/100` :125) and the 15 % residual (:351) ARE integer — so the float is exactly one boundary, the one a client controls.
  2. **THE PRINCIPAL IS MINTED WITH ITS DEBIT DEFERRED TO A COMMENT.** `l.playerBalances[targetWallet] += loanAmountMicro` (:139), and the file states the principal is **NOT** subtracted from `faucetBalance` here — "the deduction occurs in faucet_service.go during reward dispatch" (:141-145). Measured in THIS file: a loan creates spendable balance with no counter-entry, and `applyDynamicScalingLocked` is then run (:140) against a pool the new liability has not reduced.
  3. **THE TWO FAUCET COUNTERS DIVERGE ACROSS THE SERVICE'S OWN PATHS.** `HandleRepayLoan` credits ONLY the float mirror — `l.faucetBalance += float64(repayAmount)/1000000.0` (:245) — while `pullApprovedTokens` credits BOTH (`faucetBalanceMicro += amount` :457 + the mirror :458). Because `applyDynamicScalingLocked` reads the FLOAT (`economy_service.go:75`), a manually repaid loan MOVES the scaler while leaving the integer pool stale; anything reading `faucetBalanceMicro` (faucet payout capacity) sees a different number. Two owners for one number.
  4. **`HandleGetLoans` WRITES SHARED STATE AFTER RELEASING THE LOCK.** The RLock is released at :43 and the pointer mutation `loan.BorrowerName = …` runs at :47-49 on records that are STILL the live `*Loan` values in `l.loans` — a data race on a shared struct (the §32/§38 family) that also mutates the persisted record from a READ route. The intent ("no display latency") is right; the shape should be a snapshot copy.
  5. **EIGHT DISCARDED ERRORS ON THE ON-CHAIN PULL, EACH TURNING A REFUSAL INTO A ZERO VALUE.** `algod.MakeClient` :434 · `mnemonic.ToPrivateKey` :435 (a bad or missing `FAUCET_MNEMONIC` yields a zero key) · `crypto.AccountFromPrivateKey` :436 · `SuggestedParams` :437 · `types.DecodeAddress(from)` :440 and `(vaultAddr)` :441, :447 (a malformed address yields the zero address) · `strconv.ParseUint(rewardAsset, …)` :446 (yields **app id 0**) — and the transaction is then BUILT AND SIGNED with those values (:450-454). Only the sign and the send are checked.
  6. **`TransferBundleItems` CALLS `CalculateReputation` UNDER THE WRITE LOCK** (:519) and writes the map entry back (:520) — the recorded five-site family (`backend_types.go`). It runs from BOTH write-locked sections here (:118, :249) and from the default path (:343), so every loan cycle recomputes reputation with the lobby locked. Its inventory arithmetic itself is correct and defensive: nil map initialised (:481-483) and a decrement floors at zero by DELETING the key (:491-493, :501-503, :511-513) — an escrow cannot produce a negative holding.
  7. **A CLIENT-DERIVED FLOAT ENTERS A CLUB TREASURY.** `liquidationFeeBase := float64(liquidationFeeMicro)/1000000.0` (:343) is added to `owningClub.Treasury` (:348) — a float VBV amount in a stored balance, derived from a loan amount that entered as a client float. (Display formatting is permitted; storing one is not.)
  8. **THE PULL SIGNS WITH `FAUCET_MNEMONIC` FROM THE ENVIRONMENT, AND THE DOOR HAS ITS OWN NONCE RAIL.** `HandleTakeLoan` verifies with a hand-rolled msgpack decode + `stx.Txn.Note` string compare against `l.nonces[req.ClientID]` (:88-98) instead of the ONE extracted verifier — a THIRD signature shape beside the admin gate and the oracle (B2's "never a second verifier" rule).
  9. **THE AUTO-PULL'S APPROVAL CHECK DISCARDS ITS ERROR.** `approvedAmt, _ := l.oracleService.CheckAssetApproval(...)` (:302) — an indexer failure is indistinguishable from "no approval", so a transient read failure silently routes a repayable loan to LIQUIDATION. The comment names the intent ("safety net for forgetful borrowers"); the discarded error makes the net fail open in the expensive direction.
  10. **THE DEFAULTED COLLATERAL BECOMES A `*Loan` IN THE BLACK MARKET.** `l.blackMarket = append(l.blackMarket, *loan)` (:409) appends a whole `Loan` VALUE (the collateral is a FIELD of it) to a market whose other entries are listings, with a 50-entry FIFO prune (:410-412). The escrowed items are never re-listed as items, and `Status = "defaulted"` (:339) survives only in that copy because the record is DELETED from `l.loans` (:415). Whether the Black Market renders that shape is not asserted here — **ambiguous - cited lines only**.
  11. **`HandleGetLoans` ENCODES A BARE ARRAY** (:53) of `*Loan`. The non-nil `make([]*Loan, 0)` (:34) IS deliberate and commented (:32-33) — but the envelope contract is NOT restated here, so the consumer shape is **ambiguous - cited lines only**.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the loan lifecycle and of the on-chain pull rail) — its LOCK discipline is the corrected shape this repository standardised (read-validate → release → verify → one write section, memo claimed before the oracle and committed after the apply), while its MONEY discipline carries one client-controlled float, one counter-entry deferred to another file, two diverging faucet counters, and eight discarded errors on the only path here that signs a real transaction.

### `career_upgrade_advisory_test.go` — 531 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-531)
- **PURPOSE:** the test file for the operator's "unlocked, never forced" rule, which it states as FOUR RESTRICTIONS on what the code may do (:17-32): a career is UNLOCKED and never granted; the LEVEL CAP is the unlock; the employer may ASK and only the employee may ACT; and the notice fires once and re-arms after the role is lost.
- **OWNS:** the fixtures — `cpEmployerWallet` / `cpStaffReady` / `cpStaffNotReady` / `cpOutsiderWallet` (:35-38), `careerUpgradeSeedStaff` (writes exactly what a hire writes — `Club.Staff` — so the projection is proven against the REAL employment record, :43-53), `careerUpgradeSeedEmployerAndEligibleStaff` (:207-213), and `containsRole` (:56-63), which compares BY ROLE through `RoleKey` "the way every career reader in this repository does".
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `l.UnlockedCareerRolesLocked` :71, :124, :137 · `l.CareerUpgradeViewLocked` :78, :104, :167, :195, :277, :311, :325, :341 · `l.CareerPromotionRequirementsForWalletLocked` :79 · `CareerRoleGateFor` :115 · `l.CareerPathViewLocked` :103 · `l.RequestStaffCareerUpgradeLocked` :219, :345, :407 · `staffUpgradeRequestKey` :267 · `l.PromoteCareerLocked` :389, :410 · `l.CareerUnlockNoticesLocked` :402, :408, :417, :425 · `l.handleCareerStaffUpgradeRequest` :444, :452, :467, :477, :486, :507.
- **CALLS OUT:** `httptest` (the REAL door) · `encoding/json` (twice: to compare 25 serialised reads byte-for-byte, and to read refusals) · the `career_path_test.go` fixtures (`careerPathTestLobby`, `careerPathMakeEligible`, `careerPathSeedOwner`, `careerPathSeedPlayer`).
- **CALLED BY:** `go test .` only.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none — like the other journey tests it mutates `l.leaderboard[...]` DIRECTLY (e.g. :126, :134, :273, :297, :374, :413) with no lock, which is legitimate for a single-goroutine fixture but means the file proves BEHAVIOUR, not concurrency; there is no watchdog and no deadlock assertion here (the sibling `career_role_vocabulary_test.go` owns that instrument).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **WHAT IT PINS (measured):**
  1. **THE UNLOCK IS DERIVED FROM THE ONE GATE EVALUATOR AND GRANTS NOTHING** (:67-110). Every role served as unlocked is re-checked against `CareerPromotionRequirementsForWalletLocked` and must be `Eligible` — so "unlocked" cannot become a second, softer gate the career table disagrees with. Then TWO reads of the advisory must leave `PromotedRoles`, `JobRole` AND `UnlockNoticesSent` untouched (:85-100) — the "deriving writes nothing" property, which is the whole difference between an OFFER and a GRANT. The served advisory must be `IsOptional` with a non-empty `Statement`, `UnlockBasis`, `NoticeRule` and `StaffBasis`, and `UnlockedCount` must equal the list length (:102-110).
  2. **THE LEVEL CAP IS THE UNLOCK, AND NOTHING ELSE IS** (:112-141): the fixture is walked to ONE level below the declared gate (`gate.MinLessonLevel - 1`) where Warden must NOT be unlocked, then to exactly the cap where it must be — so a flag cannot masquerade as an achievement.
  3. **THE STAFF PROJECTION IS THE EMPLOYER'S OWN ROSTER** (:143-204): exactly the two wallets written to `Club.Staff`; the list is in a STABLE order (`Staff[0].Wallet < Staff[1].Wallet`, with the comment naming why — a map iteration reshuffles every refresh, the defect class the bonded-asset chest and the market both had to repair); `StaffReadyCount == 1`; per-staff `UpgradeReady` and `UnlockCount` match the employee's real record; the club role is carried through; and a wallet that employs NOBODY gets an EMPTY projection **with a non-empty `StaffBasis`** so the client can say where staff come from rather than showing a bare box.
  4. **STABILITY ACROSS READS** (:206-230): the whole view is marshalled TWENTY-FIVE times and every serialisation must be byte-identical to the first.
  5. **THE REQUEST ASKS AND NEVER PROMOTES** (:232-301) — the assertion the file itself calls the one that must never weaken. The notice must name the club, the club name and the folded role, and carry a message; then the employee's `PromotedRoles`, `JobRole` and `LessonLevel` are each proven UNCHANGED; exactly ONE request is recorded, keyed by `staffUpgradeRequestKey`; the employee sees it in `RequestsToMe` once `EmployerClubID` is written the way a hire writes it, and ANOTHER wallet sees none; **the employer's own projection shows the requested role** so the panel cannot offer it twice; and a REPEAT is REPORTED with the SAME `RequestedAt` and no second record — "a nudge that can be repeated on demand is not a notification".
  6. **THE BACKSTOP** (:283-290): the employment field is CLEARED and the request must STILL surface — a roster row can outlive a cleared `EmployerClubID`, and a request that exists but never surfaces is a notification that silently failed (the one defect this feature found while being built).
  7. **SEVEN REFUSALS, EACH NAMING ITS REASON AND MOVING NOTHING** (:303-388): no staff wallet named; the employer naming THEMSELVES ("take the upgrade yourself"); a role with no declared gate; a caller who owns no club; a target who is not on your staff; **a caller naming ANOTHER OWNER's staff** (a rival club with its own ready staff is seeded specifically to prove the roster check is per-club); and **the level cap has not opened it**. Every case asserts the employee's `PromotedRoles` was not touched. Then the two-level proof: the SAME request refused for a locked employee SUCCEEDS once THEIR gate is met — so the door reads the EMPLOYEE's record, not a flag the employer controls — and after `PromoteCareerLocked` the request is refused with "already holds".
  8. **NOTIFIED, NOT FORCED** (:392-428): ONE notice, naming the right role and wallet with a non-empty basis and message, and the message must literally contain **"yours to take"** (the notice IS the whole of the rule); a SECOND call returns none (a notice that repeats on every sample is a nag); a career already HELD is not announced; and after the role is removed from `PromotedRoles` the notice RE-ARMS — so a career lost to a demotion and re-earned notifies again instead of being silent forever. A wallet with no engine record is never notified.
  9. **THE HTTP BOUNDARY** (:430-525): anonymous **401** (refused by the door, before any lookup); `GET` **405**; THREE client-owned fields (`promoted_roles`, `unlocked`, `civil_tier`) are refused **400** at the decoder, so a body cannot even be parsed into a grant, an unlock or a civil rank; a body missing the staff wallet is **400**; a caller with no club is **403** with the reason containing "own no club" AND `StaffUpgradeRequests` still empty (a refused request is not recorded); and the ALLOWED path returns 200 carrying the advisory while the staff member's `PromotedRoles` is proven EMPTY on the way out.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS:** none in production code (this file is the pin). Two instrument limits worth recording: the deterministic-order assertion covers only a TWO-row roster (`Staff[0]`/`Staff[1]`, :176-180), so a three-way ordering bug would not surface here; and the file takes no locks, so it establishes behaviour only — the lock discipline of `UnlockedCareerRolesLocked`/`CareerUpgradeViewLocked`/`RequestStaffCareerUpgradeLocked` is covered by the `...Locked` contract and the self-lock gate, not by this file.

### `slide_theming.go` — 542 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-542)
- **PURPOSE:** §10.6 viewer-scoped slide theming — the app's own slideshows (app boot / main menu, and the World Dashboard background) are themeable by the wallet doing the looking, using ONE of that wallet's own bonded assets (:5-27). The header states the guarantee as an ABSENT FIELD: a record is keyed by the viewer's own wallet and names only that viewer's asset, so "another player's entity could be named" is not expressible.
- **OWNS:** the SIX-slot vocabulary (`SlideSlotMenu1..3`, `SlideSlotDashboard1..3`, :39-46) and the ONE declaration of each slot's default pack frame (:57-64 — `npc-anya-001`, `npc-crypto-seraph-002`, `npc-vbabes-046`, `npc-vbabes-054`, `npc-crypto-seraph-004`, `npc-anya-041`, i.e. the operator-named frames) · `isSlideSlot`/`slideSlotDefFor`/`slideSlotIDs` (:66-82, :276-284) · the served policy `SlideThemingPolicy()` (:124-168) · the read payload types `SlideThemeSlotView`/`SlideThemeSurface` (:84-121) · the request/record types `SlideThemeRequest`/`SlideTheme` (:176-192) · the registry storage accessors `slideThemeKey`/`SetSlideTheme`/`GetSlideTheme`/`SlideThemesFor`/`ClearSlideTheme`/`SlideThemeCount` (:194-274) · the authority `SetSlideThemeForWallet`/`ClearSlideThemeForWallet`/`SlideThemeSurfaceForWallet` (:310-424) · the HTTP handlers (:426-542).
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `GET|POST /api/assets/slide-theme` → `handleSlideTheme` :428 · `POST /api/assets/slide-theme/clear` → `handleClearSlideTheme` :507 · `l.SetSlideThemeForWallet` :311 · `l.SlideThemeSurfaceForWallet` :375 · `l.ClearSlideThemeForWallet` :349 · `SlideThemingPolicy` :125.
- **CALLS OUT:** `placeholderSku` + `PlaceholderDerivativeURIForSKU`/`PlaceholderDerivativeRecordFor`/`PlaceholderDerivativeStatusForClient` (`placeholder_assets.go` / `placeholder_derivatives.go`) · `l.bondedAssets.Get`/`SetSlideTheme`/`SlideThemesFor`/`ClearSlideTheme` · `IsCardAssetType` (`bonded_branding.go`) · `canonicalViewerWallet` · `l.BondedAssetCapacityForWallet` (the served §23.5.1 budget) · `l.logAdminAudit` :344 · `l.getWalletFromRequest`.
- **CALLED BY:** the route tables of BOTH servers (`/api/assets/slide-theme`, `…/clear`); the client `slide_theming.js` + `bonded_branding.js`; `placeholder_assets.go`'s slide defaults reference the same SKUs (the starter pack grants exactly these frames).
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `BondedAssetRegistry.SlideThemes` under `r.mu` — every accessor takes the lock and returns **COPIES** (:199-222 copy-on-write, :228-232 copy-on-read, :238-249 a fresh map, :254-274 delete-only). `l.bondedAssets` is a pointer read without the lobby lock (assigned once at boot). The write path holds NO lobby lock, so its `l.logAdminAudit` call (which takes the lock itself) cannot self-lock — the opposite of the `handleGameProtocol` ▸ `use_item` case the self-lock gate reported.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code):**
  1. **EVERY REFUSAL IS SERVED AS HTTP 200.** `writeJSON` (`rivalry_handlers.go:619-625`) sets `Content-Type` and encodes — it NEVER sets a status, so `handleSlideTheme`'s method branch (`"method not allowed"`, :439), the anonymous write ("wallet required", :470), the decoder refusal (:478-483) and every authority refusal (:489-495) all leave the response at **200**. A client keyed on the status code (the convention every other door in this tree uses: 401/403/405/409) cannot see a refusal here; it must read `success`. `writeJSONStatus` exists two lines below the helper and is not used by this family.
  2. **THE DEFAULT-ART RULE IS IMPLEMENTED TWICE.** The light-rendition decision — light URI + `default_derived`, else the passthrough note, else "light rendition pending" — is written once in `SlideThemingPolicy` (:136-151) and again in `slideSlotDefaultView` (:288-306). Two copies of one rule can drift; the tree's own single-owner rule says one of them should call the other.
  3. **A STALE RECORD IS REPORTED BUT NEVER PRUNED.** `SlideThemeSurfaceForWallet` appends five distinct reasons to `StaleSlots` (:394-405) and correctly renders the pack default instead — but the record stays in `SlideThemes` (and therefore in every snapshot) for ever, re-reported on every read. Reporting is right; leaving the row means the same slot reports stale indefinitely (contrast `Burn`, which drops the record).
  4. **A RAW PREFIX COMPARE ON A COMPOSITE KEY.** `SlideThemesFor` and the bulk `ClearSlideTheme` select by `canonicalViewerWallet(viewer) + ":"` (:240, :266) — correct only while a canonical wallet cannot contain `:` (a requirement stated nowhere). The SLOT half is safe by construction (a closed six-value set), and the 2-part key cannot alias the 3-part binding key space, so this is the mild version of the `bonded_asset_registry.go` finding.

### `common_types_wasm.go` — 562 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-562)
- **PURPOSE:** the CLIENT-side twin of `common_types.go` (`//go:build js && wasm`) — it re-declares the 40 types the WASM engine and the JS bridge exchange, so the browser build compiles its own copy under a tag that excludes the server's (:1-3).
- **OWNS:** every shared type as the CLIENT sees it — `BoardStateHash` :10 · the economic safety caps :16-19 · `GlobalSentiment` :22-27 · `Club` :30-65 · `CommissionEvent` :68-72 · `Lease` :75-85 · `QueueEntry` :88-94 · `LinkedWallet`/`WalletLinkInfo` :97-108 · `HoldingBonus` :111-116 · `FaceplateStats`/`FaceplateRegistry` :119-131 · `UseItemData`/`BailCardData` :141-152 · `Envelope` :155-160 · `ChallengeData`/`MoveData`/`ReportGloatData` :163-184 · `NetworkConfig` :187-202 · `ServerCard` :205-218 · `TournamentSummary` :221-232 · ARC-72 metadata :234-243 · `MatchState` :246-339 · `AuthoritativeFrame` :342-348 · `MutationEvent`/`CapturedCardInfo` :351-367 · `MatchHistory` :370-385 · `PlayerStats` :388-434 · `CareerXP` **:437-442** · `RivalryState` **:446-453** · `ItemDef` :456-465 · `JusticeCategory`/`UnderworldCategory` :468-471 · `PlaystyleTendencies` :474-481 · `ConsolePlatform`/`ConsoleAssetReceipt` :483-487 · `CardBundle`/`Auction`/`Loan`/`Rumor`/`KidnapState` :489-538 · `TournamentMatch`/`TournamentState` :540-562.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** the type declarations plus ONE method — `Player.GetEffectiveMojo()` :135 (note the receiver is `Player`, not the server's `PlayerStats`). No HTTP routes, no `js.Global()` bridge, no exported function besides the caps constants.
- **CALLS OUT:** `encoding/json` and `time` only (:5-8). No service, no lock, no file.
- **CALLED BY:** the whole `js/wasm` build — `main.go` (the client engine), `battle_service.go`'s client variants, and every `GetGameState`/`SyncFullProfile` payload shape.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** none — no mutex anywhere in the file. `FaceplateRegistry` (:127) is a package-level map that is READ only (no writer in the tree).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `js && wasm` :1 — the exact inverse of `common_types.go`, which is why the two are never compiled together.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code). The whole file's risk is ONE class: the mirror drifts silently because the compiler cannot see across the tags.**
  1. **THE MIRROR IS A STRICT SUBSET — measured field diff, both files parsed and compared.** Both declare 40 types, and the differences are ONE-DIRECTIONAL: `common_types.go`-only types `JusticeItem` (:505) and `UnderworldItem` (:512); `common_types_wasm.go`-only `CareerXP` (:437) and `RivalryState` (:446). Per type: `Club` server 34 fields / mirror 33 (missing **`StaffUpgradeRequests`**, the store `RequestStaffCareerUpgradeLocked` writes); `PlayerStats` server **88** / mirror **73** — the mirror lacks `CareerLevel`, `Career`, `CareerTier`, `Alliances`, `ActiveAllianceID`, `SectorTiles`, `Wanted`, `ActiveBuffs`, `VBVBalance`, `TotalDividendClaimedMicro`, `ActiveHostageCount`, `LastActivity`, `Credits`, `ID`, `Name`; `MatchState` server 41 / mirror 40 (missing `FaithComposition`). `Loan`, `Auction` and `NetworkConfig` agree exactly.
  2. **TWO `CareerXP` DEFINITIONS WITH DIFFERENT KEYS, AND THE CLIENT CANNOT SEE THE REAL ONE.** The mirror's `CareerXP` (:437-442) is `{current_path, level, xp, bonuses, last_promotion_at}`; the engine's real record (`rival_career_engine.go:130-…)` is `{role_xp, level, promoted_roles, current_prompts, role_name, tiers, avg_sustained_micro, demotion_warning_at, path, path_chosen_at, demotion_warning_role, last_promotion_at, demotions}`. Only `level` and `last_promotion_at` overlap. Because Go's JSON decoding IGNORES unknown keys, a server payload decoded into the mirror silently DROPS `role_xp`, `promoted_roles`, `tiers`, `path`, `demotions` — so no client surface can read a player's career XP, promoted roles or chosen path from this type, and nothing reports the loss.
  3. **TWO `RivalryState` DEFINITIONS** — mirror :446 vs engine `rival_career_engine.go:305` — the same shadowing arrangement as `CareerXP`, with the same silent-drop consequence.

The `...Locked` pair is implemented and its contract is DOCUMENTED IN PLACE (:46-53, :200-210, :216-218) — including WHY the name carries the suffix (the lock gates key on it).

  4. **THE FLOAT-ON-MONEY SURFACE IS CARRIED INTO THE CLIENT:** `Club.Treasury float64` (:38, beside the integer `TreasuryMicro` :37) · `Club.Commission` :39 · `Lease.Price float64` :80 (a float PRICE) · `CommissionEvent.Amount float64` :72 · `Rumor.Strength` :529 · `HoldingBonus.Multiplier` :114 · `ServerCard.Rarity` :210 · `NetworkConfig.PowerDivisor` :195 · `PlayerStats.Aggressiveness`/`RiskTolerance` :424-425 · `MojoDecayRate` :417 · `PlaystyleTendencies` (:474-481). The mirror therefore reproduces the server's float fields verbatim — the client is where a float price is most likely to be ARITHMETICALLY used.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code):**
  1. **EVERY SPLIT IS COMPUTED IN FLOAT ON LEDGER MONEY.** `fRouted = uint64(math.Floor(float64(actualTaxPayload) * matrix.FaucetShare))` (:326), the same for the club share (:334), the governance share (:365), the entity dividend (:444) and the creator royalty (:466) — five float multiplies per event, on the ONE function every fee in the game passes through. The shares are floats BY TYPE (:26-30) and validation is a float epsilon compare (`math.Abs(totalAllocation-1.0) > 1e-9` :226). The Ledger forbids float for fees; here it is structural, not incidental.
  2. **THE ADMIN SIPHON'S AUDIT FIGURE IS OVERWRITTEN ONE SCREEN LATER.** The first block (:231-245) computes `siphonAmt := totalTaxPayload / 10`, credits `AdminMaintenancePool` and sets `siphonAmount`; SEVEN lines later `siphonAmount = 0` (:255) resets it for the second block, so the `InterceptAndAudit` call (:360) reports only the SECOND siphon while the first has already left the pool. An audit figure that understates a siphon is the false-statement class — in the one place the file itself calls "Real-time Reconciliation" (:357).
  3. **DUPLICATED PRINT STATEMENTS ON THE PAYOUT PATHS.** The identical `" [Payout Error] Failed to distribute …"` appears twice in a row (:152-154) and `" [Payout Success] …"` twice (:176-178) — copy-paste residue inside a daemon.
  4. **THE ROLLBACK CAN RE-CREDIT A DIFFERENT DISTRICT THAN IT DEBITED.** Payouts are ACCUMULATED per governor ADDRESS across possibly several districts (:127-137 `payoutTargets[metric.GovernorAddress] += …`), but on failure the fail-safe scans `RegionalDistricts` and adds the WHOLE amount to the FIRST district matching that address (:157-165) — so a governor holding two districts gets their combined payout returned to one pool.
  5. **`GetVBVGatingMultiplier()` — THE FLOAT WRAPPER — SCALES XP HERE** (`scaledTaxXP := uint64(float64(baseXPAudited) * vbvMultiplier)` :469, `scaledBonusXP := uint64(float64(bonusXP) * vbvMultiplier)` :476, and a `vbvMultiplier > 1.0` display branch :481), the exact shape session (f)/(j) replaced with the integer owner `GetVBVGatingPermille`. This file still uses the float, so the Tax-Auditor award is float-scaled on every tax event.
  6. **THE TAX-AUDITOR HOOK DROPS THE LOBBY READ LOCK INSIDE ITS OWN LOOP AND MUTATES THROUGH A POINTER.** `RLock` :453 → `for wallet := range lobby.wallets` :456 → `RUnlock` :458 for a matching wallet, the award block runs UNLOCKED, then `RLock` :483 / `RUnlock` :487 in the same iteration. `stats` is a copy taken from the map but `stats.CareerXP` is a POINTER, so `TrackCareerXP` :478/:482 mutates the stored record while other goroutines may be reading it. Releasing inside the `range` is the safe direction; the unlocked pointer mutation is not.
  7. **A CLUB ID IS RE-DERIVED AND ITS PARSE ERROR DISCARDED** — `numericID, _ := strconv.ParseUint(strings.TrimPrefix(club.ID, "CLUB-"), 10, 64)` at :528 and again at :180 — so a club id not shaped `CLUB-<n>` silently becomes 0, no node is found, and the bailout silently does nothing (the discarded-parse family of `onboarding_service.go:466`).
  8. **A BAILOUT WRITES THE ALIAS BY FLOAT** (`club.Treasury = float64(node.TreasuryBalance)` :536, "Syncing alias for UI") and decrements the faucet with a float mirror (:542) — a stored balance written as a float.
  9. **`ProcessAllRegionalDividends` HAS NO AUDIT AND REACHES AROUND ITS OWN INTERFACE:** six `fmt.Printf`s are the entire record, and it type-asserts its ledger back to `*Lobby` (`ps.Ledger.(*Lobby)` :183, :189) to touch lobby state.
  10. **`IsTaxHavenActive` (:498-501) and `ExternalLedgerClient` (:72-74) are the only two members with no reader/caller measured in this file**; the interface exists so the scheduler can transfer, and the ONE implementation it is given is a `*Lobby` — **ambiguous - cited lines only** for whether any other implementation exists.

- **PURPOSE:** THE reader for every on-chain state record — the ONE place a checkpoint is fetched, reassembled from its envelope chunks, ordered by sequence, and resolved between a family record and an A5 batch (:17-53, :176-200).

- **ENTRY POINTS:** `l.readRecordFamily(cfg, vault, prefix) ([]byte, int64, bool, error)` :228 · `l.readRecordFamilyWithSource(...) ([]byte, int64, bool, string, error)` :241 · `l.readRecordSelections(cfg, vault, prefix) ([]recordSelection, error)` :184 · `l.indexerCheckpointTransfers(cfg, vault, prefix) ([]snapshotTransfer, error)` :88 · `isVaultAuthoredCheckpoint(sender, vault) bool` :71.
- **CALLS OUT:** `l.indexerRequest(cfg, path)` (the ONE indexer transport, `oracle_service.go`) :113 · `isVaultCheckpointTransfer` + `warnSnapshotScopeUnknown` (`snapshot_guard.go`) :192-205 · `parseRecordNote` / `assembleRecords` / `recordGapSummary` / `decodeSnapshotPayload` / `recordEnvelopeTag` (`record_envelope.go`) :207, :214, :216, :265, :569 · `RecordFamilyKeyForPrefix` / `recordNonceSeed` (`record_families.go` / the writer) :360, :498, :555 · `recordBatchFamily` / `recordBatchSequences` (`record_batch.go`) :414, :485 · **`recordSourceOrdering`** (`record_batch_read.go`) :521 · `RecordSourceBatch` / `RecordSourceFamilyRecord` / `RecordSourceNone`.
- **CALLED BY:** the three boot readers — `loadLeaderboard`, `loadEconomyState`, `loadBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked` (`lobby_manager.go`) — and, through `readRecordSelections`, the A5 batch path itself. No caller re-implements the loop and no caller re-declares the family list.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** **no mutex is taken anywhere in the file.** It reads `cfg` (a NetworkConfig VALUE passed in) and the vault address, performs HTTP, and mutates only the writer's nonce seed via `recordNonceSeed` :498, :558 (a seed, not engine state). `resp.Body` is closed by the transport's buffered reader, not here.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code). The file's design is sound; the findings are its announced limits and three sharp edges:**
  1. **IT IS MOJIBAKE-CORRUPTED — level 2, the worst case measured in the tree.** The ledger's census records `checkpoint_indexer_read.go` **L1 U+00E2 = 326, L2 U+00C3 = 316, U+00C2 = 305** — i.e. TWO levels in the visible header (`Ã¢â€¢ÂÃ¢â€¢Â…`, `Ã¢â‚¬â€�`, `Ã‚Â§36`) at :15-16, :18. A repair therefore needs TWO passes here, and the file is the reader the whole record rail depends on.
  2. **THE GAP-REPORTING IS A LOG, NOT A RETURNED FACT.** `assembleRecords` yields `gaps` which are logged with an explanation (:214-220) but never surfaced in the return contract, so a caller cannot distinguish "the newest complete record" from "the newest complete record OF A LOG THAT HAS A HOLE IN IT". `readRecordFamily`'s four-value contract (:228-232) has no field for it.
  3. **THE SENDER ASSERTION IS THE ONLY AUTHENTICATION, AND IT IS THE RIGHT ONE — but it is applied to a PROJECTION.** `From` and `To` are both set to the reported sender (:132-137) so `isVaultCheckpointTransfer` re-checks a value the reader itself constructed; the assertion that MATTERS is `isVaultAuthoredCheckpoint` (:124), and it is done. Recorded so the two checks are not mistaken for two independent controls.

- **STATE & LOCKS:** FOUR lock domains in one file — `EntityMarket.mu` :132, :239, :264 · `RivalryGroup.mu` :322, :338, :429 · `PetBattleArena.mu` :353, :388, :523 · and **no lock at all** for the lobby state they all reach into (`l.pets`, `l.vehicles`, `l.playerBalances`, `l.aiEngine`). `computeRivalryBonus` takes `rivalryGroups.mu.RLock` INSIDE `ResolveBattle`'s `ba.mu.Lock` window (:388 → :429) — a nested take of a DIFFERENT mutex, in an order no other site uses.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

  4. **A 404 IS TREATED AS A CONFIGURATION ERROR FOR THIS HOST** (:120-122) — correct given `indexerGet` advances past a 404 — but the message names the path, so when BOTH hosts 404 the caller sees only the last one's explanation; the diagnostic that spans the whole chain lives in `warnCheckpointReadUnavailable` (`snapshot_guard.go`), not here.
  5. **THE BATCH IS READ ON EVERY CALL, EVEN WHEN THE FAMILY RECORD WON** (:363, with the comment stating why: only both sequences can decide). That is one extra indexer round-trip per family read, by design, and it is the deliberate cost recorded in plan §15.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code):**
  1. **THE MONEY MOVES UNDER A LOCK THAT DOES NOT OWN IT.** `PurchaseListing` debits the buyer and credits the seller by the exact `PriceMicro` (:282-284, a pure transfer — correct for a player-to-player sale) but runs under `em.mu` only, while `l.playerBalances` is LOBBY-owned (`l.mutex`) and is one of the maps the unfenced-map census covers. A purchase concurrent with a fee route accesses the same map with no common lock — the §31/§35 hazard class, on the money map itself, and no gate models `em.mu → l.playerBalances`.
  2. **EVERY MARKET, ARENA AND FACTION WRITE REACHES LOBBY STATE WITH NO LOBBY LOCK.** `handleEntityMarketCreate` (:495) → `l.pets`/`l.vehicles`/`l.aiEngine`; `ResolveBattle` mutates `l.pets[...].Stats` and `l.playerBalances` (:415-421); `JoinFaction` mutates a global. The package globals `entityMarket`/`petBattleArena` were deliberate (they outlive a Lobby), but they hold no reference to the lock that owns what they mutate.
  3. **THE BATTLE REWARD IS MINTED.** `battle.RewardMicro = 500000` (0.5 $VBV) is credited to the winner's owner (:416, :419) with **no debit anywhere** — a new liability per resolved battle, against the Industrial Loop's "no silent minting". The arena is LIVE (three routed handlers), not dormant.
  4. **A SIGNED BONUS IS CAST TO `uint64`, INVERTING THE DEFENDER'S ADVANTAGE.** `computeRivalryBonus` returns **+5 for the challenger** and **−5 for the defender** (:433-437), and `ResolveBattle` computes `challengerPower+uint64(rivalryBonus)` (:399, repeated :403) — so `-5` becomes **18,446,744,073,709,551,611** added to the challenger's power. The same `uint64(negative)` class session (j) repaired in the rival-XP path; here it is live on a resolved battle.
  5. **THE FACTION BONUS SCALES WITH THE NUMBER OF FACTIONS, NOT WITH THE RIVALRY.** The loop ranges EVERY faction (:430-437) adding ±5 each time membership differs, so joining N factions yields ±5N; and the function never compares the two seeded KINDS (`vehicle_builders` vs `breeders`, :611-612) that the file's own comments define as the rivalry (:73-76, :106).
  6. **`ResolveBattle` PANICS ON A MISSING PET.** `l.pets[battle.Challenger].PetLevel` (:397-398) dereferences with no ok-check, so a pet sold, transferred or removed between challenge and resolution (the challenge stores only the ID) nil-dereferences under the arena's write lock.
  7. **A LISTING CAN OUTLIVE OWNERSHIP, AND THE MAP NEVER SHRINKS.** `CreateListing` verifies ownership only at creation (:136-161) and nothing cancels a listing when the entity changes hands elsewhere; `ListListings` HIDES expired listings (:254-256) but never deletes them, so `em.listings` grows without bound. Compare `bonded_market_service.go`, which re-proves §27.8 on every read and cancels stale listings.
  8. **`ListListings` RETURNS `nil` FOR AN EMPTY RESULT** (`var results []*EntityListing` :245) — encoded as JSON `null`, not `[]`: the class earlier sessions fixed in auctions, loans and the admin endpoints (`loan_service.go:32-33` states the rule).
  9. **THE MARKET, ARENA AND FACTIONS LIVE IN PACKAGE GLOBALS** (:90-92, :117) and appear in NO record family and NO snapshot — so every listing, trade record, battle and faction membership is lost on restart, while the entities they reference are recorded.
  10. **NO CALLER CHECK ON THE ARENA AND FACTION DOORS.** `handlePetBattleResolve` (:554) takes a `battle_id` from the body and resolves ANY battle — and by defect 3 the reward goes to the owner, so a third party can trigger a mint for someone else; `handleRivalryJoin` (:576) accepts any `faction_id` for any resolved wallet.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the entity market, the pet-battle arena and the rivalry factions) — three surfaces in one file with four mutexes, holding their state OUTSIDE the Lobby while mutating the Lobby's money and pets without its lock; the LISTING SHAPE is now correct (`Level`/`Stats`/`PowerScore`/`Mature` report the real build, §25.6.1) while defects 1-10 are behaviour, not presentation.

  6. `readRecordSelections`'s body was mechanically re-indented from the original `readRecordFamily` (the extraction recorded in the A5 note), which is why the lines there carry a padded blank-line rhythm (:185-224) — cosmetic, and it makes future diffs of that body noisy.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the ONE read path for every record family and of the vault-authored assertion) — the file that replaced three per-family readers with one that reassembles the envelope, orders by sequence and NAMES its source; its announced limits (gap-as-log, one extra round-trip) are stated rather than hidden, and its corruption is the second level of the mojibake the ledger already measured.

- **VERDICT:** owner (of every fee split, the entity-dividend route and the governor payout daemon) — its LOCK discipline is the repository's model (a documented `...Locked` pair, the lobby→router order, remainder rerouting to the faucet) while its ARITHMETIC is float at every split, the admin-siphon audit understates, one rollback crosses districts, and the Tax-Auditor hook still scales XP with the float wrapper the integer owner replaced.

  5. **THE MOJO RULE EXISTS IN FOUR PLACES.** `FaceplateRegistry` is declared TWICE (here :127 and `common_types.go:135`), and the rule that applies it is implemented three times — `common_types_wasm.go:135` (`Player` receiver), `common_types.go:160` (`PlayerStats` receiver) and `player_service.go:42` (`*PlayerService` method) — plus TWO inline re-reads of the registry (`main.go:529`, `economy_service.go:512-521`). Two copies of one table and three of one rule cannot be kept in agreement by construction.
  6. `HoldingBonus` (:111-116) is still DECLARED although its only consumer field was retired (`Problems.md` §40, A4) — a type kept after its use is gone; mild, but it is the sort of residue that makes a later reader think the bonus is live.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the client's view of the shared types) — a mirror by construction, and therefore the one file whose correctness is a DRIFT question rather than a logic question: measured drift exists (one type shadowed three times, 15 `PlayerStats` fields and two record fields missing on the client), and NO test or gate in the tree compares the two files.

  5. **`slideThemeSet`'s rejection of a client-owned field is a DECODER error, not a domain refusal** (:477-484) — the correct fail-closed shape, but it is reported with the same 200 (defect 1) and the same generic message as a malformed JSON body, so a client cannot distinguish "you sent `target_kind`" from "your JSON was broken".
- **VERDICT:** owner (of slide theming and of the six-slot vocabulary) — a clean layering (vocabulary → policy → storage-with-copies → authority → HTTP) whose refusals all happen before any write and whose ownership is re-proved on every read; its defects are a status-code contract that says 200 for every refusal, one duplicated rule, and stale rows that are reported rather than removed.

- **VERDICT:** test (owner of the "unlocked, never forced" evidence chain) — it converts an operator RULE into nine properties, and its central assertion ("the request asks and never promotes") is the guard that stops employment from becoming a way to force a career on somebody.

### `faucet_service.go` — 615 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-615)
- **PURPOSE:** the PAYOUT rail that satisfies `ExternalLedgerClient.TransferTokens` for the `PayoutScheduler`, and the HTTP reward claim door — the ONE surface that turns the in-memory ledger into real $VBV (:30-36, :155-160, :399-407).
- **OWNS:** `TransferTokens` (the governor path) :33-152 · `handleReward` (rate limit → identity match → score match → nonce → reverse signature → dispatch) :155-367 · `verifyVoiPayoutOptIn` :370-388 · **`dispatchReward`** (multipliers, exit siphon, per-asset opt-in/vault-balance checks, safety cap, rollback circuits, signed group, confirmation wait) :390-615 · the note prefixes it uses (`NotePrefixGovDividend` :118, `NotePrefixWin` :445) · the IP entry in `l.rateLimits` and the in-flight marker in `l.processingRewards`.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `l.TransferTokens(ctx, toWallet, amount)` :33 · `l.handleReward(w, r)` :155 (`POST /api/reward`) · `l.verifyVoiPayoutOptIn(recipient)` :370 · `l.dispatchReward(recipient, claimant, network, history)` :390.
- **CALLS OUT:** `l.multiChainRouter.TransferToChain(ctx, to, amount, "voi")` :63 · `algod.MakeClient` + `AccountInformation` (gas floor) :55-59, :428-434 · `mnemonic.ToPrivateKey(os.Getenv("FAUCET_MNEMONIC"))` :92, :610 · `l.oracleService.CheckAssetOptIn` :376, :503 · `l.oracleService.FetchARC200Balance` :526 · `Audit.LogPhysicalOutflow` :79, :609 · `l.applyDynamicScalingLocked` :76, :146, :606 · `l.playerBalances` (written) · `l.rewardStack` · `l.leaderboard` · `l.matchHistory` / `l.wallets` / `l.nonces` / `l.rateLimits` / `l.processingRewards` · `l.logAdminAudit` · `logWinAudit` :613 · `ethcrypto` (personal_sign recovery) :258-268 · `MaxGovPayoutMicro` / `MaxSinglePayoutMicro` · the algosdk tx builders, `ComputeGroupID`, `SignTransaction`, `SendRawTransaction`, `WaitForConfirmation`.
- **CALLED BY:** the `PayoutScheduler` daemon (`economy_processing.go:137`) through the `ExternalLedgerClient` interface · the route table for `POST /api/reward`.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** the pattern is **short read → release → network → short write**, applied consistently: `RLock`/`RUnlock` around every config snapshot (:38-42, :374-377, :398-404, :513-515), a scoped lock for the IP rate limit (:200-208), a scoped lock for the identity/score checks and the in-flight marker (:225-260) with a DEFERRED cleanup (:262-267), and a scoped lock for each ledger mutation (:548-550, :558-560, :567-569, :588-590, :597-611). **No lock is ever held across an indexer or chain call**, and the two maps it creates are initialised defensively (:230-232).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code):**
  1. **FLOATS DECIDE THE PAYOUT AMOUNT.** `multiplier := 1.1` (reputation, :454-458) and a mojo ladder `1.25 / 1.15 / 1.10 / 1.05` (:462-472) are folded in as `amt := (baseAmt * uint64(multiplier*100)) / 100` (:473) — quantised to hundredths, so "Integer Supremacy" holds only at that rounding. The BOUNTY portion is a full float round-trip: `finalBounty := float64(history.BountyRewardMicro)/1000000.0 * mojoMultiplier` then `amt += uint64(finalBounty * 1000000)` (:490-492). `totalUnits += float64(amt)/1000000.0` (:532) accumulates in float for the log only.
  2. **THE 2 % EXIT SIPHON LEAVES THE PLAYER AND REACHES NO DESTINATION.** `exitSiphonMicro := (virtualBalance * 2) / 100` and `actualPayoutMicro := virtualBalance - exitSiphonMicro` (:466-468), followed by an AUDIT line only (:470). The comment says it "returns to the Faucet pool to maintain mathematical circularity"; **nothing credits it** — the 2 % is burned from the player's balance, against the Industrial Loop's remainder rule (every remainder must reach the faucet, the treasury or an approved sink).
  3. **THE VIRTUAL BALANCE IS ZEROED BEFORE THE TRANSACTION EXISTS, AND FOUR ROLLBACKS ASSUME IT WAS NOT.** `virtualBalance := l.playerBalances[claimant]; l.playerBalances[claimant] = 0` (:449-451), then four separate failure paths restore it in four separate lock windows (:549, :559, :568, :589). Between the zeroing and any rollback the wallet observably reads 0, and a process death in that window loses the balance entirely — it is in no ledger and on no chain.
  4. **A SUCCESSFUL PAYOUT CAN STILL SILENTLY DROP SECONDARY ASSETS.** The per-asset skips `continue` (:504, :508, :519, :524, :530) and only the PRIMARY asset's absence triggers the partial rollback (:566-571); when the primary IS included but a later asset was skipped, the virtual balance stays 0 while only part of the group is sent — recorded because the rollback comment claims a completeness nothing checks.
  5. **THE RATE LIMIT AND THE IN-FLIGHT MARKER KEY ON DIFFERENT SUBJECTS.** The limiter uses the REMOTE IP for a 30-second window (:200-208) while `l.processingRewards` uses the CLAIMANT (:236-241) — two wallets behind one IP share one bucket, and one wallet from two IPs gets two attempts (the claimant key still serialises them, so this is throttling, not a hole).
  6. **FOUR SIGNATURE RAILS NOW, AND THE ALGORAND BRANCH PROVES NO SIGNATURE.** The EVM path recovers the signer and compares it to the claimant (:258-290); the Algorand path compares `stx.Txn.Sender.String() != req.Claimant` and the note (:300-311) with **no cryptographic check** — the msgpack decode is treated as proof — and a JSON fallback decode widens the accepted shape (:295-299). B2's "never a second verifier" rule is now measured at four shapes (admin gate, oracle digest, `verifyEVMSignature`, and this door).
  7. **DISCARDED ERRORS ON EVERY SEPARATE PATH.** `algod.MakeClient` :55, :430 · `crypto.AccountFromPrivateKey` :96, :439 · `types.DecodeAddress(toWallet)` :105 · `strconv.ParseUint(voiConfig.AssetID, …)` :115 · `types.DecodeAddress(vaultAddr)` :116, :437 · `sp, _ := client.SuggestedParams().Do(...)` :443 · `crypto.SignTransaction` inside the group loop :578. A malformed address or asset id yields a zero address / app id 0 and the transaction is STILL BUILT AND SIGNED — the `loan_service.go:434-447` family, in the file that actually pays players.
  8. **AN OVER-DRAFTING PAYOUT IS SILENTLY FLATTENED RATHER THAN REFUSED.** `TransferTokens` dispatches through the router (:63) and then subtracts `amount`, writing **0** when the reservoir is short (:70-74, and again at :600-604) — the shortfall is discarded instead of recorded, so the ledger simply reports a drained pool.
  9. **THE GROUP CONFIRMATION IS NOT AWAITED.** `transaction.WaitForConfirmation(client, firstTxID, 4, context.Background())` is called with **no return capture** (:595), so an unconfirmed or failed group still updates the ledger, the faucet and the scaling. The legacy single-transaction path guards its debit on `err == nil` (:136-145), so the two paths disagree about what "sent" means.

- **VERDICT:** owner (of the payout rail and of the reward claim door) — its LOCK discipline is exactly the shape this repository standardised (no lock across a chain call, every mutation in a scoped window, the in-flight marker with a deferred cleanup) while its MONEY arithmetic is float-derived at two multipliers and one bounty, its 2 % exit siphon is removed without a destination, and the virtual balance is zeroed before the transaction exists.

- **PURPOSE:** the ENTITY INVESTMENT domain — a player buys into an `EntityMarketNode`'s dividend pool, claims accrued yield, and reads a portfolio; plus the hourly revenue-injection ticker and the dormant-but-corrected `ProcessEntityRevenueDistribution` (:16-37, :60-66).

- **ENTRY POINTS:** WS `handleInvestEntity` / `handleClaimDividends`; HTTP `POST /api/invest/entity`, `POST /api/claim/dividends`, `GET /api/invest/portfolio`, `GET /api/invest/dividends/history`; internal `InitializeEntityInvestmentSystem` (boot) · `startDividendDistributionTicker` · `ProcessHourlyEntityRevenueDistribution` · `ProcessEntityRevenueDistribution` · `CalculateTotalPortfolioValue`/`…Locked` · `NewEntityDividendTracker` · `proportionalMicro`.

- **STATE & LOCKS:** this file is the repository's **reference example of the corrected shapes, stated in place at every site**: the direct-invest doors take `l.mutex` for the body, price the portfolio **BEFORE** `node.Mu` (the hoist that removed the self-deadlock), hold `node.Mu` for the transaction and never re-take it (:132-160, :541-547, :602-605); the claim doors take `node.Mu` per entity and release before the next (:274-320, :700-751); the router mutex is a scoped `RLock`/`RUnlock` around **lookups only** at five sites; the hourly ticker walks `marketNodeRefs()` (a value snapshot released inside the router lock) then takes each node's WRITE lock ALONE with the freeze check INSIDE it (:905-944); `HandleDividendHistory` snapshots two maps under `l.mutex.RLock()` and derives outside (:811-822); `proportionalMicro` takes no lock by contract (:436-441). `l.dividendTracker.Mu` exists and is **never taken by anyone** — the file says so itself (:813-815).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1


- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code). The lock work is exemplary; the defects are all ARITHMETIC, OWNERSHIP or TRUTHFULNESS:**
  1. **THE DIVIDEND CLAIM IS FLOAT THROUGHOUT, ON A MONEY PATH.** `daysSinceLastDistribution := now.Sub(lastDistTime).Hours() / 24.0` (:287, :717), `dailyYieldRate := 0.005` with `accruedDividends := uint64(float64(node.DividendPoolMicro) * dailyYieldRate * daysSinceLastDistribution)` (:295-297, :725-727), then the share `uint64(float64(accruedDividends) * float64(investedAmount) / float64(node.DividendPoolMicro))` (:303, :733) — three float operations to produce an integer, while the SAME file demonstrates the exact integer form (`proportionalMicro`) in the revenue distributor (:419). Two owners for one rule, one of them float.
  2. **THE ANTI-CONCENTRATION CAP AND THE PROTOCOL FEE ARE FLOAT MULTIPLIES.** `maxEntityInvestment := uint64(float64(portfolioValueMicro) * 0.25)` at BOTH doors (:201, :596) and `feeMicro := uint64(float64(data.AmountMicro)*0.01 + 0.5)` (:226, :636) — while `proportionalMicro` computes exactly the first one without a float, and `/100` would be exact for the second.
  3. **A CLAIM MINTS AND NEVER DRAWS DOWN THE POOL.** `l.playerBalances[wallet] += totalDividend` (:325, :753) and `stats.TotalDividendClaimedMicro += totalDividend` (:330, :758) while **`node.DividendPoolMicro` is NEVER decremented by a claim** — the only accounting that moves is `LastDistribution[entityID] = now` (:308, :738), which throttles the rate but consumes nothing. `ProcessEntityRevenueDistribution` and the hourly ticker INJECT into that pool; nothing here ever takes out, so the same pool can be claimed repeatedly for ever.
  4. **THE CLAIM APPLIES A SECOND, UNRELATED YIELD.** The pool is fed at `reserve/24000` per hour (:933, ≈0.1 %/day of the RESERVE) while the claim computes **0.5 %/day of `DividendPoolMicro`** (:295) — two rates over two bases, so a claim's yield is not a share of what was injected.
  5. **ONE SHARED LAST-DISTRIBUTION STAMP, RESET BY THE FIRST CLAIMER.** `LastDistribution[entityID] = now` is written inside a CLAIM (:308, :738), so whoever claims first resets the clock for every other investor and later claimers inside the same hour are refused. With defect 3 the effect is a first-claimer lottery rather than a pro-rata distribution.
  6. **THE PORTFOLIO SHARE VALUATION IS 100× OUT, AND THE COMMENT SAYS SO.** `sharesAsMicro := uint64(shares * 100.0) // Convert float shares to micro-shares` (:479) — a micro-unit is 1e−6, not 1e−2 — and the total is `sharesAsMicro * valuePerShare` with `valuePerShare := node.ReserveBalance / node.TotalSharesIssued` (:483-488), 10,000× the true share value, against `stats.Portfolio` being a `map[string]uint64` everywhere else. It matters because this number is the DENOMINATOR of the 25 % cap (defect 2), so the cap is effectively ~2,500× the portfolio.
  7. **A FLOAT YIELD FIELD IS STORED AS STATE.** `EntityInvestmentRecord.CumulativeYield float64` (:22), written at :207 and :627 from `float64(node.CumulativeYieldPerShare)/100.0`, is persisted in `l.playerInvestmentRecords` and never read back in this file.
  8. **THE TRACKER'S MUTEX IS DECORATIVE, AND IT HOLDS RAW POINTERS INTO LIVE NODES.** `tracker.EntityPools[entityID] = &node.DividendPoolMicro` (:52) stores each node's field ADDRESS while holding only the router read lock, and `l.dividendTracker.LastDistribution` is written at :308/:738 and read at :287/:717 with **no `EntityDividendTracker.Mu`** — the file's own comment admits the mutex is never taken (:813-815) and names the lobby lock as the map's real owner. That is true today (both claim paths hold it) and is stated nowhere else.
  9. **`proportionalMicro`'s CLAMP INFLATES INSTEAD OF REFUSING.** `if part >= whole { return amount }` (:434-436) — a "share" that is not a share of the whole pays the WHOLE amount, so a caller whose `whole` was computed wrongly gets a full payout rather than an error. Documented as a deliberate clamp; recorded because the failure mode is silent over-payment.
  10. **FOUR SITES LAUNCH AN UNBOUNDED GOROUTINE PER PLAYER ACTION** (`go func() { l.broadcast <- l.getLobbyUpdateMsg() }()` at :250, :332, :664, :761), each building the full lobby payload. A cost, not a bug — recorded because the guard lives inside the builder, not at the launch sites.
  11. **`HandleDirectInvest` MARSHALS TWO PAYLOADS AND DISCARDS BOTH.** `_, _ = json.Marshal(investmentPayload)` (:648) and `_, _ = json.Marshal(portfolioUpdate)` (:668) while the WS twin SENDS both (:232-251) — so the HTTP door silently drops `investment_confirmed`/`investment_update` events, and the two doors differ in exactly that.

- **PURPOSE:** the §30/§31 Pet-World engine — the entity STAT vector (integer 1..100), the event that trains/rewards/punishes it, the power overlay that caps an entity's effective level in the 3D world, the owner relation graph, the orphan/adoption flow, and the payout loop that credits owners (:14-26, :418-424).

- **ENTRY POINTS:** HTTP `GET /api/entity-events/regions`, `POST …/host|resolve`, `GET /api/owner/combined-stats`, `POST …/adopt|reclaim`, `GET …/orphan-status`, `GET /api/faith/coherence`, `POST …/faith-war-gambit`; internal `ComputeEffectivePowerLevel` · `ApplyEventResult` · `applyStatDelta` · `BaseUserEntityStats` · `CombineStats` · `CombineOwnerStats` · `BuildRegionPowerOverlay` · `IsOrphaned` · `AdoptEntity`/`ReclaimEntity` · `ProcessEntityEvent`/`ResolveEntityEventPayout` · `SortEntitiesByPower` · the graph accessors.
