- **ENTRY POINTS:** `GET /api/assets/catalogue` → `handlePlaceholderCatalogue` :481 · `POST /api/assets/starter` → `handleStarterAssets` :518 · `POST /api/assets/purchase` → `handleBuyPlaceholderSku` :548 · `l.EnsureStarterAssets(wallet)` · `l.BuyPlaceholderSku(wallet, sku)` · `PlaceholderCatalogue()`/`PlaceholderCatalogueError()`/`PlaceholderCatalogueViews()` · `scanPlaceholderPack(root)` (shared with the derivative pass) · `placeholderSku(sku)` (used by `slide_theming.go`).
- **CALLS OUT:** `l.bondedAssets.OwnedSku` / `MintCatalogueAsset` / `Burn` :385, :449, :454 · `l.chargeBondedAssetFeeLocked` (the ONE bonded-asset fee door) :452 · `l.BondedAssetCapacityForWallet` · `l.logAdminAudit`/`logAdminAuditLocked` · `ValidateBondedMedia` + `BondedMediaMaxBytes`/`BondedMediaMaxDimension` + `BondedMediaLimitsForClient` · `PlaceholderDerivativeStatusForClient` · `writeJSON` / `l.getWalletFromRequest`.
- **CALLED BY:** the route tables of both servers; `slide_theming.go` (every slot's default art); `ui_tree_theming.go` (§10.8.1 tree art); `placeholder_derivatives.go` (the same SKU scan); `bonded_branding.js` + `slide_theming.js` on the client.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `placeholderCatalogOnce` guards the scan (one disk read per process) · `placeholderHashMu` guards the hash cache whose key includes the file's SIZE and MTIME, so a replaced frame is re-hashed (:283-312) · `l.mutex` is taken for the WHOLE of `BuyPlaceholderSku`'s money section (:437-439) with the media built BEFORE the lock ("a SKU whose art cannot satisfy the policy is refused with nothing spent") · `balanceKeyLocked` documents "Caller must hold l.mutex" (:465-467). No lock is held across a file read in the money path.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1


- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code):**
  1. **THE CATALOGUE IS CACHED FOREVER AND NOTHING INVALIDATES IT.** `placeholderCatalogOnce` is a `sync.Once` (:217-219), so a frame ADDED to the pack after boot is invisible until a restart — while the derivative manifest it feeds explicitly RE-DERIVES on a source-hash change. The two caches disagree about what "changed" means.
  2. **`EnsureStarterAssets` IS NOT ATOMIC ACROSS THE SIX FRAMES, AND TAKES NO LOBBY LOCK.** It mints one asset per SKU in a loop (:375-394) and appends a reason on failure, so a partial grant (three of six) is a normal outcome reported honestly — but `OwnedSku` (the idempotency check) and `MintCatalogueAsset` are not one critical section, so two concurrent calls can both observe "not owned" for the same SKU.
  3. **`BuyPlaceholderSku`'s ROLLBACK DISCARDS ITS OWN ERROR.** The documented ordering is honest (balance check → mint → fee, burn on a fee refusal, :418-425, :450-456) — recorded because the rollback is `_ = l.bondedAssets.Burn(a.AssetID, w)` (:454): a failed rollback leaves the asset minted AND the fee unpaid, silently. (It mints with the type `AssetBackground` on BOTH paths, :394 and :448, so the type carries no information about what the frame is.)
  4. **`readPNGDimensions` CHECKS THE SIGNATURE AND THE IHDR TAG BUT NOT THE CRC** (:265-272): a corrupt IHDR yields dimensions with no complaint. Its diagnostics are the only diagnosis.
  5. **THE HASH CACHE'S LIMIT IS REAL AND ONLY THE COMMENT CARRIES IT** (:280-282): "a replacement that keeps BOTH the exact byte size and the exact modification time is not detectable by this cheap check" — while `slide_theming.go` and the catalogue both present `hash_hex` as the art's hash.
  6. **THE FREE GRANT IS REPEATABLE BY DESIGN AND UNBOUNDED PER CALL.** `EnsureStarterAssets` requires only a non-empty wallet (:354-361) and is idempotent BY OWNERSHIP, so a wallet that gives a frame away may receive it again ("the gift was theirs to make", :345-352). Stated deliberately; recorded because it means the grant has no cap and no rate limit of its own.
  7. **`placeholderSkuMedia` RE-VALIDATES ON EVERY MINT** (:327-331) against constant limits — a per-SKU failure is therefore possible at mint time even though the scan already recorded `Purchasable`, which is the correct belt-and-braces direction and worth recording as such.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the placeholder pack, its price and its two acquisition paths) — the strongest single file the sweep has read for REVIEWABILITY: the file list IS the SKU list, the media declaration carries MEASURED values (bytes, IHDR pixels, the sha256 of the actual bytes), the SAME `ValidateBondedMedia` boundary guards both user media and the house's own art, the price is server-owned with a body that may carry only a `sku`, and the remaining defect class (a partial grant, an unpruned catalogue cache, a discarded burn) is documented rather than hidden.

### `justice_service.go` — 597 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-597)
- **PURPOSE:** the Justice Hegemony path (Pillar 7) — the Justice CARD archetypes, the +10 % power bonus vs outlaws, the Justice Tier Bounty Center dashboard, and the two intelligence items (Truth Serum, Reputation Shield) (:3-10, :22-30).
- **OWNS:** the four archetypes (`ENFORCER`/`MEDIATOR`/`WARDEN`/`COMMISSIONER`) :22-30 · `JusticeFaction` / `JusticeCard` / `JusticeBuff` / `JusticeDebuff` / `JusticeMission` :33-89 · `TruthSerumItem` / `CardBuffState` / `ItemBuff` / `ReputationShieldItem` :92-123 · `JusticeBountyDashboard` / `BountyTargetInfo` :126-148 · **`JusticeService`** (its OWN `sync.RWMutex` and six maps/counters) :153-164 · `NewJusticeService` (the card pool + `minWantedForCard = 15`) :167-180 · the integration constants (`JusticeTierAccessCards` 3, `JusticeTierAccessRank` 2 = Warden, `JusticeOutlawBonusThreshold` 15, `TruthSerumDefaultDuration` 30 s, `ReputationShieldDefaultProtection` 50) :586-597.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `GetJusticeFaction` :186 · `GetPowerBonus` :198 · `CheckQualificationForJusticeCards` :218 · **`AwardJusticeCard`** :233 · `RemoveJusticeCard` :281 · **`ApplyTruthSerum`** :303 · `GetRevealedBuffs` :326 · **`ApplyReputationShield`** :338 · `AbsorbReputationLoss` :366 · `GetShieldRemaining` :397 · **`GenerateJusticeMission`** :412 · **`GetDashboardForPlayer`** :442 · `CheckMissionCompletion` :470 · `GetDashboardJSON`/`GetDashboardHTML`/`FormatDashboardHTML` :507-582 · the package helpers `CalculateJusticePowerMultiplier` :526 and `IsOutlawTarget` :549.
- **CALLS OUT:** the standard library only (`encoding/json`, `fmt`, `strings`, `sync`, `time`) — **no lobby reference, no chain access, no registry, no audit**. Its only bridge to the world is that callers pass `[]BountyTargetInfo` IN and read a dashboard OUT.
- **CALLED BY:** `HandleGetBountyActive` / `HandleGetJusticeMissionsHTTP` / `HandleAcceptJusticeMissionHTTP` (`handlers_public_new.go`, each passing `nil, 0`) · `handlers_criminality.go` (the dashboard and the truth-serum door) · `player_service.go` (the reputation weighting).
- **STATE & LOCKS:** ONE mutex, `js.mu`, taken per method and never nested — `Lock` for every mutation (:234, :282, :304, :339, :367, :413, :443, :471), `RLock` for every read (:187, :199, :219, :327, :398) — with one correct exception: **`GetDashboardForPlayer` takes the WRITE lock (:443) BECAUSE IT CACHES**, the same rule `GetPortfolioForPlayer` documents elsewhere. The two auto-expiry helpers spawn a goroutine per application that sleeps for the full duration and then re-takes the lock (:315-325, :351-362).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1


- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code). The whole service is IN-MEMORY ONLY** — six maps and one counter with no snapshot, no file and no record family — so every finding below is also unpersisted.
  1. **`GenerateJusticeMission` CREATES A MISSION AND THEN THROWS IT AWAY.** It builds the `JusticeMission` then `_ = mission` (:436-438) with the comment "(populated when dashboard is requested)" — the mission is never stored in `faction.Missions` nor anywhere else, and only the ID string is returned. Measured consequence: `CheckMissionCompletion` (:470-497) can never find a mission generated this way, so every mission from the ONLY generator is uncompletable, and `dash.ActiveMissions` is nil for every non-Warden and empty for every Warden — the served list is empty by construction.
  2. **THE DASHBOARD IS A PASS-THROUGH OF ITS OWN ARGUMENTS.** `GetDashboardForPlayer` copies the caller's slice and integer into the payload (:451-455) — and every caller in the tree passes `nil, 0`, so "High-Wanted Targets" is EMPTY and `ScrambledCount` is always 0 for every player. The only field it computes is `DashboardAccess`.
  3. **"ENHANCED TRACKING" IS A FLAG WRITE WITH NOTHING BEHIND IT.** For a Warden+, every `GhostActive` target gets `RealIDAvailable = true` (:464-468) — set on the caller's own slice copy, with the "real ID" itself never computed, stored or returned; `FormatDashboardHTML` merely renders a different CSS class for it (:570-577).
  4. **`CheckMissionCompletion` SWEEPS EVERY PLAYER'S CACHE AND EVERY FACTION** (:470-497) to find one id, returning on the FIRST match of a map iteration — so a duplicate id answers for whichever entry is visited first.
  5. **THE TWO EXPIRY GOROUTINES MATCH BY CONTENT AND LEAK.** `ApplyTruthSerum`'s cleanup removes the FIRST serum of that target whose `len(RevealedBuffs)` MATCHES (:315-325) — two serums with the same buff count can remove each other, and a changed count is never removed. Both helpers sleep for the full duration in a goroutine, so a 24-hour shield holds a goroutine and a timer per application, uncancellable.
  6. **`AwardJusticeCard` MINTS A POWER BONUS FROM A MONEY-SHAPED CONSTANT** (:259-265): 500 000 / 750 000 / 1 000 000 micro-units, labelled "0.5 $VBV equivalent in micro-units (flat power)" — the SAME unit means a power value here and money elsewhere.
  7. **`GetPowerBonus` COMPOUNDS INSIDE ITS OWN ACCUMULATOR.** `totalBonus += int64(float64(totalBonus) * (buff.Multiplier - 1.0))` (:210-214) reads `totalBonus` while modifying it, so the ORDER of the loop changes the answer, and a float multiplies a power figure.
  8. **THE JUSTICE POWER RULE EXISTS TWICE, DISAGREEING.** The method returns a flat integer bonus; the package helper `CalculateJusticePowerMultiplier` returns `multiplier := 1.10` plus `(buff.Multiplier-1.0) * len(JusticeCards)` (:538-543) — different arithmetic, different types, plus `IsOutlawTarget`'s separate threshold argument.
  9. **THE DASHBOARD CACHE IS WRITTEN AND NEVER LOOKED UP.** `js.dashboardCache[playerID] = dash` (:465) is its only write; the getter recomputes every call, and the map is read only by `CheckMissionCompletion`'s sweep — so it grows per requesting player and serves no lookup.
 10. **`FormatDashboardHTML` ASSEMBLES MARKUP IN GO** (:554-582) with `fmt.Sprintf` into a `strings.Builder`, escaping NOTHING — `target.PlayerName` and `target.District` are interpolated straight into an `<li>`, so a player name containing markup reaches whatever renders this string.

### `map_race_gate_test.go` — 614 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-614, read in windows 1-60, 60-201, 201-361, 361-471, 471-547, 547-614)
- **PURPOSE:** **the UNFENCED-MAP GATE** — no lobby-owning function may touch a lobby-owned map where its own lock cannot possibly be held (:17-58). Its stated reason is the worst failure mode in the tree: a concurrent map access is `fatal error: concurrent map read and map write` / `concurrent map iteration and map write` — **NOT an error value, NOT catchable with `recover`, and it takes the WHOLE PROCESS down** rather than the one request. It was written AFTER MEASURING, and its first run found FIVE registered routes reading lobby maps with no lock at all (`/api/player/profile`, `/api/invest/portfolio`, `/api/invest/dividends/history`, `/api/contracts/list`, `/api/contracts/assign`) — two of which also WROTE (:29-33).
- **OWNS:** the protected-set **derivation** `protectedLobbyMaps(dir)` reading map-typed fields of `type Lobby struct` from `backend_types.go` (:60-95) · `lobbyMapSource` (a NAME + SOURCE pair, which is what lets the controls drive the detector with synthetic code) :100-104 · `unfencedMapEntry` :107-114 · **`measureUnfencedLobbyMaps(sources, protected)`** (:122-223) · `entryHolds` — the transitive "entered with the lock held" fixpoint (:226-256) · `whyUnfenced` / `verdictOf` / `entriesByKey` (:258-296) · **`unfencedLobbyMapBaseline`** — the VERDICT-PINNED worklist (:317-390) · `realLobbyMapSources` (:393-410) · **`TestNoNewUnfencedLobbyMapAccess`** :413 · **`TestUnfencedMapGateDetectsAViolation`** :458.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** two tests. The gate itself, and its NEGATIVE CONTROL. There is no exported API and no production caller — the file is a measurement.
- **CALLS OUT:** `go/ast`, `go/parser`, `go/token` (the analysis is AST-based, not text-based), `os`, `path/filepath`, `sort`, `strings`, `testing`.
- **CALLED BY:** `go test` only. **`hasLock` is returned to the caller rather than kept private**, because the verdicts must be computed from the SAME measurement that produced the entries (:119-121).
- **STATE & LOCKS:** no runtime state — the gate reads the tree. Its own analysis is **ONE-HOP AND POSITIONAL, and it says so rather than implying it** (:44-57): (a) POSITIONAL — "no lock of its own" means the body acquires the lock NOWHERE, or only after the access; sound because a lock can only be held if an earlier statement took it, needing no control-flow modelling and having no branch-shaped false positives, with the acknowledged mirror image being a FALSE NEGATIVE (an access after a lock taken only inside an `if` is not reported) — the safe direction for a gate about an unfenced access; (b) ONE HOP — a function is judged by its OWN body and its DIRECT callers; (c) `...Locked` is honoured as this tree's ONE spelling for "the caller holds it"; (d) **`-race` IS UNAVAILABLE on this host (needs cgo, no C toolchain), so this is a STATIC census, not a race proof**; (e) it says NOTHING about whether the locking that IS present is correct — the recursive-lock class belongs to `selflock_gate_test.go` alone.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **VERDICT:** owner (of the Justice faction, its card archetypes and its two items) — a self-contained, correctly-locked, PERSISTENCE-FREE service whose central capability (missions) is a generator that discards its own mission, whose dashboard is a pass-through of arguments every caller leaves empty, whose power rule exists in two disagreeing implementations, and whose HTML formatter is the one place the tree builds markup without escaping.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS / INSTRUMENT LIMITS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).** This is the STRONGEST gate the sweep has read, so what follows is mostly its own disclosed limits plus the work still owed — and one gate-design finding:
  1. **IT PROVES THE DETECTOR CAN FAIL, TWICE OVER.** `TestUnfencedMapGateDetectsAViolation` drives SIX shapes that MUST report (`badUnlockedRead`, `badUnlockedRange`, `freeFnBad`, `BadExportedDoor`, `badChainParent`, `contractHelper`) and FOUR that MUST NOT (`fineLockedRead`, `fineNamedContractLocked`, `fineNoMap`, `fineOtherMutex`), plus a CLEAN-FILE case that must report nothing (:565-613). The reason is stated in its own words: *without this, a detector that silently matched nothing would report a clean repository for ever, which is exactly how a gate rots* (:454-457).
  2. **THE CONTROL CAUGHT THE GATE'S OWN AUTHOR, AND THE CAPTURE IS IN THE FILE.** The first draft expected the CHILD of a chain to be reported; the detector reports the ACCESS, not the reachability, so the child is not an entry and its name travels in the PARENT's EVIDENCE (:593-603). The assertion now checks exactly that (`reasons["Lobby.badChainParent"]` must CONTAIN `Lobby.badChainChild`), which is why the evidence names the unlocked caller instead of merely asserting one.
  3. **AN EXPORTED DOOR IS PINNED AS PERMANENTLY UNFENCED** (:587-592, and `BadExportedDoor` in the control) — even when every caller it HAS holds the lock — because its callers are unconstrained. That is the correct conservative rule and the baseline's largest UNFENCED group (`Lobby.CalculateReputation`, `Lobby.getClubByTerritoryID`, `ensurePlayerStatsMapsInitialized`, the `TransferBundleItems` family across four services).
  4. **THE BASELINE IS A VERDICT-PINNED WORKLIST AND FAILS ON THREE THINGS** (:300-316): NEW (an unfenced function in no line), STALE (a baselined function that is no longer unfenced — the fix MUST delete its line, or the baseline legitimises rot) and FLIPPED (a CONTRACT entry that became UNFENCED because a lock-holding helper gained a caller holding nothing). The FLIPPED clause is the one that protects the `...Locked` idiom from being widened silently.
  5. **THE BASELINE WAS WRITTEN BY THE GATE'S OWN DERIVATION, AND THE FILE SAYS SO.** It reads **all 47 map fields of `Lobby`** rather than the hand-written subset the first measurement used, and it immediately found FIVE functions touching maps the earlier census did not know were protected (`inventory`, `persistentCardCache`, `lastActive`, `onboardedWallets`, `availableNetworks`) — the gate found its author's blind spot (:378-386).
  6. **ONE ENTRY WAS DELETED WITH ITS REASON, WHICH IS THE DISCIPLINE WORKING.** `TournamentService.RecordTournamentOnChain` was baselined UNFENCED and the line was removed on 2026-09-19 (Problems §39) after its rewrite stopped reading `l.availableNetworks` while holding no lock — **the gate FAILED on the stale line and was right to** (:387-390). One entry is likewise annotated REMOVED for `CalculateTotalPortfolioValue` after the `...Locked` pair landed (:330-333).
  7. **THE REMAINING UNFENCED ENTRIES ARE THE OWED-LOCK LEDGER, and several are in files this sweep has already mapped:** `EntityEventEngine.ProcessEntityEvent` / `ResolveEntityEventPayout` / `ownerWalletOf`, `Lobby.handleAdoptEntity` / `handleFaithWarGambit` / `handleReclaimEntity`, `EntityMarket.CreateListing` / `PurchaseListing`, `PetBattleArena.ChallengePet` / `ResolveBattle`, `ReligionGovernance.BuyGovernorship` / `BuyoutReligion`, `TournamentService.DetermineTop5` / `ProcessTournamentResult`, `ContractEngine.HandleCompleteContract` (DEAD despite its doc claiming three callers), `AchievementService`/`AuctionService`/`LoanService.TransferBundleItems` and `Lobby.handleOrphanStatus`. The sweep's per-file findings for `entity_event_engine.go` and `handlers_public.go` are the SAME defects seen from the inside.
  8. **`entryHolds` TREATS A CYCLE AS PROOF OF NOTHING** (:236-238, `visiting[key] → false`) — correct, and worth recording because the tree HAS reachability cycles.
  9. **HONEST LIMITS, restated as defects-in-waiting:** the positional rule is a false NEGATIVE generator (a lock taken inside an `if` hides every later access — the safe direction); "one hop" means a helper reached through a LONGER chain stays UNFENCED even when every caller eventually holds the lock, so those entries owe a fix even if they are currently safe (the conservative default, per its own text: *"prove it is safe" is the work*); a map accessed through an ALIAS is INVISIBLE here (`l.marketNodes` IS `l.tokenSinkRouter.MarketNodes` — the aliased-map hazard Problems §35 fixed, which needed a THIRD gate because this one's subject is a **field of `Lobby`**); and the derivation reads `backend_types.go` by CONVENTION, so a second `Lobby`-shaped struct or a map added to another file's embedded struct would not be protected.
 10. **THE GATE'S SCOPE IS STATED BY EXCLUSION TOO:** it does not judge whether the locking present is CORRECT, and it does not own the recursive-lock class — that belongs to `selflock_gate_test.go` alone (:56-57). Two gates, two classes, one owner each.

### `market_service.go` — 614 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-614, read in windows 1-171, 171-361, 361-531, 531-614)
- **PURPOSE:** the **AMM / entity-share market** (Pillar 2) plus the entity dividend rail (Pillar 1) and two Justice interventions (Pillar 3): `getOrCreateMarketNodeLocked` + the Bancor node, the buy/sell bonding curve with its anti-whale slippage, `HandleClaimDividends` / `HandleHarvestAllDividends` / `HandleJusticeFreezeDividends`, `handleTradeShares`, the dynamic rumor fee and `observeGlobalSentiments`.
- **OWNS:** `ErrExcessiveSlippage` (`slippage limits exceeded due to low liquidity density`) :14-16 · **`getOrCreateMarketNodeLocked`** :19-64 · **`newEntityMarketNodeSeed`** — the ONE creation site (10 000 units issued, 50 000 $VBV reserve, `ReserveRatio 0.33`, pool/yield/freeze/reputation zeroed) :69-80 · `MarketNodeRef` :83-87 · **`(tsr *TokenSinkRouter) marketNodeRefs()`** — the sanctioned VALUE SNAPSHOT of the aliased map :89-115 · `HandleClaimDividends` :121 · `HandleJusticeFreezeDividends` :156 · `HandleHarvestAllDividends` :204 · **`(node *EntityMarketNode) GetSpotPrice()`** :253 · **`CalculateBuyCost`** :276 · **`CalculateSellReturn`** :308 · `CalculateDynamicRumorFee` :341 · **`handleTradeShares`** :365 · `observeGlobalSentiments` :592.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** three WS doors (`HandleClaimDividends`, `HandleJusticeFreezeDividends`, `HandleHarvestAllDividends`) and the `handleTradeShares` WS case; the four AMM methods are pure functions called by the trade door and by the invest door in `entity_investment_service.go`; `marketNodeRefs` is the read door every unfenced walker uses (theme-engine signature, hourly dividend daemon, `handleFaucetStatus`, `saveEconomyState`).
- **CALLS OUT:** `l.oracleService.ResolveEnvoiName` (:196, :381 — **resolved BEFORE the lock in the trade door**, which is the tree's own performance rule), `l.tokenSinkRouter.RouteEntityDividend` (:456) and `RouteCriminalTax` (:478, :519), `l.sendNoteTx(NotePrefixShareTrade+…)` on the ON-CHAIN audit rail for a trade above 100 $VBV (:483-486, :567-570), `l.applyDynamicScalingLocked` (repeatedly), `l.sendToClientLocked` / `l.getLobbyUpdateMsg` / `l.broadcast`, `l.logAdminAuditLocked`, and `math` (Pow/Ceil/Floor/Round).
- **CALLED BY:** `entity_investment_service.go` (the buy/sell cost functions and the node), `lobby_manager.go`'s portfolio value walk, the 15-minute snapshot worker, `theme_engine.go`, `handleFaucetStatus`.
- **STATE & LOCKS (this file contains the tree's CLOSEST-READ LOCK CONTRACTS):** `getOrCreateMarketNodeLocked`'s doc comment states the whole arrangement (:19-32) — **the caller MUST hold `l.mutex`, and the MAP is nevertheless owned by the ROUTER's mutex**; `Lobby.marketNodes` and `TokenSinkRouter.MarketNodes` are THE SAME map, *"so a spelling is not a lock"*; the lobby lock is **NOT sufficient** because the snapshot worker, the dividends-history route and the market-vitality signature all walk it holding `tsr.Mu` and NO lobby lock; the acquisition is taken AFTER the lobby lock — the order the tree already uses (`Lobby.mutex -> tsr.Mu`) — and no caller holds `tsr.Mu` on arrival, so it cannot self-lock; it is gated by `market_nodes_alias_gate_test.go`. The **no-router branch returns a SEED and stores nothing**, with the reason stated: publishing a lobby-only map from there *"would be a SECOND OWNER for one concept"* (:36-41). `marketNodeRefs` is deliberately a SNAPSHOT rather than a `walk(fn)` helper, because a closure would run INSIDE the router lock and the moment it took a node lock would create `tsr.Mu -> node.Mu` — the REVERSE of the `node.Mu -> tsr.Mu` order the investment doors use (:91-97); a nil receiver answers an empty snapshot.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **VERDICT:** owner (of the unfenced-map class) — derived subject, verdict-pinned baseline that fails on NEW/STALE/FLIPPED, a real-tree negative control, and its own limits written into it. It is the reason the six unfenced lobby-map doors were fixed and the reason they cannot come back silently, and it is the precedent the sweep is using to judge every other gate it reads.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THE AMM IS FLOAT THROUGHOUT, ON LEDGER MONEY.** `GetSpotPrice` (:253-273) returns `float64`, applies a PERCEPTUAL reputation multiplier (`1.0 + Reputation/1_000_000.0`), and floors at the literal `0.01` **described as "0.01 micro-VBV"** — a float price with a micro-unit label. `CalculateBuyCost` (:276-303) composes `math.Pow(1.0+supplyRatio, 1.0/ReserveRatio)`, a quadratic whale penalty `1 + supplyRatio²×5`, and returns `uint64(math.Ceil(finalCost))`; `CalculateSellReturn` (:308-337) mirrors it with `math.Floor` and a 90 % penalty clamp. `CalculateDynamicRumorFee` (:341-362) builds a 500 $VBV floor plus 2.5 % of a float market cap. **Every one of these is a float decision on money, and the Architecture Ledger prohibits floats for balances, fees and any economic accumulator** — the same class `entity_investment_service.go` and `economy_service.go` carry.
  2. **THE REFUSAL THAT NAMES ITSELF IS NEVER RETURNED BY THIS FILE.** `ErrExcessiveSlippage` is declared here and the two cost functions compute a `slippagePercent` for its sake, but **the trade door never compares it to any limit** (:276-303 returns it; `handleTradeShares` :447-499 stores it into the audit payload only). A client is therefore charged the whale penalty and never refused for it, while the error variable — the file's only refusal vocabulary — has no reader in it.
  3. **`HandleClaimDividends` SKIPS THE FREEZE IT PRETENDS TO HONOUR.** `node.IsDividendFrozen` is SET by `HandleJusticeFreezeDividends` (:180) and the notification claims dividends *"are now being diverted to the Faucet as Regulatory Fines"* — but **no reader of `IsDividendFrozen` exists on the claim path**: `HandleClaimDividends` (:121-153) and `HandleHarvestAllDividends` (:204-250) both read `CumulativeYieldPerShare` and pay, frozen or not. Measured: the freeze is a flag plus a broadcast, with no diversion and no payout change anywhere in this file.
  4. **THE UNCOLLECTED `payoutMicro` IS COMPUTED AND DISCARDED.** `node.DividendPoolMicro` is never debited by a claim — `l.playerBalances[wallet] += payoutMicro` (:146, :237) has **no counterparty in the file** (this is the same MINT the sweep recorded from `entity_investment_service.go`'s side, seen here from the payout side). `yieldDelta × shares / 1e12` is a SHARE of a cumulative figure, not a draw from a pool, so a claim can be paid repeatedly against the same pool.
  5. **THE YIELD UNIT IS A `1e12` FIXED-POINT SCALE WITH NO OWNER.** Both claim paths hard-code `1000000000000` (:142, :231) while the INVEST side feeds the pool as micro units — two scales for one quantity, with no named constant and no conversion function, which is exactly how a dividend can be off by six orders of magnitude and still look like a number.
  6. **THE FLOAT `Amount` IS THE TRADE'S SOURCE OF TRUTH.** The request body carries `Amount float64` (:370-373); it is snapped with `math.Round(amount*100)/100` (:387) and then converted `uint64(data.Amount*100 + 0.5)` (:434). So the client declares its own quantity as a float, the tree's "integer supremacy" comments apply to what happens AFTER the float, and the `+0.5` is a hand-rolled round-half-up applied to a value that was already rounded once.
  7. **RUMOR STRENGTH MULTIPLIES MONEY.** `rumorMultiplier` is accumulated as `rumorMultiplier *= rumor.Strength` over every live rumor for the target (:414-419) and then applied to BOTH the buy cost (:448) and the sell return (:509) — a float multiplier on a price, sourced from rumors, with no cap and no integer owner.
  8. **THE TRADE HOLDS `node.Mu` FOR ITS WHOLE ECONOMIC BODY** (:411-412) — the fee routing (`RouteCriminalTax`), the scaling recalculation, the audit write and the portfolio update all run inside the node's write lock. The tree's own rule stated at :91-97 is that the two locks are taken SEQUENTIALLY, and `RouteCriminalTax` taking `tsr.Mu` inside a `node.Mu` section is the order the investment doors rely on — so this is the documented order, applied to a much longer body than the investment doors use.
  9. **THE FAUCET CHECK IS A FLOAT COMPARISON AGAINST A MIRROR.** The sell path refuses when `l.faucetBalance < totalValueBase` (:530) — `totalValueBase` derived by dividing micro by `1000000.0` and compared against `faucetBalance`, the FLOAT mirror `economy_service.go` already carries beside `faucetBalanceMicro`. Two representations of the pool decide a payout.
 10. **`observeGlobalSentiments` AVERAGES FLOATS OVER THE WHOLE LEADERBOARD AND LOGS ONLY** (:592-614) — it takes the lobby WRITE lock to compute averages of playstyle floats, writes `l.globalSentiment`, and logs. No caller in this file; it is a daemon-shaped function with no record family.
- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS.**
 11. **THE PROTOCOL FEE IS ROUTED TWICE, and the DIVIDEND SEED IS CARVED OUT OF THE RESERVE.** On a buy, `dividendSeed = feeMicro × 0.15` is routed to the entity's pool (:456) **and** the FULL `feeMicro` is routed through `RouteCriminalTax` (:478) while the reserve receives `totalValueMicro - feeMicro` (:460) — so the seed is a bookkept split that never reduced the fee actually routed, and the layer the buy is charged is not the layer the curve is credited.
 12. **THE SELL PATH'S RESERVE REDUCTION IS GROSS WHILE THE PLAYER IS PAID NET** (`node.ReserveBalance -= totalValueMicro` :552 against `l.playerBalances[wallet] += netToPlayerMicro` :546, where `netToPlayerMicro = totalValueMicro - feeMicro`) — so the fee is removed from the reserve AND from the player AND routed to the sink, i.e. the fee is counted twice against the pool. This is a one-line contradiction with a comment claiming *"Gross reduction maintains curve integrity"*.
 13. **THE DIVIDEND AUTO-CLAIM INSIDE A TRADE PAYS AGAINST A POOL THAT IS NOT DRAWN DOWN** (:398-410) — the same `(yieldDelta × shares)/1e12` mint as §4, executed as a side effect of trading, with its own audit key (`DIVIDEND_AUTO_CLAIM`) and no counterparty.
 14. **`l.leaderboard[targetWallet].MarketFrozenUntil` IS READ THROUGH A MAP INDEX, then `ensurePlayerStatsMapsInitialized(targetWallet)` is called before it** (:391, :394) — the copy-back idiom is used everywhere else in the file (`tStats := l.leaderboard[...]` then write back), so the frozen check and the reputation refresh (:405-407) are the two places the VALUE map is read/indexed inline, and the reputation write-back does `tStats.Reputation = l.CalculateReputation(tStats)` while a `stats` copy of the trader is already held.
 15. **THE `sell` BRANCH CHARGES A FEE WITH THE `buy` MATRIX AND PAYS THE PLAYER FROM THE FAUCET MIRROR, never from the reserve** — recorded because the two branches are asymmetric in a way the comments do not mention: a buy moves value INTO the reserve, a sell moves it out of the FAUCET while also reducing the reserve.
 16. **TWO SERIALISATIONS OF THE SAME PORTFOLIO.** The on-chain `tradeDetails` payload writes `float64(totalValueMicro)/1000000.0` (a float, on the audit rail) and the client's `portfolio_update` divides `float64(v)/100.0` (:578-583) with the comment *"Convert uint64 micro-shares to float64 shares for client-side display"* — the unit label says micro-shares, the scale is 100, and the display conversion is `math.Round`-free.
 17. **`observeGlobalSentiments` DIVIDES BY A LEADERBOARD LENGTH TAKEN BEFORE THE LOOP** (:597-598) — `count := float64(len(l.leaderboard))` is captured, then the map is RANGED; the lock is held so no concurrent write can occur, but the count and the ranged set are separate reads of a map that is only stable BECAUSE the write lock is held — and the function's own averages (`AvgAggressiveness`, `AvgRiskTolerance`) are floats that are never converted at any boundary the sweep can see.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the AMM curve, the entity-share trade door and the dividend claim rail) — and the file that documents the tree's hard-won ALIASED-MAP contract precisely while carrying floats on every money decision in it. Its locks and its map ownership are the corrected shape; its economics are floats, its "slippage refusal" is computed and never enforced, its dividend freeze is a flag with no reader on the payout path, and its claims MINT against a cumulative figure rather than drawing from the pool they name.



### `rivalry_handlers.go` — 632 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-632, read in windows 1-176, 176-401, 330-471, 471-632)
- **PURPOSE:** the **HTTP/WS handlers for Career & Rivalry** (Pillars 12-13) plus the faction shop and the one JSON writer pair every handler family shares: the rivalry invitation/accept/decline/challenge lifecycle, `/api/faction/shop/{faction}` + `/api/faction/shop/buy`, `/api/career/progress`, `/api/rivalry/state`.
- **OWNS:** the three request payloads `rivalryRequestData` / `rivalryActionData` / `factionBuyData` :17-30 · `HandleRivalryRequest` :33 · `HandleRivalryAction` (accept / decline / challenge) :144 · **`HandleFactionShop`** :328 · **`HandleBuyFactionItem`** :357-517 · **`HandleGetCareerProgress`** :519-565 · `HandleGetRivalryState` :567-616 · **`writeJSON`** (the tree's shared JSON helper) :622 and **`writeJSONStatus`** :633.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** the registered routes — `POST /api/rivalry/request`, `POST /api/rivalry/{accept,decline,challenge}?action=`, `GET /api/faction/shop/{faction}`, `POST /api/faction/shop/buy`, `GET /api/career/progress`, `GET /api/rivalry/state`. Caller identity comes from `?wallet=` and then `X-Player-Wallet` in EVERY handler here.
- **CALLS OUT:** `l.playerService.GetHegemonyPath(stats.JobRole)` (:431 — the ONE reader of the Justice/Underworld alignment behind the faction gate), `l.clients[...].send` directly (bypassing `sendToClientLocked`) with `json.Marshal`, `careerProgressLocked`, `log.Printf`, `writeJSON`/`writeJSONStatus`.
- **CALLED BY:** `server_main.go`'s route table; the client's `rivalry_viewer.js` / `career_tree.js` / `faction_shop.js`.
- **STATE & LOCKS (the file records two FIXES in place, which is why it is worth reading):** `HandleGetCareerProgress`'s body carries BOTH comments (:534-536, :554-556) — *"The WRITE lock, because this door lazily initialises CareerXP and `l.leaderboard` holds VALUES: the record must be written back, and a map write under a read lock is a concurrent map write against every other reader (it used to run under `defer ...RUnlock()`)"* and *"The LOCK-HELD form: `GetCareerProgress` takes the lobby lock ITSELF, so calling it from here was a recursive read lock while the lock was already held."* It now takes `Lock()` (not a deferred one), builds with `careerProgressLocked(clientID, stats)`, and **releases BEFORE it encodes** (:561). The two rivalry doors and the buy door take the write lock for their whole body; `HandleFactionShop` and `HandleGetRivalryState` take `RLock`.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1


- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THE NAME-SLICING PANIC CLASS IS LIVE, SEVEN TIMES IN THIS ONE FILE.** `senderID[:8]+"..."` (:133, :137), `clientID[:8]` (:246, :262, :315), `clientID[:8]` in the faction-shop log (:509) — every one panics on a wallet shorter than 8 characters. The wallet arrives from `?wallet=` / `X-Player-Wallet` with **no length check anywhere in the file**, so the doors that are supposed to verify identity are the doors that crash on a short one. This is the same class the sweep recorded in `black_market_service.go` and `creator_store_service.go`.
  2. **"CANNOT RIVAL YOURSELF" IS CHECKED ON THE WRONG SPELLING.** `senderID == targetWallet` (:70) compares the REQUEST spelling against the body spelling, byte for byte, while every other lookup in the tree resolves wallets **case-insensitively** (`balanceKeyLocked`, `leaderboardKeyLocked`, `walletMatches`). Two spellings of one wallet pass the check, and `l.leaderboard[senderID]` (:77) then reads the map with the RAW request spelling.
  3. **THE INVITATION IS STORED WITH NO `ToWallet` AND READ WITH ONE.** `HandleRivalryRequest` appends `PendingRivalInvite{FromWallet, ID, Timestamp, Level}` (:101-106) — no `ToWallet` — while BOTH consumers match on `inv.FromWallet == rivalWallet && inv.ToWallet == clientID` (:206, :276). So **an invitation created by this door can never be accepted or declined**; the accept path can only find an invite written by some other producer.
  4. **THE SENDER'S LIST IS THE ONE APPENDED TO.** The pending invitation is appended to `senderStats.Rivalry.PendingInvitations` (:101) and written back to `l.leaderboard[senderID]` (:107) — i.e. **the INVITER holds the invitation meant for the target**, and the pending-notification is sent to the target separately (:110-128). Combined with §3, both halves are wrong in opposite directions.
  5. **THE DECLINE PATH ANSWERS `success:true` WHATEVER HAPPENED.** `found` is set (:281) and only LOGGED (:284); the response is an unconditional `{"success": true}` (:286) — so declining a non-existent invitation is reported as a success, and the `newInvites` slice is written back even when nothing matched.
  6. **THE CLIENT IS SENT A TRUNCATED WALLET AS THE "RIVAL WALLET".** The acceptance notification's payload text formats `clientID[:8]+"..."` and `rivalWallet[:8]+"..."` while its own `"rival_wallet"` field carries the full `rivalWallet` (:240-245) — the human-readable half is deliberately truncated and the machine-readable half is not, in the same payload.
  7. **THE FACTION SHOP'S PRICE TABLE IS A SECOND COPY OF THE CATALOGUE.** `HandleFactionShop` builds the eight `ItemDef`s with their `CostMicro` (:366-402) and `HandleBuyFactionItem` then **re-declares the same eight prices in a `switch`** (:464-487). The two are hand-maintained copies; a price changed in the catalogue and not in the switch (or vice versa) makes the shop display one number and charge another, with no test comparing them.
  8. **THE FACTION GATE VALIDATES THE PLAYER'S PATH, NOT THE ITEM'S FACTION.** `hegemonyPath` is derived from the player's `JobRole` (:429-437) and then `itemFaction` is assigned FROM that path (:440-445), so `itemFaction != faction` can only fail when the player's own path differs from the requested shop — the ITEM's declared `Category` (`JusticeCategory`/`UnderworldCategory`) is never consulted. A JUSTICE-path player therefore can buy `data_scramble` by asking for the JUSTICE shop.
  9. **THE EFFECT APPLICATION IS A STUB WITH A COMMENT THAT ADMITS IT, AND IT WRITES A NIL MAP.** *"(simplified - in production this would use the engine methods)"* (:499) — `stats.ActiveItemBuffs[itemName] = 5` (:502) writes a map that is **initialised nowhere in this file** (the balance/inventory are guarded, this is not), so a wallet without an `ActiveItemBuffs` map panics; and the hard-coded `5` contradicts the `DurationMin` the catalogue just served (5, 60, 10080, 1440 minutes). Note `DurationMin: 0` on `security_override`, which the stub would render as 5 minutes.
 10. **THE ITEM IS PURCHASED AND NOTHING CHARGES THE SINK.** `l.playerBalances[clientID] -= itemCost` (:494) is the whole money movement — there is **no `tokenSinkRouter` call, no faucet routing and no audit line** (`log.Printf` only, and it formats `clientID[:8]`). The Industrial Loop's "every fee reconciles" rule is not met by this door, and the price is removed from a liability with no destination.
 11. **`bounty_license` IS SOLD AND ITS RECURRING RULE IS IGNORED.** The catalogue declares `Recurring: true, RecurringDays: 7, CostMicro: 50 $VBV` (:384) and the buy path sets `stats.BountyLicenseActive = true` (:506) — nothing charges the weekly fee, tracks an expiry or deactivates it, so the "Recurring license" is a boolean.
 12. **`writeJSON` NEVER SETS A STATUS** (:622-629) — it writes a header and encodes, defaulting `success` to false when absent. **`writeJSONStatus` exists four lines below and is not used by this family at all**, so every refusal in this file answers **HTTP 200** with `{"success":false,...}`; a status-code client cannot distinguish a refusal from a success. This is the same finding the sweep recorded for `slide_theming.go`'s `writeJSON` family.

### `underworld_contracts.go` — 794 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-794, read in windows 1-151, 200-280, 280-471, 471-661, 661-794)
- **PURPOSE:** the **Dynamic Underworld Contract Template Engine** (Pillar 3) — the template registry, dynamic instantiation with difficulty scaling, eligibility evaluation, $VBV-gated XP/reward computation, assignment and completion for the Underworld Contracts system (:3-7).
- **OWNS:** `ContractTemplate` (:25-38 — including `RivalMultiplier float64`) · `DynamicContract` :41-50 · **`UnderworldContractTemplates`** — the static registry of ~19-28 templates (sabotage / heist / laundering / UnderworldBoss families) :57-225 · **`ContractEngine`** (`templates` + `instanceCounter`) :232-235 · `NewContractEngine` (seeds the counter with `rand.Intn(10000)`) :238 · **`GetAvailableContracts`** :246-320 · **`IsCompatibleRole`** :323 · **`GetCompatibleRoles`** :347 · **`EvaluateRivalPresence`** :374 · `IsJusticeCareer` :399 · **`ComputeScaledXP`** :408 · `GetContractXPForCareer` :428 · `contains` :444 · **`HandleGetAvailableContracts`** :474 · `GetCareerTierForPlayer` :523 · **`HandleAssignContract`** :547 · **`HandleCompleteContract`** :694.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `HandleGetAvailableContracts(lobby, wallet)` → `/api/contracts/list` · `HandleAssignContract(lobby, wallet, templateID)` → `/api/contracts/assign` · `HandleCompleteContract(lobby, wallet, templateID)` — documented as *"called from battle_service.go, club_service.go, handlers_criminality.go at resolution points"* (:693).
- **CALLS OUT:** `lobby.clubService.GetAllClubs(lobby)` (the territory-control probe, twice) · `stats.CareerXP.GetVBVGatingMultiplier()` / `ComputeScaledXP(1, "")` / `TrackCareerXP` · `lobby.playerBalances[wallet]` · `lobby.logAdminAuditLocked` · `lobby.broadcast` through `jsonListEnvelope` with a non-blocking `select`/`default` drop · `math/rand`, `strings`, `time`.
- **CALLED BY:** `handlers_criminality.go` (the two doors), `battle_service.go` and `club_service.go` at resolution points.
- **STATE & LOCKS (this file CARRIES THREE LOCK FIXES in its own comments, and they are the reason to read it):**
  1. `EvaluateRivalPresence` states the RANGE-over-a-concurrent-map hazard and the fix precisely: *"THE READ LOCK, because this walks the WHOLE `l.leaderboard` map and every caller of this function (the /api/contracts routes) holds no lock, while matchmaking, the career door and the economy tickers write it constantly... Only the job roles are copied out, so the locked window is one map walk and the judgement (`IsJusticeCareer`) runs outside it"* (:365-372). **This is why the helper's 197→199 self-lock registry movement was recorded — it is one of the two `*Lobby` lock sites the sweep's `Problems §31` note counted.**
  2. `HandleGetAvailableContracts` — *"READ UNDER THE LOCK: this is the /api/contracts/list path and it holds nothing... The record is a value, so the copy is safe to use once the lock is released"* (:479-482).
  3. `HandleAssignContract` — *"THE READ-MODIFY-WRITE IS ONE CRITICAL SECTION: `stats` was read at the top of this function, so writing that copy back outside the lock both RACES (a concurrent map write is a `fatal error`, not recoverable) and can DISCARD an update that landed in between. Re-read under the write lock so the field is set on fresh state"* (:669-673).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **VERDICT:** owner (of the rivalry HTTP/WS doors, the faction shop and the shared `writeJSON` pair) — a file that CARRIES TWO EARLIER FIXES in its own comments (the career door's write lock and its `...Locked` delegation, both correctly explained in place) and that, around them, still: **stores an invitation the accept path cannot match** (`ToWallet` never set, matched by both consumers); appends the invitation to the INVITER's list; answers `success:true` for a decline that found nothing; holds **seven name-slicing panic sites** with no length check; keeps a **second hand-maintained copy of the faction price table**; validates the player's path instead of the item's faction; writes a possibly-nil `ActiveItemBuffs` map; charges the purchase with **no sink routing or audit**; ignores its own recurring-license rule; and answers **HTTP 200 for every refusal** while the status-capable helper sits unused four lines below.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **`HandleCompleteContract` RUNS WITH NO LOCK AND WRITES THE LEADERBOARD MAP.** It reads `stats, exists := lobby.leaderboard[wallet]` (:699 — unfenced), mutates `stats.ActiveUnderworldContractID = ""`, and writes `lobby.leaderboard[wallet] = stats` (:766) — **and then awards XP through `stats.CareerXP.TrackCareerXP`** (:761). Unlike its two siblings in the SAME FILE, both of which carry the read/write-lock comments, this door has no lock at all: it is a documented resolution point reachable from three services, it writes a VALUES map, and (per the map gate) it is baselined **UNFENCED** as `ContractEngine.HandleCompleteContract`, whose own note says it is *"DEAD despite its doc claiming three callers"*.
  2. **THE RIVAL BRANCH COMPUTES `rivalPresent` TWICE AND RE-RANGES THE MAP WITH NO LOCK.** It calls `ce.EvaluateRivalPresence(lobby, wallet)` (the LOCKED helper) and then, INSIDE the `if`, immediately re-walks `lobby.leaderboard` by hand (:742-749) — **`if ce.EvaluateRivalPresence(...)` is a range over the leaderboard with no lock at all**, defeating the entire purpose of the helper written three lines' worth of comment to make safe. The comment even says *"Already computed in HandleGetAvailableContracts — but recompute for safety"*, which describes a cost, not a reason.
  3. **THE REWARD IS MINTED WITH NO COUNTERPARTY AND THE COMMENT CLAIMS THE OPPOSITE.** *"Award reward micro-units (deterministic — no silent minting)"* (:731) and *"Route reward through authoritative balance map (deterministic finance — no silent minting)"* (:752) — the body is `lobby.playerBalances[wallet] += rewardMicro` (:753): **no debit, no sink, no fee, no route**. A `BaseRewardMicro` up to **200 000 $VBV** (CONTRACT-033) appears in the player's ledger from nothing.
  4. **THE CONTRACT'S OWN TIME LIMIT IS NEVER ENFORCED.** `ExpiresAt` is computed at assignment (:662) and `TimeLimitHours` is per-template (4-48 h), but **nothing reads `ExpiresAt`** in this file: no expiry sweep, no refusal of an expired assignment, and `ActiveUnderworldContractID` is a bare ID string with no deadline stored beside it. The "one-contract-at-a-time" rule (:556) is therefore permanent once assigned — a player who takes CONTRACT-001 and never completes it can never take another.
  5. **THE ASSIGNMENT STORES THE TEMPLATE ID IN A FIELD NAMED FOR AN INSTANCE.** `fresh.ActiveUnderworldContractID = templateID` (:675) — while the door generates an `InstanceID` and returns it — so the "instance" is decorative, the Instanced-nonce idea is unused, and two players' CONTRACT-001 are indistinguishable in state.
  6. **THE SAME REWARD IS COMPUTED BY THREE SLIGHTLY DIFFERENT EXPRESSIONS.** `GetAvailableContracts` scales inside the loop (:290-301); `HandleAssignContract` re-derives it (:649-657); `HandleCompleteContract` derives it a THIRD time (:734-738). All three use `float64(tmpl.BaseRewardMicro) * vbvMultiplier` plus an identical wanted-bonus formula — three copies of one economics rule, and the PLAYER'S PAYOUT is the third copy, not the one the UI was shown.
  7. **EVERY SCALING DECISION IS A FLOAT ON MONEY AND XP.** `uint64(float64(...) * vbvMultiplier)` (:290, :649, :734), the wanted bonus `float64(delta)/20.0` with a `/20.0` comment claiming *"+50% at max wanted"* (which the arithmetic cannot produce — it is unbounded by design and capped by nothing), `RivalMultiplier` applied as a float multiply (:299, :656, :748), and `ComputeScaledXP`'s tier bonus `float64(1+(careerTier-1)) * 0.1` capped at 1.5 (:413-419) — the cap makes **every tier from 6 upward identical**, and the expression is self-admittedly *"(explicit float64 arithmetic)"*.
  8. **`vbvMultiplier` IS DERIVED BY CALLING A SCALER WITH DUMMY ARGUMENTS.** `float64(stats.CareerXP.ComputeScaledXP(1, ""))` (:485) with the comment *"Normalize: GetVBVGatingMultiplier pattern"* — the gate multiplier is obtained by SCALING THE NUMBER 1 and treating the result as the multiplier. Elsewhere the same file calls `GetVBVGatingMultiplier()` directly (:531, :725), so the two doors can disagree about the same player's multiplier.
  9. **`GetCareerTierForPlayer` MAPS THE FLOAT MULTIPLIER TO A TIER WITH HARD-CODED FLOAT THRESHOLDS** (:523-545): `>=32 → 6`, `>=16 → 5`, `>=4 → 4`, `>=2 → 3`, `default → 2` — note the **default is 2, never 1**, and the ladder skips a rung between 4 and 16, so thresholds are not the permille ladder the integer owner `GetVBVGatingPermille` defines. A player with no extra $VBV is tier 2 and can accept `RequiredCareerTier: 2` contracts.
 10. **`IsJusticeCareer` IS A HAND-WRITTEN NAME LIST AND ITS OWN COMMENT ADMITS IT DELEGATES NOTHING.** `{"Warden", "Commissioner", "Enforcer", "Mediator", "Judge", "Justice Recruiter"}` with `return false // Delegate to IsJusticeAligned() if available via career engine interface` (:399-406) — a second, drifting vocabulary beside `RoleKey`/`IsJusticeAligned` (session (g) built `RoleKey` precisely because `"IntelAgent"` vs `"Int.Agent"` silently matched nothing), so a Justice-aligned career spelled in the identifier form is not "justice" here — and the `RivalMultiplier` (up to **5.0×**) hangs off that answer.
 11. **`NewContractEngine` SEEDS ITS COUNTER WITH `rand.Intn(10000)`** (:241) — a RANDOM instance-id seed, in a repository whose determinism rule is explicit, and the counter is never persisted, so a restart re-seeds the namespace.
 12. **`contains` FOLDS CASE WHILE ITS CALLERS COMPARE WITH `==`.** `GetCompatibleRoles` uses `contains` (case-insensitive, :444-451) while `IsCompatibleRole` and `GetContractXPForCareer` use `==` (:324-343, :431) — so the same two careers compare equal in one function and unequal in another, which is how a role spelled `"Smuggler"` and `"smuggler"` can be eligible for a contract and ineligible for its XP.

### `placeholder_derivatives.go` — 830 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-830, read in windows 1-151, 151-401, 245-296, 295-401, 400-641, 640-830)
- **PURPOSE:** §10.6/§10.7 — **the server derives LIGHT renditions of the placeholder pack**. The shipped pack is 117 PNGs / ~314 MB (avg 2.7 MB, 14 frames above 4 MB, one at 8.3 MB), which is fine as ORIGINALS and useless as a background. Its header states why the fix MUST be server-side: a client-side downscale *"would still have to download the 2.7 MB first, so it would fix nothing"*, and a hand-made "small" file *"would be art nobody generated"* (:5-15).
- **OWNS:** the two tiers `PlaceholderDerivativeSlideTier` / `ThumbTier` and their widths **512 / 128** :47-58 · `placeholderDerivativeRoot` (`Public/Assets/Generated/NPC-helpers`) :61 · `placeholderDerivativeURIPrefix` · the manifest file/version constants :69-76 · `PlaceholderDerivative` (per-tier measured facts) :79 · `PlaceholderDerivativeRecord` (source provenance + both tiers + `Failed`) :92 · `PlaceholderDerivativeManifest` :105 · **`PlaceholderDerivativeStatus`** (the SERVED census, with the deliberate ABSENCE of a `reused` field documented) :115-135 · the four package-level mutexes/globals :138-156 · `loadDerivativeManifest` :162 · **`saveDerivativeManifest`** (atomic temp+rename) :199 · **`PlaceholderDerivativeStatusForClient`** :228 · `PlaceholderDerivativeRecordFor` / `derivativeRecordForTarget` / `derivativeStoreRecord` :274-323 · `PlaceholderDerivativePassResult` :329 · **`placeholderDerivativePriority`** (taken FROM the slide declaration so it cannot drift) :337 · `orderSkusForDerivation` :360 · **`derivativeFilesPresent`** (*"the manifest is an index, not a promise, so the FILE is what decides"*) :384 · **`ensureDerivativeForSKU`** :402 · **`runPlaceholderDerivativePass`** (the ONLY writer) :445 · `EnsurePlaceholderDerivatives` :489 · `StartPlaceholderDerivativeWarmup` :504 · `PlaceholderSkuView` / `PlaceholderCatalogueViews` :520-560 · `PlaceholderDerivativeURIForSKU` :566 · **`integerBoxDownscale`** — the integer alpha-weighted box filter :599-670 · `servedURIPathFor` :675 · `placeholderDerivativeTarget` + its production constructor :686-717 · `pathForURI` / `outPathFor` / `uriFor` :720-740 · **`derivePNGToWidth`** :744 · `hashFileSHA256` / `fileSize` :803-829.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** `EnsurePlaceholderDerivatives()` (the synchronous pass), `StartPlaceholderDerivativeWarmup()` (the background pass, `sync.Once`), `PlaceholderDerivativeStatusForClient()`, `PlaceholderDerivativeRecordFor(sku)`, `PlaceholderCatalogueViews()`, `PlaceholderDerivativeURIForSKU(sku, tier)` — consumed by `placeholder_assets.go`, `slide_theming.go` and `ui_tree_theming.go`.

- **STATE & LOCKS:** `placeholderDerivManifestMu` guards the **map of manifests + the pass flags**, and is *"held only for short map/field updates, never across a decode, so a reader never waits on the pass"* (:139-141); the index is **keyed by GENERATED ROOT** so a test can run a pass without disturbing the shipped pack; `placeholderDerivPassMu` serialises whole passes AND single-frame derivations, *"so two callers can never encode the same frame into the same path at once"* (:151-153). Every read returns a **COPY** (`cp := *rec`) so a concurrent pass cannot mutate what another goroutine is rendering (:308-310).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **VERDICT:** owner (of the Underworld contract templates, the dynamic instantiator and the assign/complete doors) — a file whose TWO list/assign doors carry the tree's corrected **READ-VALIDATE → RELEASE → COMPUTE** and **ONE-CRITICAL-SECTION** shapes (with `EvaluateRivalPresence` extracting its walk for exactly that reason) while its THIRD door, `HandleCompleteContract`, runs the same map with **no lock at all and then re-ranges it by hand inside the branch the helper was written to make safe**; that MINTS the payout under two comments claiming it does not; that computes one economics rule three times over floats; that never enforces `ExpiresAt`; that hides a second career vocabulary behind the rival multiplier; and that seeds its instance counter from `rand`.



- **OBSERVED DEFECTS / INSTRUMENT LIMITS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).** With `placeholder_assets.go`, this is the strongest REVIEWABILITY file the sweep has read; the findings are edge cases and each is stated with its size.
  1. **`integerBoxDownscale`'s PREMULTIPLIED NARROWING IS WRONG FOR A TRANSLUCENT PIXEL.** The filter accumulates `sumC = Σ(c·a)`, divides by `sumA` — i.e. it **un-premultiplies** — and then stores the result as if it were premultiplied, with the comment *"image.RGBA is premultiplied: the accumulated values already are, so they are narrowed from 16-bit to 8-bit and stored directly"* (:664-666). After `outC = sumC/sumA` the value IS the straight colour, so a **partially transparent** pixel is written at full colour intensity instead of scaled by coverage — a bright halo on semi-transparent art, precisely the artefact the file's own header says alpha weighting exists to prevent (:16-18). The accumulation polarity must match the destination: store `sumC/n` (premultiplied) or convert `outC·outA` back down.
  2. **THE FILTER'S BOX BOUNDS COME FROM THE SCALED IMAGE, NOT THE SOURCE.** `dh := sh*dw/sw` then `y0 := y*sh/dh` (:614-641) — `dh` truncation leaves the last source row(s) uncovered when `sh` is not a multiple of `dw`: a deterministic edge crop, unrecorded.
  3. **`derivativeFilesPresent` PROMISES MORE THAN IT CHECKS.** Its doc says *"the FILE is what decides"* (:380-383) while the body returns `true` for a NIL derivative (:386-388), so a record with both tiers nil passes. The reuse gate's `prev.Failed == ""` (:409) is what saves the current code — a latent hole, recorded because contract and body differ.
  4. **REUSE RE-HASHES THE SOURCE BUT NOT THE DERIVED FILE.** Reuse requires the source hash to match and the files to exist (:406-410) — but the derived file is only `Stat`ed for size > 0, so a truncated or hand-edited derivative of the right size is served as current and the recorded `HashHex` (which the payload invites a client to verify) would not match.
  5. **A HALF-FAILED RECORD IS NEVER COUNTED AND NEVER READY.** One tier failing sets `rec.Failed` while the OTHER tier is stored (:433-440); `derivativeRecordForTarget` answers `false` only when BOTH are nil (:305-307); and the census's failure case requires BOTH nil (:269-272). So such a frame is counted **Pending for ever** — it never becomes Failed and can never make `Ready` true.


### `server.go` — 868 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-868, read in windows 1-161, 161-401, 286-340, 400-641, 512-576, 640-868, 732-790)
- **PURPOSE:** the server's **composition root and boot sequence** — `newLobby()` builds every map, service, daemon, registry and client that the process owns; plus the CORS/upgrade middleware, the network-config loader, the liquidity-sampling daemon, the redacted registry writer, the WebSocket handshake, the Justice and Underworld Lobby **wrapper** methods and the shared wallet extractor.

- **ENTRY POINTS:** `newLobby()` is called once by `main()`; the wrappers are the route table's targets; `CollectLiquiditySamples` is the 24 h daemon's tick.
- **CALLS OUT:** `NewTokenSinkRouter`, `NewBootstrapEngine(...).BootstrapAuthoritativeState()`, `NewPersistenceSyncWorker(...).StartSyncDaemon`, `NewTelemetryLogger("9090")`, `NewGracePeriodMatrix`, `NewRateLimiterService`, `NewPayoutScheduler`, `NewLoadBalancedClient` + `NewMultiChainRouter` (the two-branch chain wiring), `NewEntityInvestmentService`, `NewIdentityBridge`, `NewContractEngine`, `NewTenantVaultRegistry`, `NewBondedAssetRegistry`, `NewLocalModelPromotionRegistry`, `NewEntityEventEngine`, `StartPlaceholderDerivativeWarmup`, the faith globals (`religionGov`, `faithChurchEngine`, `SetGlobalLobbyRef`), and eleven `load*` readers.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `newLobby` builds the struct literal with **23 maps**, then performs the **aliased-map bind under the map's OWNER mutex** — *"Taken under the map's ONE owner mutex (the router's): the lobby field is a second NAME for the same map, and the alias gate (`market_nodes_alias_gate_test.go`) requires every access to it to hold that mutex"* (:176-181). `threadEnvSecrets` and `saveNetworkConfigs` take the lobby lock; `loadNetworkConfigs`' decode takes `Lock()` for the unmarshal only, and its failure is **reported loudly** because *"a malformed file must NOT silently answer with an empty registry (which would look exactly like 'no networks are configured')"* (:529-532). `CollectLiquiditySamples` takes the WRITE lock for its whole body — it is the write site that made the career gate work.

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / LIMITS.**
  6. **`Res.Derived` COUNTS RENDITIONS WHILE `Res.Total` COUNTS FRAMES.** `res.Total = len(skus)` (:453) but `res.Derived += written` where `written` is 1 or 2 (:470) — a full pass over 117 frames reports up to **234 against 117**. The SERVED census is correct and independent, so this is confined to the pass result and its log line, i.e. the artefact an operator reads to decide whether the pass worked.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THE BOOT-INITIALIZED `justiceHandlers` FIELD IS WRITTEN TWICE AND READ ZERO TIMES, AND EVERY REQUEST BUILDS A FRESH ONE THAT OMITS BOTH INJECTIONS.** Measured by grep across every `*.go`: (a) `newLobby` constructs it WITH the dependency — `justiceHandlers: &JusticeHandlers{service: nil, playerSvc: &PlayerService{}}` (:131) — and then injects the service, `l.justiceHandlers.service = l.justiceService` (:277); (b) **all five wrappers** instead do `h := &JusticeHandlers{lobby: l}` (:770, :775, :780, :785, :790), i.e. `service` **nil** and `playerSvc` **nil**; (c) inside `justice_handlers.go` **`h.service` and `h.playerSvc` have ZERO occurrences** (the file reaches the real service through `h.lobby.justiceService`, eight sites). So the two injected fields are dead, the boot field is dead, and the wrappers allocate a struct per request to run it with both fields empty. It does not panic only because nothing reads them — the fields exist to be read.
  2. **THE JUSTICE WRAPPERS BYPASS THE `JusticeService` INSTANCE THE LOBBY HOLDS.** They allocate a handler and let it resolve `h.lobby.justiceService`, while `newLobby`'s `service` injection `l.justiceHandlers.service = l.justiceService` (:277) targets the discarded struct — a second, defeated wiring path for the same dependency.
  3. **THE WEBSOCKET HANDSHAKE WRITES THE IDENTITY DIRECTLY TO THE CLIENT CHANNEL WITH NO LOCK AND NO NON-BLOCKING GUARD** (:747-755): `lobby.register <- client` then, OUTSIDE the registration, `client.send <- msg` — a hand-built `json.RawMessage` with `fmt.Sprintf` and `%s` for four values including `vaultAddress`, and the send blocks if the client's 256-slot buffer is full. Every other broadcast in the tree goes through `broadcast`/`sendToClientLocked`; this one bypasses both, and the hub's own deadlock history (unbuffered channels, session (b)) is why they are buffered.
  4. **`mained` SCAFFOLD IS SERVED TO THE CLIENT IN THE HANDSHAKE, INCLUDING A PROJECT ID.** The identity payload carries `vault`, `vbv`, `avoi` and `wc_project_id` (:750-753) — read straight from env-derived Lobby fields. A WalletConnect project id is a public identifier by design, so this is not a leak, but it is the ONE place the tree publishes an env-sourced value that the network-registry projection redacts by rule — recorded so the two policies are not read as one.
  5. **`corsMiddleware` DEGRADES TO WILDCARD ON AN EMPTY ENV** (:29): `allowAll := allowedOrigins == "" || ... == "*"`, so an operator who never sets `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` gets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on every route including the wallet-and-balance ones. `upgrader.CheckOrigin` has the same shape (`return true // Permissive in dev or if explicitly wildcarded`, :62). Two places implement the same policy, and the WebSocket one at least LOGS its refusal (:75) while the HTTP one silently sets no header.
  6. **THE EIGHT BUILT-IN CHAIN DEFAULTS ARE A SECOND REGISTRY.** `loadNetworkConfigs`' missing-file branch hard-codes Voi/Algorand/Ethereum/Solana/Polygon/Bitcoin/Flow/WAX with their PowerDivisors and PowerBases (:479-540) and then **immediately persists them** (`l.saveNetworkConfigs()`). This is the branch the (o) session traced when the empty `asset_id` panic lived here — and it means a deleted `networks.json` does not fail, it RESEEDS the registry, with defaults that can differ from the tracked file.
  7. **`PowerDivisor` IS WRITTEN AS A FLOAT LITERAL IN FOUR CHAINS** (`1e9`, `1e18`, `1e8` ×2, :497, :521, :537, :551) against `PowerDivisor: 1000000` and `1` elsewhere — the field's type is a float, so a power divisor is a float in a tree whose Ledger forbids floats on economic values; and `1e18` cannot be represented exactly in `float64`, so Polygon's divisor is an approximation of a number.
  8. **`CollectLiquiditySamples` IS THE FILE'S STRONGEST FIX AND STILL CARRIES ITS HISTORY.** It reads `l.playerBalances[l.balanceKeyLocked(wallet)]` with the whole defect chain documented in place (:640-651: `VBVBalance` assigned nowhere, every sample 0, the career gate unmet for ever, plus the float multiply) and then runs the lifecycle — `EvaluateCareerStandingLocked` per player + `NotifyCareerStandingLocked`, and `CareerUnlockNoticesLocked` + `NotifyCareerUnlockLocked` — with the ALWAYS-TRUE broadcast guard and the never-demoted defect both named as closed (:670-681). Honest limit recorded in the same comment: the window is **14 samples at 24 h = 2 weeks**, and `AvgSustainedMicro` is a running mean over that window with no timestamps, so a gap in the daemon's uptime silently broadens the window.
  9. **`saveNetworkConfigs` TAKES AN `RLock` WHILE WRITING THE FILE.** It reads the map under the read lock, builds the redacted projection, and then writes to disk — the write is outside the lock by design (correct, it is a file) — but the function's name says "save" while its lock says "read", so a caller scanning for the write path sees an unlocked-looking writer. The redaction itself is correct and single-owned (:683-690).
 10. **`handleGetAvailableContracts` / `handleAssignContract` USE `http.Error` FOR DOMAIN REFUSALS** (:834, :847 — `err.Error()` as the body, `http.StatusConflict`) while every other door in the family answers JSON; and the LIST door sets its own `Content-Type` and encodes without `writeJSON`, so this one file carries **three** response conventions (JSON envelope, `http.Error` text, and the wrapper family's own) plus `writeJSON`/`writeJSONStatus` from `rivalry_handlers.go`.

### `seasonal_event_engine.go` — 882 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-882, read in windows 1-171, 171-401, 400-661, 540-580, 660-882)
- **PURPOSE:** the **Industrial Loop's event layer** (Pillar 7-B) plus §26's Treasure Hunt and user-authored events and §25.3's region aggregation — create/activate/register/resolve/distribute seasonal events with a treasury pool and a multiplier, treasure caches, authored events with entry fees and royalties, and `GetRegionViews` (the `/api/regions` payload the 3D explorer and menu warp read).

- **ENTRY POINTS:** the HTTP handlers (both conventions — Lobby-receiver `handle*SeasonEventHTTP` and engine-receiver `Handle*SeasonEvent`), the WS/engine methods, and `GetRegionViews` for the region route.
- **CALLS OUT:** `l.ComputeThemeVector(wallet)` per participant (:145 — the §27.5 OutcomeBias hook) · `l.getClientIDFromWalletLocked` + `l.sendToClientLocked` · `l.broadcast <- jsonListEnvelope(...)` directly · `l.logAdminAuditLocked` · `l.tokenSinkRouter.RouteCriminalTax` · `l.aiEngine.GetAllCitizens` / `GetCitizen` · `l.entityEvents.BuildRegionPowerOverlay` · `math/rand`, `uuid`.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** the engine's own `seasonEngine.Mu` for every event/pool/cache mutation, `RLock` for the readers. `ResolveEvent`'s comment states the intentional crossing: *"the held lock is seasonEngine.Mu; ComputeThemeVector uses the independent l.mutex, so this is safe"* (:142-144). `ClaimTreasureCache` and `EnterUserEvent` both take `l.mutex` for their money movement, `GetRegionViews` takes `l.mutex.RLock()` for the pets/vehicles walk.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **THE BOOT SEQUENCE, AS MEASURED (the order is the file's real engineering content).** `newLobby` runs, in order: ~23 maps + 24 service literals assembled in one struct literal → reward config resolved by `resolveEnvBaseRewardMicro()` THEN `reconcileRewardRegistryLocked()` → `NewTokenSinkRouter` → **the aliased market-node bind under the router lock** → the siphon notifier wired to `broadcastToAdmins` → `NewPayoutScheduler(24h)` → `BootstrapAuthoritativeState()` (disk recovery) → the 15-minute persistence worker STARTED → telemetry on :9090 → the audit kernel wired to telemetry → the grace-period matrix → the rate limiter + its cleanup goroutine → the 24 h liquidity daemon STARTED → registries loaded/constructed (bonded assets, tenant vaults, local models, entity events, theme engine) → **`StartPlaceholderDerivativeWarmup()` (background, deliberately unawaited)** → faith religions seeded or loaded → `SetGlobalLobbyRef(l)` → the eleven `load*` readers (networks, txids, linked wallets, leaderboard, economy) → `LoadOnboardedWalletsFromIndexer` (goroutine) → the two-branch chain wiring keyed on `DEFAULT_NETWORK` (default `VOI`) → **the Voi app id applied AFTER the router exists, with the nil dereference it caused documented in place** (:405-425) → registrations from indexer (goroutine) → watchdog engine → payout engine → salary dispenser (goroutine) → persistent card cache → the genesis-reserve log **guarded by `!diskRecovered && !chainRecovered`** → bootstrap metrics. The comment for that guard states the reason: *"This prevents double-counting reserves that are already accounted for in recovered audit counters"* (:451-454).
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THE EVENT REWARD IS NEVER PAID, BUT THE PLAYER IS TOLD IT WAS.** `DistributeEventRewards` (:156-195) computes `rewardPerParticipant = pool.TotalAllocated / len(participants)`, then writes ONLY pool counters (`pool.PerParticipant[wallet] += …`, `pool.Distributed += …`, `totalDistributed += …`) and **sends a real notification**: `seasonal_event_reward` carrying `reward_micro` (:187). A grep of the whole file confirms **no `playerBalances` line exists in that function** — no credit, no debit, no sink call. The event "distributes treasury rewards" and nothing moves but a counter and a message.
  2. **`HandleClaimSeasonReward` REPORTS A CLAIM, PAYS NOTHING, AND CAN BE CALLED FOR EVER.** It recomputes the same per-participant share, then answers `{"status":"reward_claimed","reward_micro":reward}` and logs *"Player claimed seasonal event reward ... amount=%d micro-VBV"* (:460-518) — with **no balance credit, no pool debit and no record that the claim happened**, so each call returns the full share again and again; it also `break`s after the FIRST eligible event, so the multi-event case is never served in one request.
  3. **`ClaimTreasureCache` WRITES A LOBBY-OWNED MAP UNDER THE ENGINE'S LOCK ONLY.** The **bond debit** reads and writes `l.playerBalances[strings.ToLower(claimerWallet)]` (`:710`, `:715`) and calls `tokenSinkRouter.RouteCriminalTax` (`:717`) **while holding `seasonEngine.Mu` and no lobby lock at all** — the concurrent-map-write class. The file itself shows the correct shape 15 lines later: the payout takes `l.mutex` for the very same map (`:725-727`). Measured by grep: six `playerBalances` sites in the file, three of them under `l.mutex`, and the bond block is not one of them.
  4. **THE POOL-UPDATE BROADCAST CAN ONLY FIRE FOR A CLIENT REGISTERED UNDER AN EMPTY WALLET.** `HandleAdminUpdateRewardPool` sends via `lobby.getClientIDFromWalletLocked("")` (:671), and that helper is `for id, w := range l.wallets { if strings.EqualFold(w, wallet) { return id } }` — so an empty argument matches only an empty stored wallet. The update is broadcast to nobody, silently, and the branch reports nothing.
  5. **FOUR PAIRS OF DUPLICATE DOORS.** `handleCreateSeasonEventHTTP` :295 and `HandleAdminCreateEvent` :547 · `handleRegisterForSeasonEventHTTP` :367 and `HandleJoinSeasonEvent` :435 · `handleGetSeasonEventsHTTP` :388 and `HandleListSeasonEvents` :423 · `handleResolveSeasonEventHTTP` :393 and `HandleAdminEndEvent` :617. The engine-receiver variants take `*Lobby` as a PARAMETER and duplicate the decode + validate + encode body nearly verbatim (and the second pair omits the `Content-Type` on the success path that the first sets), so one route's refusal can differ from its twin's without either changing.
  6. **EVERY EVENT ECONOMIC VALUE IS A FLOAT OR A RANDOM.** `CreateSeasonEvent` validates `multiplier` as a float in `(0, 5.0]` and stores it as `Multiplier float64` on the event (:41-44, :59); `GetEventMultiplier` returns `1.0` for "no event" and the float otherwise (:112-121); the HTTP doors decode `DurationHours float64` and convert with `time.Duration(req.DurationHours * float64(time.Hour))` (:307-309, :562-564, :407-409, :511-513, :611-613 — the conversion is written out in FIVE places); and `GenerateRandomSeasonalEvent` draws its type, title, duration **and its 1.0-3.0 multiplier and its budget** from `math/rand` (:273-293), i.e. an event's reward multiplier is not reproducible.
  7. **`l.broadcast <- …` IS A BLOCKING SEND MADE WHILE HOLDING `seasonEngine.Mu`.** `ActivateEvent` (:93), `AutoTriggerEvents` (:209), `HandleJoinSeasonEvent` (:453) and `HandleAdminCreateEvent` (:573) all send straight onto the channel with no `select`/`default` guard — unlike `underworld_contracts.go`, which drops the event when the buffer is full and says why. A slow hub therefore stalls the seasonal engine **while the engine lock is held**, and the ordering `seasonEngine.Mu -> l.mutex` is already used by `GetRegionViews` and `ResolveEvent`.
  8. **`ResolveEvent` HOLDS THE ENGINE LOCK ACROSS A PER-PARTICIPANT LOOP** calling `l.ComputeThemeVector(wallet)` (:143-147), discarding every result (`_ =`). The comment defends the lock pair but not the loop: the work is a per-player lock acquisition inside a held engine lock, and its output is thrown away, so the section exists to warm a cache nothing reads there.
  9. **A GOROUTINE PER SCHEDULED EVENT, SLEEPING FOR THE WHOLE DELAY PLUS 5 MINUTES** — `ScheduleSeasonalEvent` (:248-270) claims an event then sleeps again for the announcement phase; the goroutine cannot be cancelled, and the event is created only AFTER the delay, so the "scheduled" event does not exist (and is not listed anywhere) until its own goroutine wakes.
 10. **THE TREASURE REWARD IS MINTED FROM A FIELD THE CALLER SET.** `ClaimTreasureCache` pays `c.RewardMicro` (:724) — a value passed into `SpawnTreasureCache` by its caller (:695-707) — and then **also routes the same `payout` into the sink** (`TREASURE_REWARD`, FaucetShare 1.0, :729): the credit and the sink routing are issued for the same amount, with no pool or escrow behind either, so the reward both mints to the claimer and books to the faucet. (The bond is routed the same way, `TREASURE_BOND`, while the bond itself was taken from the player — that pair at least balances.)
 11. **`EnterUserEvent`'s ROYALTY IS CORRECT AND ITS ENTRY IS NOT.** The entry is debited from the participant under `l.mutex` (:781) and the royalty credited to the creator (:785) — but the FULL `entry` is ALSO routed to the sink at FaucetShare 1.0 (:789), so the amount is credited to the creator and given to the faucet, i.e. the entry is spent twice from the system's point of view. The audit line repeats the arithmetic inline as `(entry*e.RoyaltyBps)/10000` (:793) — a second copy of the split computed outside the lock that performed it.

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / LIMITS.**
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THREE ADMIN ITEMS CHARGE THE PLAYER A SECOND TIME — AND THE FILE STATES THE OPPOSITE RULE ONE BRANCH LATER.** `illicit_commission_permit` (:686-690), `regulatory_bypass_permit` (:723-727) and `pro_social_commission_license` (:761-765) each route `uint64(item.Price*1000000)` to the sink as `ILLICIT_LICENSE_FEE` / `REGULATORY_BYPASS_FEE` / `PRO_SOCIAL_LICENSE_FEE` — while the item was **already bought** (the shop is the only way to hold one), and **`item.Price` is a FLOAT multiplied by 1 000 000** in the same expression. The `security_override` branch's comment states the opposite for the same class of item: *"Override fees are already captured via item purchase"* (:648-650). Two rules for whether an item's effect charges again, decided per branch by hand.
  2. **FIVE MISSION/CONTRACT PAYOUTS MINT FROM A CONSTANT WITH NO DEBIT.** `playerBalances[wallet] += rewardMicro` at :597 (MISSION-008, 3 000 $VBV), :611 (MISSION-007, 2 500), :625 (MISSION-005, 2 000), :914 (CONTRACT-002, 750), :928 (MISSION-003, 800) — each followed by `applyDynamicScalingLocked()` and a "$VBV" notification, with **no pool, escrow, sink or counterparty anywhere**. The mission is "cleared" by writing `ActiveJusticeMissionID = ""` on the stats copy.
  3. **MISSION-005 HAS NO TARGET CONDITION AT ALL AND THE SELF-AUDIT GUARD DOES NOT COVER IT.** MISSION-008 requires a governor-owned club AND the owner's `FavoriteCardID` to match; MISSION-007 requires the owner's `WantedLevel >= 15`; **MISSION-005 requires only that the audit reached an F grade** (:623-632) — and the "Self-Audit Loophole Prevention" check above names only MISSION-007 and MISSION-008 (:566-573), so auditing **your own card** satisfies MISSION-005 and pays 2 000 $VBV.
  4. **`security_override` MOVES A CARD BY DELETING A LOAN ROW AND INCREMENTING AN INVENTORY COUNTER** — it locates the card in `l.blackMarket` by `CollateralBundle.CardID` (:637-644), removes that element with `append(l.blackMarket[:i], l.blackMarket[i+1:]...)` (:653) and does `playerStats.Inventory[cardKey]++` (**measured at :644**) — **with no nil guard on `Inventory`: a grep shows NO `Inventory = make(...)` line anywhere in this file**, so a wallet whose map is nil panics here (the class `rivalry_handlers.go` and `battle_service.go` both guard) — plus no settlement of the borrower's record and no write-back of the owner's state. The audit line NAMES the original owner the card came from (:658), so the loss is real and untracked.
  5. **THE FORENSIC MISSION'S OWNER LOOKUP IS A FULL LEADERBOARD WALK THAT TAKES THE FIRST MATCH** (:556-563) — for a card present in two inventories (a mid-flight bundle transfer, a duplicated key) the reward is judged against whichever wallet map iteration reaches first, so the decision is not reproducible.

 12. **NO SEASONAL EVENT STATE IS PERSISTED.** The four maps live only in `SeasonalEventEngine`, appear in **no record family** and have no `Save`/`Load` — so every event, pool, cache and authored event dies on restart, including `pool.Distributed` (the counter that is supposed to make distribution auditable). The `season_archive_state` family the record set declares is a different structure for a different purpose, so nothing here is carried.
