- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS.**
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **A BUG THAT IS NOW SELF-DOCUMENTING, KEPT AS EVIDENCE.** `RecordTournamentOnChain`'s own comment (:812-822) records that it used to "write" both note streams with `IndexerRequest(l, cfg, prefix+json)` — an HTTP **GET** whose URL PATH was the note — so the archive was written NOWHERE, a nonsense request fired per chunk, and `summary.Links` was filled with those URLs while documented as *"TxIDs for additional match data"*. Fixed 2026-09-19 (Problems §39): notes go through `sendAuditNoteStream` and `Links` is left **empty rather than invented**. The sweep's clearest example of a FABRICATED field replaced by an honest absence.
  2. **EVERY PAYOUT VALUE IS A FLOAT.** `payoutPercentages := []float64{0.40, 0.25, 0.15, 0.10, 0.10}` → `percentMicro := uint64(payoutPercentages[i] * 100)` → `shareMicro := (effectivePotMicro * percentMicro) / 100` (:708-716); `DispatchTournamentRewards` applies `multiplier := 1.1` for Rep ≥ 500 then `uint64(float64(baseAmt) * multiplier)` and `uint64(float64(potShareMicro) * multiplier)` (:873-881); the faucet deduction is `uint64(totalUnits * 1000000)` where `totalUnits` was accumulated as `float64(amt) / 1000000.0` — a micro value round-tripped through a float sum (:908, :944). `effectivePotMicro := PotMicro - uint64(govTax * 1000000)` (:700) computes the tax in FLOAT to subtract it AFTER the same tax was computed in integer micro two lines earlier.
  3. **THE RESERVATION IS SET FROM A FLOAT MULTIPLY AND CLEARED ABSOLUTELY.** `l.pendingTournamentPayoutsMicro = uint64(float64(effectivePotMicro) * 1.1)` (:723) — the "10 % buffer" is a float multiply — and after the payout group completes it is set to **0 outright** (:756) rather than decremented, while `DispatchTournamentRewards` DOES decrement it per successful asset (`-= appliedPotPortionMicro`, :949, correctly assigned only when the primary asset dispatched). Two sites, two meanings for one field.
  4. **THE GOVERNOR TAX IS PAID ON A TERRITORY STRING, THEN THE CLUB'S TREASURY IS OVERWRITTEN FROM A ROUTER NODE.** `centerClub := l.getClubByTerritoryID("arena_center")` (:657) · `govTaxMicro = (PotMicro * 5) / 100` (:660) · the faucet is debited and the router routes the same amount for governance (:668-670) · then `centerClub.TreasuryMicro = node.TreasuryBalance` (:674-677) DISCARDS whatever the club's own accounting held, with `numericID, _ := strconv.ParseUint(strings.TrimPrefix(centerClub.ID, "CLUB-"), 10, 64)` discarding its error.
  5. **`DetermineTop5` IS INTENTIONALLY UNEQUAL AND SAYS SO** — Governors (2+ territories) sort ABOVE higher-reputation semi-finalists (:612-632), a stated Pillar-1 political rule deciding 25 % + 15 % of the pot. Note the two lookups are not the same comparison: `getRep` lowercases the wallet while `isGov` lowercases and then `EqualFold`s it against the club owner.
  6. **THE HISTORY READ IS PAGE-LIMITED TO 1000 TRANSFERS WITH NO PAGINATION** — `/arc200/transfers?contractId=…&from=…&limit=1000` (:265) — so past 1 000 vault transfers the archive silently truncates, and the checksum/receipt verification runs against a partial history while reporting success for whatever it saw.

### `lock_order_gate_test.go` — 990 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-990, read in windows 1-151, 151-421, 600-761, 760-990)
- **PURPOSE:** **the CROSS-MUTEX LOCK-ORDER GATE** — no pair of mutexes may be acquired in both orders anywhere in the tree (:17-60). Its reason is stated as the third shape the other gates cannot see: `selflock_gate_test.go` asks whether a function calls a helper taking the lock it ALREADY HOLDS, `mutex_relock_gate_test.go` asks whether a body acquires a mutex it already holds, and **both are blind to a deadlock that needs TWO locks**. The reader-reader nuance is made explicit too: a queued WRITER on an `RWMutex` blocks every later reader, so a reader-reader pair is fatal as soon as a third goroutine queues a write — and in this tree, where request paths hold the lobby WRITE lock across their bodies, **a queued lobby writer is the NORMAL state**.
- **OWNS:** the analysis VALUE `loAnalysis` (field types, mutex names, method takes, ambiguous set, edges, censuses, seeds, and the ALIASED-MAP subject it measures for the sibling gate) :66-92 · `loDeclRef` :95 · `loVarsFor` :99 · `loEdge` :112 · `loSyncMutexType` :117 · `loTypeString` :134 · `loBaseType`/`loPathOf` :161-178 · **`loDeriveFields`** :183 · **`loDeriveMethodTakes`** (the one-call-edge registry, with the AMBIGUOUS rule) :221 · `loWalker` :272 · `loObjType` :287 · **`loCanonMutex`/`loCanonField`/`loCanonFieldOf`** (the canonicalisation) :306-350 · `note`/`order`/`block`/`stmt` :352-380 · **`call`** (lock ops + the ONE call edge + the `...Locked` SEED) :610-648 · **`loAnalyse`** :651-733 · **`loInversions`** :737 · `loSources` :760 · **`lockOrderBaseline`** (EMPTY) :785 · `loPairInverts` :788 · **`TestNoCrossMutexLockOrderInversion`** :801 · **`TestLockOrderGateDetectsAnInversion`** (the control) :868-990.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** two tests — the gate and its NEGATIVE CONTROL.
- **CALLS OUT:** `go/ast`, `go/parser`, `go/token`, `os`, `path/filepath`, `sort`, `strings`, `fmt`, `testing`. `mutexSource` is REUSED from the sibling gate deliberately (:756-759): *"each gate must be independently reason-able, and a shared loader is how one gate's change silently becomes another's"*.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** no runtime state; the analysis is a VALUE so *"the real tree and the synthetic control cannot contaminate each other"* (:63-65). **The walk is shared verbatim with the sibling gate's semantics**: a `defer`red release does NOT clear the held state at its source line; `if`/`else`, `switch`/`select` clauses and loops are JOINED (a loop may run zero times, so it carries out only what was already held); a `go` literal starts from an EMPTY held set; a `defer`red CALL is not analysed as an edge at all (LIFO).
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS.**
  7. **`DispatchTournamentRewards` READS THE VAULT BALANCE FROM AN APPLICATION BOX — THE READ SESSION (s) PROVED IMPOSSIBLE FOR AN ARC-200 TOKEN.** `client.GetApplicationBoxByName(appID, vaultAddrObj[:])` then `new(big.Int).SetBytes(boxResponse.Value[:32])` (:908-915): an ARC-200 app **has no such box**, so the fetch fails and the asset is **SKIPPED** (:912-916) — the group can silently omit every asset whose box cannot be read and still report success with a `skipped` list, and the tournament's own primary reward is read this way. The live fix in session (s) replaced exactly this read with `/arc200/balances` in `oracle_service.go`; this file still holds the old shape.
  8. **EVERY ERROR IN THE SIGNING PATH IS DISCARDED.** `sp, _ := client.SuggestedParams().Do(...)` (:887) · `vaultAddrObj, _ := types.DecodeAddress(l.vaultAddress)` (:886) · `txn, _ := transaction.MakeApplicationNoOpTx(...)` (:929) · `_, stxn, _ := crypto.SignTransaction(faucetAccount.PrivateKey, txns[i])` (:940). A malformed vault address yields a zero-value address and the transaction is STILL built and signed, and a failed signature contributes an **EMPTY slice** to the group being broadcast — the same discarded-error class the sweep recorded on `auction_service.go`'s ARC-200 payment path.

  1. **IT DOCUMENTS THE TWO INVERSIONS IT FOUND ON ITS FIRST RUN, AND BOTH ARE LIVE-PATH STORIES.** `AICitizenEngine.mu` <-> `Lobby.mutex`: the behavioural tick held `ace.mu` for its whole body and took the lobby lock, while `runAutonomousTournament` (a 15-minute daemon) and the Zen-Garden doors held the lobby lock and reached into the engine (:30-34). `Lobby.mutex` <-> `TokenSinkRouter.Mu`: the persistence worker held the router lock and took the lobby lock, while **34 fee-routing sites** take the lobby lock and then `RouteCriminalTax` — which takes the router lock **for WRITING, the side that decides the deadlock** (:35-38). Both are fixed and the baseline is EMPTY, "and it stays that way by measurement rather than by trust".
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **`GetEvidenceAccuracyBonus` EXISTS TWICE — as a PACKAGE FUNCTION (:952) and as a METHOD (:1067) — WITH DIFFERENT ARITHMETIC.** The package form is a tier switch returning `2.0`/`1.0` for Forensic Analyst; the method is `1.0 + float64(tier)*0.15`, i.e. LINEAR and uncapped. The same name means two different things depending on whether a receiver is attached.
  2. **`GetFenceTier`'s DOC COMMENT DESCRIBES THE $VBV-SUSTAINED LADDER WHILE ITS BODY MAPS XP.** The doc lists tiers 1-6 against $VBV thresholds (:825-833 — Tier 3 "≥ 25K $VBV", Tier 6 "≥ 2M $VBV") but the body is `getTierFor(cxp, "Fence")` (:840), the XP ladder capped at **4**. The function can never return 5 or 6, its documented ladder is unreachable, and `GetFenceFeeDiscount`'s `tier >= 3` branch is the highest that can fire — the same unearnable-gate class session (h) caught once already.
  3. **TWO MORE COMMENTS OVERSTATE THEIR OWN BODIES.** `GetPlanningBuff` claims *"Tier 1 = ×1.05, … Boss (tier≥7) = ×1.35 cap"* (:988) while `getTierFor` returns at most 4; `GetHeistDividendRate` claims *"base 0.02 (2%) per member in planner's org"* (:992-993) then returns `0.02 + float64(tier)*0.01` labelled *"Tier 1 = ×1%"* (:1016) — the label contradicts the value and **no member count is read at all**.
  4. **FIVE FLOAT HELPERS REMAIN ON DISCOUNTS AND RATES:** `GetRumorFeeDiscount` and `GetJusticeMissionFeeDiscount` are both `float64(LessonLevel) * 0.01` (:182, :188) — the SAME expression twice; `GetFenceFeeDiscount` :783; `GetAuditPrecisionBonus` :896; `GetTransitTaxExemption` :1026 (where `1.0` means 100 % exemption and `0.0` means none, a sign convention the type does not carry); `GetWardenDetentionBonus` :1042.
  5. **`CalculateLessonLevel` IS A READ-SHAPED HELPER THAT WRITES THE RECORD, WITH A CURVE ITS COMMENT DESCRIBES WRONGLY.** *"Exponential curve: each level requires 1500 more XP than previous"* (:201) against `totalXP >= uint64(level*level*30+level*1500)` (:204) — the `n²·30` term makes each level cost an ADDITIONAL `60n+30`, the loop is `O(√XP)` per call, and it ASSIGNS `cxp.LessonLevel = level` (:207).

### `mutex_relock_gate_test.go` — 1174 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-1174, read in windows 1-151, 300-521, 520-761, 760-1001, 1000-1174)
- **PURPOSE:** **the SAME-MUTEX RE-LOCK GATE, in TWO PASSES** — no function may take a mutex it already holds, directly (pass 1) or across ONE CALL EDGE (pass 2). Its reason is the strongest in the lock family: `sync.Mutex`/`sync.RWMutex` are NOT re-entrant, and because this tree's request paths hold the lobby WRITE lock across their bodies, *"for ever" means the WHOLE PROCESS: every request, every WebSocket, every ticker* (:20-24). Its seam with the sibling gates is stated exactly: `selflock_gate_test.go` owns the CALL shape **from `*Lobby` receivers only** (*"which is exactly why it is structurally blind to any other type's mutex"*), `map_race_gate_test.go` owns map access, and **this gate owns the DIRECT shape on ANY receiver type** — the shape two LIVE doors carried, `handleInvestEntity` and `HandleDirectInvest`, each of which took `EntityMarketNode.Mu` for writing and re-took it for reading on the success path so **every successful direct investment froze the process** (:25-35).
- **OWNS:** `mutexSource` :78 · **`relockEntry`** (+ its `String()` that renders the witness edge) :84-101 · `isSyncMutexType` :104 · **`derivedMutexFields`** (the derived subject + a struct census) :118 · the `relockWalk` type and its `line`/`report`/`lockCall` methods :~170-300 · **`stmt`** (the MUST-held statement walk) :302-380 · **`clauses`** (switch/select joining, with the no-`default` pre-state rule) :382-410 · `stmtExpressions` :414 · **`exprForLits`** (closures + the one-edge pass over expression positions) :418 · **PASS 2:** `mutexAcq`/`methodMutexTake`/`signature` :447-468 · **`deriveMethodMutexTakes`** :473-540 · `relockReceiverType` :544 · **`callEdge`** (the call-site receiver identity) :557 · **`measureRelocksAcrossOneCallEdge`** :576 · `measureSameMutexRelocks` :630 · `realMutexSources` :645 · **`sameMutexRelockBaseline`** and **`sameMutexRelockViaCallBaseline`** (BOTH EMPTY) :668-676 · **`TestNoSameMutexRelock`** :678 · `TestMutexRelockDerivationSeesTheTwoLiveDoors` :~880 · `TestSyncMutexTypeRuleIsNotOverBroad` :~915 · **`TestSameMutexRelockGateDetectsAViolation`** (15 shapes: 5 must report, 10 must not) :~940 · **`TestSameMutexRelockGateDetectsARelockAcrossACallEdge`** (6 shapes: 2 must, 4 must not) :~1055.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** five tests — the gate, the derivation check, the type-rule check and TWO negative controls.
- **CALLS OUT:** `go/ast`, `go/parser`, `go/token`, `os`, `path/filepath`, `sort`, `strings`, `fmt`, `testing` — and `funcDeclKey` / `exprKey` / `cloneHeld` / `intersectHeld` from the sibling gates' shared vocabulary.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** no runtime state. **The analysis is MUST-HELD with FOUR stated rules** (:46-70): branches joined (a mutex counts as held after an `if` only if EVERY path acquired it; a loop may run zero times so it carries out only what was already held — *"that is what removed the flat-source-order false positives which made the sibling gate's first version unusable"*); **a `defer`red release is NOT a release at its source line** (which is what makes this tree's dominant `Lock(); defer Unlock(); …` idiom visible); a `go` literal starts from an EMPTY held set while a non-`go` closure **shares** the goroutine and IS analysed from the current set; and an expression that cannot be rendered to a stable key is **SKIPPED, never guessed**. Pass 2 adds: `*Lobby` receivers EXCLUDED because *"a shape owned by two gates is how one of them starts lying"*; a method name declared by two types with different acquired-field sets is SKIPPED; a `go` callee is not an edge though its arguments are; a `defer`red callee is not analysed (LIFO) and a `range`-expression call is not walked — both stated false negatives; **DEPTH 1 only**; and `-race` is UNAVAILABLE here (needs cgo), so it is a STATIC census of a shape that is a deadlock BY CONSTRUCTION, not a race proof.

  6. **ONE PAIR IS STILL PRICED IN TWO OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS — `"ForensicAnalyst↔Gossip"` → −10 (:423) and `"Gossip↔ForensicAnalyst"` → +5 (:438)** — and `rivalPairTable`'s own tombstone (:469-479) records that the +5 row was unreachable and has been DELETED FROM THE TABLE while **the `switch` case remains**. So `GetRivalXPDelta` answers +5 for a name no scan can produce: dead pricing kept beside a live comment stating it is dead.
  7. **`GetRivalXPGainPermille` MAKES AN ANTAGONISTIC PAIR WORTH MORE THAN A SYNERGISTIC ONE, DELIBERATELY.** `1000 + |delta| * 10` (:378-382): `BountyHunter↔Kidnapper` (−15) → 1150 against `HeistPlanner↔Kidnapper` (+12) → 1120. The comment defends it (*"the point of declaring it antagonistic"*) and it reproduces the float it replaced, but the SIGN of a pair is invisible in the award size — only the magnitude is.

- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **EVERY CAREFULLY-BUILT XP AWARD IN THIS FILE IS MULTIPLIED BY ZERO — THE `ComputeScaledXP` GATE READS `RoleXP` WHILE `trackCareerXPLocked` WRITES `PromotedRoles`.** Measured across two files: `ComputeScaledXP`'s `hasCareer` falls back to *"any RoleXP value > 0"*, and `TrackCareerXP` writes `cxp.RoleXP[role] += xp` (never `PromotedRoles`, which the sweep confirmed has **no writer**). So the gate is a function of a map the same call site is populating — and the sibling engine's own doc records the compounding: *"`PromotedRoles` is written by NOTHING … so `CareerHasRole` is permanently false."* The awards here are therefore approximate by construction, and their scaling changes as the map fills.
  2. **`handleCyberIntercept` READS THE QUALIFICATION ITSELF AND GETS IT RIGHT, WHICH PROVES THE POINT:** it states *"`CareerHasRole` cannot be the gate: nothing in the repository ever WRITES `PromotedRoles` … so it is permanently false"* (:~905-910) and uses `HasCareer(cxp, "Intel-Agent") || RoleKey(JobRole) == RoleKey("Intel-Agent")`. **The same file then uses `CareerHasRole` as the ONLY role check in `handleReleaseHostage`, `handleAOSRaid` and four other sites** — one file, two opposite conclusions about whether the field is usable.
  3. **A RANSOM IS PAID WITHOUT ANY CHECK AGAINST WHAT THE HOSTAGE REGISTRY RECORDS.** `handleRansomPayment` debits `data.RansomAmount` from `data.VictimWallet`'s balance (a BODY-declared victim) while the registry's `HostageSituation.RansomAmount` is what `RegisterKidnap` stored — so the two can disagree, and the file's own comment (session (j)) records that `ResolveRivalXPAward` was fed `data.RansomAmount/10`.
  4. **THE AOS RAID MOVES 5 000 $VBV OF REWARD THAT NOTHING FUNDED.** On success each participant is credited `(5000 * 1000000) / len(participants)` (:~1160-1170) with no pool, escrow or debit — the same mint class as the file's contract payouts (CONTRACT-005 3 000, CONTRACT-010 5 000, MISSION-002 500). The 1 500 $VBV deployment fee IS debited and routed to `faucetBalanceMicro`, so the raid both takes and creates money in one body.
  5. **THE RAID'S OUTCOME IS A FLOAT DICE ROLL.** `successChance := 0.40 + (float64(teamSize-1) * 0.10)` then `rand.Float64() < successChance` (:~1155) — `math/rand` decides a money-and-XP outcome, in a repository whose determinism rule is explicit. The same pattern appears in `handleCyberIntercept`'s `rand.Intn(15)` signal variance.
  6. **A FAILED RAID STILL CHARGES THE FEE.** The deployment fee is debited **before** the roll (:~1150) and the failure branch only penalises `WantedLevel`/`Reputation` (:~1175-1180) — so the dice roll's loser pays 1 500 $VBV for nothing, which is a deliberate design only if something says so, and nothing does.
  7. **THE TEAM LOOKUP RANGES `l.clients` AND READS `l.leaderboard` BY A WALLET IT DID NOT RESOLVE** (:~1125-1132): `cw, ok := l.wallets[client.id]` then `l.leaderboard[cw].EmployerClubID == stats.EmployerClubID` — a map index whose value is used without the `ok` that the map would give, on a wallet spelling taken from the connection map, and the `break` at `teamSize >= 4` makes team composition CONNECTION-ORDER dependent (a player's share of the 5 000 depends on which lobby clients are online and in what iteration order).
  8. **`processInsuranceRecovery` RESTORES A CARD BY INCREMENTING A COUNTER AND RE-RANGES THE WHOLE LEADERBOARD PER RECOVERED CARD** (:~745-830) — `victimStats.Inventory[cardKey]++` with no guard that the count was decremented (it assumes it), and then a full `for statsWallet := range l.leaderboard` loop per card to award Warden/Forensic onlooker XP. Same shape in `handleReleaseHostage` (three separate full-leaderboard sweeps in one body) and in `handleRansomPayment` (five).
  9. **THE SECTOR-WIDE XP ONLOOKER LOOPS ARE THE FILE'S DOMINANT COST AND ITS DOMINANT BEHAVIOUR.** Ransom payment awards XP to *every* Warden and *every* Forensic Analyst on the server, twice each, plus a Bounty Hunter loop and a Sector Peacekeeper loop; `handleReleaseHostage` and `processInsuranceRecovery` repeat it. So ONE player action writes XP to every matching wallet in the leaderboard, inside the write lock — a per-event O(players) cost and a balance surface no single event's design states.
 10. **THE INTERCEPT'S ROUTE IS `handleCyberInterceptWrapper`, AND ITS REFUSALS ITERATE.** The cyber-intercept body uses `writeJSONStatus` for its early refusals and then `http.Error(w, "Event not found or already intercepted", http.StatusBadRequest)` (:~937) for the re-intercept branch — two response conventions in one handler, with the second sending a TEXT body where every other branch sends JSON.

- **STATE & LOCKS:** **It uses `Lobby.mutex` and declares that as the design** (:127-131: *"Reuses the Lobby.mutex RWMutex pattern established by rivalry_engine.go — no additional mutex needed because all ThemeEngine reads/writes go through Lobby.mutex"*), and it states the caching decision in the same breath — caching was DEFERRED *"to avoid RWMutex re-entrancy hazards"* (:132-134, and again at :252-255). `computeThemeVectorLocked`'s contract is explicit: *"Caller MUST hold l.mutex (read or write)"*. **`computeRegionThemeCoherence` carries session (m)'s fix in full** (:510-540): the resident wallets are SNAPSHOT under `RLock`, released, and each vector derived OUTSIDE, with the reason recorded — *"ComputeThemeVector takes the lobby lock ITSELF, so calling it here was a RECURSIVE READ LOCK … the process freezes"*.

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / THE FILE'S GOOD HALF.**
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **`computeRegionRumorCoherence` IS THE ONE SIGNAL READER THAT IS CASE-SENSITIVE, AND A CASING DIFFERENCE READS AS ZERO.** `inRegion` is keyed by the LEADERBOARD's wallet spelling while it is probed with `inRegion[rumor.TargetWallet]` (:608) — and `l.rumors` keys are whatever the rumor writer stored. A casing difference turns every rumor into a miss, with no error and no log: the same class the tree has closed five times for balances, card views and wallet keys.
  2. **RUMOR STRENGTH IS A FLOAT ROUND-TRIPPED THROUGH AN INT** — `strength := int(clampInt64(int64(rumor.Strength*10), 0, 100))` (:612) — while the file's own header claims *"all math is integer, deterministic, no RNG"*. The clamp also saturates a strength above 10 and flattens a negative strength to 0.
  3. **SIX SIGNAL FUNCTIONS TAKE THE LOBBY READ LOCK RECURSIVELY.** Each begins with `regionClubs := l.clubsInRegion(region)` (which takes `RLock`) and then takes `l.mutex.RLock()` AGAIN for its own walk (:510, :568, :590, :622). A recursive RLock is safe only while NO writer queues — and this tree queues lobby writers constantly, which is the mechanism session (m) turned from three "notes" into three failures. `ResolveEvent`'s comment asserts the lock pair is safe because `ComputeThemeVector` uses `l.mutex` *"independently"*, which is true of `seasonEngine.Mu` but not of these nested `RLock`s.
  4. **THE HOLDINGS LADDER IS WRITTEN TWICE IN ONE FILE, WITH A RUNG MISSING FROM ONE COPY.** `holdingsToScore` (:684-706) encodes 5B/1B/250M/50M/10M as 100/80/60/40/20; the Element switch (:196-215) encodes 5B/1B/250M/50M as Aether/Machine/Earth/Water/Fire — so a wallet between 0 and 10M falls to `ElementFire` while `holdingsToScore` calls it 0…5.
  5. **`OutcomeBias` RE-READS THE STATE IT WAS HANDED A VECTOR FOR.** It calls the `CalculateTotalPortfolioValue` DOOR (which takes the read lock itself) and then takes `l.mutex.RLock()` again for `stats` (:262-300), with its own comment saying *"read live, not from cached vector, for accuracy"* — two reads that can disagree under load.
  6. **THREE IDENTICAL FEATURE GATES ARE WRITTEN THREE TIMES WITH THREE DESCRIPTIONS.** `governor`, `regional` and `governance` in `handlePlayerProgression` each compute the SAME `float64(ownedTerritories)/2.0` from the same `isGovernor` boolean, with `gate_text` differing ("Own 2 territories", "Become Governor first", "Governor status required"). Every progress value in that handler is a float derived from an integer balance or count (`/5000*1000000`, `/5.0`, `/2.0` ×4, `/10.0`, `/1000*1000000`).

### `selflock_gate_test.go` — 1479 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-1479, read in windows 1-161, 300-561, 760-1001, 1000-1241, 1240-1479)
- **PURPOSE:** **THE SELF-LOCK GATE — in TWO passes plus a BEHAVIOURAL half.** Pass 1: no function may reach a lobby helper that takes the lobby lock ITSELF while already holding it. The transitive pass: no body may be ENTERED with the lock held and reach such a helper however many calls away. Behavioural: the recommended `...Locked` forms must actually RUN under the write lock. Its reason is stated first: `l.mutex` is a `sync.RWMutex`, which is not re-entrant, so a re-entrant call *"does not slow down, does not error, and does not return: it DEADLOCKS, and because the write lock is never released it freezes every request and every WebSocket in the process"* (:20-24).
- **OWNS:** provenanced by **SEVEN instances found BY READING** — `handleBailCard`, `HandleRepayLoan`, `HandleDetectCounterfeit`, `use_item ▸ legal_pardon`, `handleCyberIntercept`, `handleKidnapRequest` and the `sendToClient` family (:26-31), with the observation that *"most of them sat behind DEAD arithmetic or an unexercised branch, so the code simply never ran and no behaviour test could have caught them"* · **`heldKind`** (`heldNone`/`heldRead`/`heldWrite`) + `joinMust` :95-125 · `lobbyHelper` with its `sibling` and `locks()` :128-146 · **`deadlocksUnder`** — `held != heldNone`, with all three combinations argued :160-178 · `lockAuditor` :300-340 · `selfLockSite` :345 · `recordSite` :352 · `rootIdentLocal` :365 · **`stmt`/`goCall`/`isLobbyLockRelease`/`clauses`/`simple`** :380-560 · `loadLobbyTree`/`deriveHelpers`/`indexLobbyTree` :~570-760 · **`TestNoSelfLockingLobbyHelper`** (pass 1) :761 · **`TestSelfLockDerivationSeesTheRightMethods`** (the DERIVATION's own control) :800 · **`TestSelfLockGateDetectsAViolation`** (13 shapes: 8 must, 5 must not) :~840-1000 · **`TestTheRecommendedLockedFormsRunWhileTheWriteLockIsHeld`** (behavioural, fails by TIMEOUT) :1002 · **`transitiveSelfLockAudit`** + `propagate`/`report` :1030-1170 · `TestNoTransitiveSelfLockingLobbyHelper` :1180 · **`TestTransitiveSelfLockDetectsAViolation`** (9 shapes) :1245 · **`TestTheMatchmakingPairingPathCompletesUnderTheWriteLock`** :~1400 · **`TestTheCareerProgressDoorCompletesAndWritesUnderTheWriteLock`** :~1440.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** seven tests — two gates, two controls, one derivation control, and three behavioural watchdogs.
- **CALLS OUT:** `go/ast`, `go/parser`, `go/token`, `net/http` + `net/http/httptest` (for the behavioural doors), `os`, `path/filepath`, `sort`, `strings`, `time`, `testing`.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** no runtime state; the analysis walks ASTs. **FOUR DECISIONS CARRY THE WEIGHT, and the file names the cost of getting each wrong** (:44-70): **MUST, NOT MAY** — the lock counts only when held on EVERY path (`if`/`else`/clauses/loops joined), because the previous flat-source-order version let one `switch` case's `Lock()` poison every later case and **40 of its 44 reports were that single false positive** (*"which is precisely how a gate gets switched off"*); **a DEFERRED RELEASE releases at FUNCTION EXIT**, so it does not clear the held state where written — *"what makes the dominant spelling of this defect visible"*; **THE RECEIVER'S TYPE DECIDES** (`*Lobby` identifier only, and the assumption is MEASURED: all 557 Lobby methods use receiver `l` and **no type embeds `*Lobby`**); **`fallthrough` IS MODELLED** because the tree uses it twice.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / COSTS.**
  7. **`constellationPaths` IS A HAND-WRITTEN GRAPH WITH NO VALIDATION** (:~1185) — seven arrays of feature KEYS as strings, tied to nothing: a renamed feature silently yields a path pointing at a key the `features` map does not contain, and no gate covers this table the way the tree gates `WD_ROUTES`.
  8. **THE HEADER'S "DEFERRED → 0 (NOT FABRICATED)" IS CONTRADICTED BY THE BODY THAT WIRED THEM.** The header (:12-16) states FaithCoherence / DomesticCoherence / EntityLegitimacy are *"scored 0, NOT fabricated"* because *"no real data structures exist in code yet"*; the body says **"§27.7 / §32: now COMPUTABLE"**, **"§27.7.1: NOW WIRED (structs present at ai_citizen_engine.go:49-63)"** and **"§27.7.3: NOW WIRED"** (:~404-430) with three real aggregators. **The header is stale**, which is the failure direction that matters: a reader who trusts the top of the file under-reports three live signals, and a reader who trusts the body cannot tell which of the two was written last.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS / INSTRUMENT LIMITS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).** This is the gate that has caught the most real defects in the tree; what follows is its own disclosed limits, the controls that prove it works, and the findings it owns.
  1. **IT CARRIES THE FULL PROVENANCE OF THE CLASS: SEVEN INSTANCES, ALL FOUND BY READING, AND WHY READING WAS NOT ENOUGH.** The header names every one and states that **most sat behind dead arithmetic or an unexercised branch**, so the code never ran and no behaviour test could have caught them (:26-31) — *"Reading is not a gate"*. That sentence is the reason this file exists in the form it does.
  2. **`deadlocksUnder` IS `held != heldNone`, AND THE READ+READ CASE IS ARGUED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The doc lists all three combinations — write+write, read+write, **read+read** — and states the mechanism for the third: *"Go's RWMutex blocks a second RLock the moment a writer queues, and the first RLock is still held, so the WRITER is what decides; the write lock is taken by matchmaking, club, market, treasury and admin paths constantly, so the queue is the normal state under load rather than a corner case"* (:160-178). It records that this case **used to be a NOTE**, and that the transitive pass then measured **THREE live sites** of it (a GET handler, a per-club loop, a per-player loop) — all fixed — and that `TestSelfLockGateDetectsAViolation` now carries a `badRecursiveRead` shape **so a future edit that softens it back to a note fails the build instead of quietly widening the hole**.
  3. **THE DERIVATION HAS ITS OWN CONTROL, WHICH IS RARE AND MATTERS.** `TestSelfLockDerivationSeesTheRightMethods` drives a synthetic file and asserts the registry recognises a self-locking method, **does NOT mistake a `...Locked` form for one**, **does NOT derive `takesNothing` or `notTheLobby`** (a `*Service` method taking the lock), and **connects a helper to its `...Locked` sibling** so the failure message can name the fix (:800-838). A registry is a detector's input, so it is measured too.
  4. **THE MAIN CONTROL CARRIES THIRTEEN SHAPES — 8 MUST, 5 MUST NOT — AND THREE OF THE FIVE ARE THE FALSE POSITIVES THAT BROKE THE PREVIOUS VERSION.** The eight: a deferred unlock, read-then-write, **recursive read**, one switch case, both branches, a `*Lobby` parameter, an `if` CONDITION, and a **`go` ARGUMENT**. The five: the sibling form, **a later switch case that never holds the lock**, released-first, a non-Lobby receiver, and **`go l.sendToClient(...)`** — the last being *"the exact shape the real tree uses twice (`go l.sendNoteTx(...)` in market_service.go), and reporting it was a false positive on correct code"* (:~880-1000). It also asserts the count EXACTLY (8), so an extra report is a failure, and that the message NAMES the sibling.
  5. **THE `go` STATEMENT IS MODELLED AS TWO HALVES, WITH A REASON FOR EACH.** A `go` callee is NOT a call site — *"A GOROUTINE DOES NOT INHERIT THE LOCK … it blocks until the parent releases, which it does"* — while **its ARGUMENTS and the receiver expression ARE** walked, because `go f(x)` evaluates `x` in the calling goroutine. Both halves are pinned by named control shapes (`fineInNewGoroutine` / `badInGoArgument`), and `goCall` is documented as *"the mirror image of `collectCalls`, descending the same two places for the opposite reason"*.
  6. **THE INTRA-PROCEDURAL LIMIT IS STATED PRECISELY AND THEN CLOSED BY THE SECOND PASS.** The stated guarantee is *"no function reaches a self-locking helper while holding the lock ITSELF, which is the shape of every one of the seven instances"* — and the comment shows the blind spot explicitly: `F holds l.mutex -> calls G (no lock of its own) -> G calls H (takes l.mutex)`. **The transitive pass was NOT built on suspicion:** its first run found the deadlock inside `initiatePairedMatch` reached from `processMatchmaking`'s write lock — **the matchmaking pairing path, which froze the process on EVERY successful pairing** (:1017-1030).

- **PURPOSE:** **the hosted-server entrypoint and THE COMPLETE ROUTE TABLE** — `main()` with its secret pre-validation, `killExistingServer`, the graceful-shutdown wiring, and then the whole API surface: **326 `mux.HandleFunc` registrations**, every one wrapped in `lobby.rateLimiter.WithRateLimit(handler, tier)`.

- **ENTRY POINTS:** the binary's `main()`; **every API route in the product is registered here**, and `console_server.go` mirrors this table under the `console` tag.
- **CALLS OUT:** `godotenv.Load()`, `newLobby()`, `lobby.run()`, `serveWs`, `lobby.rateLimiter.WithRateLimit` (on every route), `lobby.executeGracefulShutdown`, `http.FileServer(http.Dir("./Public"))`, `json.NewEncoder` / `http.Error` / `writeJSON` per-handler, `exec.Command("netstat")` + `taskkill`.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** `main` itself touches the lobby lock exactly once — the signal handler takes `lobby.mutex.Lock()`, prints, and **immediately unlocks** before `executeGracefulShutdown` (:112-117), which is the correct shape (the shutdown path takes its own locks per step). The `clientErrLog` slice is guarded by its own `clientErrMu` with a **bounded 200-entry ring** trimmed on append.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm && !console` :1

- **(continued) LIMITS, THE BEHAVIOURAL HALF, AND THE FINDINGS.**
  8. **EVERY LIMIT IS NAMED, AND TWO OF THEM ARE ASSERTED TO DECIDE NOTHING TODAY.** Intra-procedural (the transitive pass closes it); a `defer`red **CALL** is reported at its `defer` statement with the LIFO caveat stated — *"Measured: the tree contains no `defer` of a self-locking helper at all, so this limit currently decides nothing"*; a `break`/`goto` carrying a freshly taken lock is not modelled (*"the gate errs toward silence there, never toward noise"*); an untyped local through which a call is made is **skipped rather than guessed**; `_test.go` is not scanned *"because a test that holds the lock on purpose is TESTING the lock"*; and in the transitive pass, an ambiguous held-service name is left unresolved, paths are **JOINED not tracked** so every finding names its WITNESS edge, and *"calls the resolver cannot place are simply not edges and are counted NOWHERE, because a field that always answers 0 is a false statement"*.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THE ROUTE TABLE IS A HAND-MAINTAINED MIRROR OF `console_server.go` AND NOTHING ASSERTS PARITY.** This file's own header states the split (*"the console build can supply its own loopback-only main() while reusing all the helpers"*), and the sweep's `console_server.go` entry records that **its API table has nothing asserting the two agree** — so a route added to one table silently does not exist in the other build.
  2. **`killExistingServer` IS A PRODUCTION PATH THAT SHELLS OUT TO `netstat` + `taskkill`, WITH BOTH ERRORS DISCARDED** (:27-56). It runs unconditionally before the listener (:1690), matches by SUBSTRING (`strings.Contains(line, ":"+port)`), ignores `cmd.Output()`'s error on the early return, ignores `killCmd.Run()`, and guards only against killing ITSELF. On a host where another service holds that port — or where `PORT=8082` collides with a local `algod` REST port, as `.env.example` ships per session (o) — it kills that process.
  3. **TWO SIGNAL HANDLERS ARE REGISTERED, AND THE SECOND `os.Exit`s PAST THE GRACEFUL PATH.** `main` installs a `signal.Notify` handler that takes the mutex, seals state and runs `executeGracefulShutdown` (:107-127); just before listening it installs a SECOND on the same signals that prints and calls **`os.Exit(0)`** (:1700-1708) with the comment *"Graceful shutdown on Ctrl+C"* — which is precisely what it does not do. Whichever goroutine wins the signal decides whether state is committed.
  4. **/api/season/admin/* CARRIES ONLY A DEFAULT WALLET TIER.** `create-event`, `end-event` and `update-reward-pool` (:605-630) are the SEASONAL ADMINISTRATION doors, wrapped in **`wallet-default`** rather than an admin tier — and `end-event` resolves and PAYS OUT. A rate-limit tier is not an authentication boundary; compare `GET /api/admin/networks`, explicitly admin-gated since session (a). Nothing here supplies the missing gate.
  5. **THE CREATOR-STORE PATH ROUTES PARSE `parts[4]` BY POSITION WITH NO VALIDATION** (:672-760): `/api/creator/store/product/`, `/purchase/` and `/profile/` each split the path and take `parts[4]` when `len(parts) >= 5`, so a trailing-slash variant yields an EMPTY id and the handler proceeds with it.
  6. **THE SECONDARY-SALE ROYALTY IS ROUTED FOR A SALE WHOSE MONEY NEVER MOVES, FROM A CLIENT-DECLARED PRICE.** `cs.ProcessSecondarySale(...)` runs first (its own entry records it updates only a profile counter), and THEN `RouteCriminalTax("CREATOR_SECONDARY_SALE", req.SalePriceMicroVBV, matrix, …)` routes a real split of a **body-supplied** `SalePriceMicroVBV` through a **float** `RevenueSplitMatrix` (:1500-1530) — with the failure only logged.
  7. **THE NOTIFICATION BLOCK UNDER IT HOLDS THE LOBBY LOCK AND CALLS A LINEAR SCAN FOUR TIMES** (:1528-1545) — `getClientIDFromWalletLocked` walks `l.wallets` each time, **twice for the same buyer wallet** (`!= ""` then a second call to capture it), inside the write lock, in a `main`-registered handler.

  1. **`CareerUpgradeViewLocked`'S "IT NEVER MUTATES" IS TRUE OF THAT FUNCTION AND FALSE OF THE READ PATH BESIDE IT.** The doc states *"no request is recorded, no notice is marked sent and no career is touched … two reads in a row return the same thing"* — while `UnlockedCareerRolesLocked` and `CareerUnlockNoticesLocked` are ADJACENT functions in the SAME section whose purpose is to RECORD that a notice was sent (`CareerXP.UnlockNoticesSent`), reached from the same liquidity-daemon pass. A reader of either function cannot tell from the docs which one writes.
  2. **`JobRole` IS OVERWRITTEN BY EVERY PROMOTION WHILE `PromotedRoles` ACCUMULATES.** `PromoteCareerLocked` sets `stats.JobRole = req.Role` while the grant list grows — and `JobRole` is what ~40 call sites across the tree read as "the player's career" (the rival hooks, the item gates, `handlers_criminality.go`'s role checks). So a twice-promoted player is read by all of those as their LAST promotion only, and `HasCareer`/`CareerHasRole` (which read `RoleXP`/`PromotedRoles`) disagree with `JobRole` for everyone holding more than one. The demotion path repairs it (`remaining[0]` or `"Freelancer"`), which confirms the field is the display copy of a set.
  3. **THE PATH IS CHOSEN ONCE AND NO DOOR CAN CLEAR IT.** `ChooseCareerPathLocked` refuses a second choice (*"the choice is made once"*) and eligibility short-circuits on `CurrentPath != ""` — deliberate, but **nothing in the file clears or changes `CareerXP.Path`**, and no audit line records a path CHANGE. A mis-click has no recourse in the domain, and the operator has no correcting door.
  4. **THE CIVIL RANK PICKS A REGION BY MAP ITERATION ORDER.** `CivilStandingForWalletLocked` walks clubs, sums territories, and sets `RegionOpened`/`Region` from **the first** club with a non-empty `RegionName` — so the SERVED `Region` can differ between two identical requests for a multi-club player. `OwnedClubsForWalletLocked` later in the SAME file sorts ids deterministically, which is the pattern the standing loop does not follow.
  5. **THE `...Locked` CONTRACT LIVES IN THE DOCS, NOT IN THE NAMES, FOR HALF THE FILE** — roughly half the functions carry the suffix and the caller contract sentence, the other half are unlocked doors. Nothing verifies the contract at a boundary, which is why the tree has four gates for lock SHAPES and none for "is this `...Locked` callee actually called under the lock". The residual risk of the ownership boundary, recorded.

Elsewhere the file takes `l.mutex.RLock()` for config reads and `Lock()` only for the writes it owns (`inventory`, `faucetBalanceMicro`+`faucetBalance`+`vaultBalanceLive`+scaling in ONE section).

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / THE FILE'S STRONGEST GUARANTEES.**
  6. **`assertCareerPathDialect` IS A FIVE-PART BOOT PANIC AND IT IS THE FILE'S HARDEST GUARANTEE.** It refuses at `init()`: a path count other than 3; a path that does not fold to itself (which would make two paths one); an alias targeting a non-path; **a career declared in `careerPathByRole` with NO promotion gate in `careerRoleGates`** (naming the omission); a career whose `HomePath` DISAGREES between the two tables; a duplicated gate row (*"one owner per gate"*); an unknown rank; a `MinSustainedMicro` that is not a rung of the `VBVTier*` ladder; a level cap outside 1..100; **a `MinRoleTier` above `CareerRoleTierMax`, which would make the career permanently unearnable with no error anywhere — the silent-skip class**; and three matrix invariants (justice↔criminal must be `direct`, two neutrals must `interpreted`, two same-side players must not be direct). It caught the third role-spelling split (`"AOS"` vs `"AOS Leader"`) on its first run and three unearnable gates in session (h).
  7. **`CareerRoleTierMax` EXISTS BECAUSE THREE DIFFERENT TIER SCALES LIVE IN THE ENGINE, AND IT MEASURES THEM.** The comment states them: `getTierFor` bands XP into **1..4**, `CareerXP.GetCareerTier` is `xp/1500` and **unbounded**, and `CheckCareerTierGate` is the $VBV ladder **0..5** — *"a gate written against one of the other two scales would be silently unsatisfiable."* The constant, the boot panic and `TestEveryDeclaredRoleTierIsReachable` are three independent defences against that one mistake.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **A RAW-ed25519 PROTOCOL HERE BESIDE THE SDK'S DOMAIN-SEPARATED ONE IN THE ADMIN GATE — the exact conflict B2 measured and stopped on.** This file imports `crypto/ed25519` and calls `ed25519.Verify(publicKey, digest, signature)` over `CompilePayloadDigest`'s sha256 (raw ed25519 over a compiled digest), while session (s)/(B2) proved `algosdk`'s `crypto.VerifyBytes` **prepends a `bytesPrefix`** and that `handlers_admin.go` uses that form. So the tree holds **at least two AVM signature protocols**, and this file is the evidence that the raw one is a REAL second convention rather than a mistake — which is what makes B2's per-context-conventions correction right instead of a single verifier.
  2. **TWO NONCE MAPS THAT ONLY EVER GROW, IN NO RECORD FAMILY.** `GenerateNextNonce` inserts with a 2-minute TTL; `VerifyIncomingCommand` deletes only the nonce it CONSUMES (an expired one is deleted at its own verification attempt) — so a nonce that is never presented is never removed (no sweeper, no cap, no eviction), and `ConsumedNonces` only ever gains keys. Both maps are monotonic over a process lifetime and appear in no record family.
  3. **`CompilePayloadDigest`'S BYTE LAYOUT IS AN UNPUBLISHED CLIENT CONTRACT.** `CommandType` (1 byte) then five big-endian `uint64`s into a sha256 — a client must reproduce that layout EXACTLY to sign, and nothing in the file serves or documents it. The anti-padding intent is right; what is missing is the thing the tenant contract later solved for its own proof: a PUBLISHED template executed by a test.
  4. **THE ALGORAND BRANCH ENFORCES THE SAME PURPOSE BINDING BY A DIFFERENT, SOFTER PATH.** Voi reads `/arc200/transfers` and then `fetchTransactionNote` from `/v2/transactions/{txid}` — two facts, two endpoints, with the rule stated: *"A note that cannot be READ is a refusal, never a silent pass."* The Algorand branch reads ONE endpoint and matches `asset-transfer-transaction`/`payment-transaction` inline; a note it cannot read simply does not match and returns `false, 0, nil`, i.e. the refusal is indistinguishable from "not found yet" (which is deliberate for a 404, and silent for a decode failure).
  5. **`CheckNativeVaultBalanceOnChain` LOOPS `nodeCount` TIMES CALLING `GetBestClient()`** — each iteration asks the cluster (which takes its own lock and may hand back the node it just marked) — and its gas alert `info.Amount < 1000000` **broadcasts to admins on every such read with no dedupe**. The `IsSemantic404Error` branch is the §36 fix (an unfunded account is a healthy answer, not a blacklist) and it breaks correctly.
  6. **`parseARC200Amount` IS THE FILE'S STRONGEST BOUNDARY AND ITS COMMENT NAMES THE TRAP:** it refuses a non-digit string, a value whose LENGTH exceeds 20 digits, and a `ParseUint` overflow — *"a value above math.MaxUint64 is refused rather than truncated"*, and *"the ARC-200 mint/burn sentinel is not a balance"*. That is the Ledger's uint64 discipline applied where a uint256 arrives as a STRING.

### `handlers_admin.go` — 2182 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-2182, read in windows 1-171, 1249-1300, 2084-2181, plus a full `^func ` census of all 50 declarations)
- **PURPOSE:** **THE ENTIRE ADMIN SURFACE** — the audit log and its `...Locked` pair, the admin broadcast pair, the HTML-escape helper, the health broadcast, and 40 admin doors: refill, rules, reward registry, networks, power scaling, system messages, bans, stat resets, base reward, maintenance mode, tournaments, season rollover, audit export, simulations, forfeiture, force payout, node/sanity/ledger audits, emergency shutdown, DLC registry and the tax audits — plus the **signature gate** (`checkAdminAuth` → `verifyAdminSignature` → `isAdminWallet`).
- **OWNS:** **`logAdminAudit`/`logAdminAuditLocked`** (with a 5 MiB rotation to `.old` and a `server_load` field from `countUniqueMatchesLocked`) :29-71 · **`broadcastToAdmins`/`broadcastToAdminsLocked`** — the pair whose doc carries the self-deadlock it fixed and names the two callers that hold the WRITE lock :76-106 · `escapeHTML` :109 · `broadcastHealthReport` :131 · `handleRefillVault` :150 · `handleUpdateRules` :175 · **`handleAdminAddReward`** :203 · `handleAdminRemoveReward` :318 · `handleSetActiveNetwork` :362 · `handleAdminRestockDLC` :388 · **`handleAddNetwork`** (with the carry-forward upsert) :433 · **`handleUpdatePowerScaling`** (validate → persist → apply) :511 · `handleSystemMessage` :567 · `handleBanPlayer` :611 · `handleGloatBan` :653 · `handleAvatarBan` :674 · `handleResetStats` :724 · `handleUpdateBaseReward` :766 · `handleMaintenanceMode` :808 · **`handleUpdateRewardAsset`** (REFUSES, env-owned) :846 · `handleStartTournament` :881 · `handleOpenRegistration` :981 · `handleSeasonRollover` :1017 · `handleExportAuditLog` :1035 · `handleSimulateTournament` :1075 · `handleGetAdminLogs` :1103 · **`handleAssetForfeiture`** :1172 · **`handleForcePayout`** :1249 · `handleSimulateMojoDecay` :1294 · `handleSimulateMutationFailure`/`Success` :1325-1385 · `handlePlayerReport` :1386 · `handleLedgerAudit` :1434 · `handleNodeHealthAudit` :1514 · `handleSystemSanityCheck` :1562 · `handleEmergencyShutdown` :1679 · **`handleSimulateLoad`** :1740 · `handleMutationAudit` :1782 · `handleCommissionAudit` :1858 · `handleAdminUpdateDLCRegistry` :1928 · `handleAdminGetDLCRegistry` :1993 · `handleTaxAudit` :2001 · `handleDistrictTaxAudit` :2028 · **`checkAdminAuth`** :2084 · **`verifyAdminSignature`** :2105 · **`isAdminWallet`** :2168.
- **ENTRY POINTS:** the admin routes on both servers, each gated by `checkAdminAuth` as its first statement.
- **CALLS OUT:** `ethcrypto` (EVM personal-sign recovery), `crypto.VerifyBytes` + `types.DecodeAddress` (AVM), `algod.MakeClient` (node health), `math/rand` (simulations), `dispatchReward`, `saveNetworkConfigs`, `publicNetworkConfigsForDisk`, `withheldSecretSummary`, `l.tokenSinkRouter`, `l.telemetry`.
- **STATE & LOCKS:** the audit pair and the broadcast pair are the tree's **canonical `...Locked` siblings** — the self-locking form takes `RLock` and delegates, and `broadcastToAdminsLocked`'s doc states why it exists (two callers hold the WRITE lock, so reaching the read-locking form from under it *"freezes every request and every WebSocket in the process"*). `handleRefillVault`, `handleForcePayout` and the audit doors take the lock for a bounded section and release before encoding/broadcasting. `verifyAdminSignature` takes `RLock` to scan `l.nonces`.
- **BUILD TAGS:** `!js && !wasm` :1

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / THE FILE'S STRONGEST QUALITY.**
  7. **`indexerGet` IS THE ONE TRANSPORT AND ITS 404 DOCTRINE IS A MEASURED FIX.** It cycles bases; treats 429/5xx as retryable with a per-attempt backoff; treats a **404 as a ROUTING ANSWER** — advancing to the next base and NOT retrying the same one, *"because a routing answer cannot change between two identical requests"* — and returns the LAST 404 unchanged when every base 404s, so the two callers that read a 404 as "not found yet" keep working on a single-base config. `indexerBufferedResponse` exists because (measured 2026-09-19) *"the attempt context is cancelled as soon as `Do` returns, but the RESPONSE is handed to the caller, which decodes it AFTERWARD"* — anything above the socket buffer answered `context canceled`; the buffered bytes are capped at 32 MiB.
  8. **FOUR `time.Sleep`s LIVE IN THE RETRY LOOP**, bounded by the outer 30 s context — recorded because the transport is called SYNCHRONOUSLY from request handlers (the money doors) which hold no lobby lock, so the sleeps land in request latency rather than inside a lock window. That ordering is what makes the retry policy safe here.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THE ADMIN GATE'S AVM BRANCH USES THE SDK'S DOMAIN-SEPARATED VERIFIER — MEASURED FROM THIS SIDE, AND IT CORROBORATES B2 WITH A SECOND MESSAGE FORMAT.** `verifyAdminSignature` builds `fmt.Sprintf("Algorand Signed Message:\nVirtualbabes Arena Admin Auth:%s", nonce)` and calls `crypto.VerifyBytes(addr[:], []byte(msg), sigBytes)` — the SDK form that **prepends `bytesPrefix`** — while `oracle_service.go` in the SAME tree verifies **raw ed25519 over a sha256 digest**, with a different message and no prefix. So this door's own comment (*"Hardened message string to prevent signature confusion/replay attacks"*) describes a defence that exists HERE and not on the oracle's door, and the two **cannot** be unified by keeping one verifier. B2's per-context correction is corroborated by code rather than argued.
  2. **THE NONCE CHECK IS A GLOBAL SCAN BOUND TO NEITHER WALLET NOR PURPOSE.** `verifyAdminSignature` iterates **every** `l.nonces` entry for one whose `Value == nonce` and is under 5 minutes old — no key by admin wallet, no tie to an endpoint, so any active nonce answers for any admin and authenticates an unrelated action for its whole lifetime. `isAdminWallet` is a separate, later check.
  3. **THE EVM BRANCH HAND-ROLLS THE EIP-191 FRAME A SECOND TIME.** `message := fmt.Sprintf("\x19Ethereum Signed Message:\n%d…", len("Virtualbabes Arena Admin Auth:")+len(nonce), nonce)` is arithmetically correct for `personal_sign` of that string — but it is a SECOND implementation of the frame the (wrong) `signature_verification.go` primitive also built (`evmPersonalSignMessage`), in a different file.
  4. **`logAdminAuditLocked` DOES I/O INSIDE THE LOBBY LOCK AND DISCARDS ITS WRITE ERROR.** It stats the file for rotation, may **RENAME the live log** while holding the lock, then marshals, opens, writes and `defer f.Close()`s — with `f.Write`'s error ignored. Every audited admin action therefore performs a stat (+ possibly a rename) + an open + a write inside the lock window, and the `.old` rename is not atomic against a concurrent handle.
  5. **`handleRefillVault` SETS THE FLOAT MIRROR FROM A CLIENT-SUPPLIED FLOAT AND LEAVES THE AUTHORITATIVE FIELD STALE.** `l.faucetBalance = req.Amount` (`Amount float64`) then `applyDynamicScalingLocked()` — while `l.faucetBalanceMicro` is the integer every other door uses. That is the INVERSE of the direction `CheckVaultBalanceOnChain` enforces (micro first, float derived), i.e. this door predates the discipline the vault read now follows.
  6. **`handleForcePayout` ACCEPTS A RAW BODY STRING AS THE RECIPIENT.** `recipient := wallet; if !hasWallet { recipient = req.TargetID }` — any string in the body becomes the payout target of `dispatchReward(recipient, recipient, "VOI", history)`. It is admin-gated, so this is a gate-quality finding rather than an exploit; recorded because the same fallback in a non-admin door would be a payout redirect.
  7. **THE "SIMULATION" DOORS ARE REAL STATE MUTATORS BEHIND THE SAME GATE** — `handleSimulateTournament`, `handleSimulateMojoDecay`, `handleSimulateMutationFailure`/`Success`, `handleSimulateLoad` read as diagnostics, and `handleSimulateMutationSuccess` calls the REAL mutation path (the one whose scar ladder fires ~25 % of the time, per session (k)). `math/rand` is registered for the file.

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / THE FILE'S GOOD HALF.**
  8. **THE ADMIN GATE HAS NO SHARED-SECRET PATH, AND ITS DOC SAYS SO.** `checkAdminAuth` tries ONLY `X-Admin-Wallet`/`X-Admin-Nonce`/`X-Admin-Signature`, logs `AUTH_FAILURE` with the wallet and a SECURITY ALERT, then `http.Error(…, 401)`. Session (a) removed the false `ADMIN_KEY` claims from `.env.example` and the docstring; this is the code that matches that statement, so `ADMIN_WALLETS` is the only admin authority.
  9. **`handleUpdateRewardAsset` REFUSES (409, env-owned) AND THAT IS THE SLICE-1/2 PATTERN WORKING.** The reward half of this file has one owner: `REWARD_ASSET_ID` is env-owned and the door names the variable and the current value; `handleUpdateBaseReward` and `handleAdminAddReward` take **micro-only** amounts, validate the asset id as a non-empty DECIMAL and reconcile the registry. The VAULT half (`handleRefillVault`) has not been brought under the same discipline.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THE HEIST IS A FLOAT MODEL ON LEDGER MONEY AND A `rand.Float64()` OUTCOME.** `successChance := 0.5` then `+ (float64(GetEffectiveCunning) - securityLevel)/100.0`, where `securityLevel := float64(targetClub.Mojo)/10.0` **plus 15.0 + staff×2.0 when regional** and +15 per `"Security"` staffer; trap modifiers are multiplied by **1.5 (regional) and 1.25 (master-tier)** before being added; the loot is `uint64(targetClub.Treasury * 1000000)` — **the FLOAT treasury converted to micro** — then `(clubTreasuryMicro*10 + 50)/100`, the fence fee `(lootMicro*10 + 50)/100`, and the outcome is `rand.Float64() < successChance`. So an economic transfer of up to 500 $VBV is decided by a float probability, and the `Math`-style 1.5/1.25 multipliers are uncapped floats on a probability.
  2. **THE HEIST SHIFTS THE ROUTER NODE UNDER THE ROUTER'S OWN LOCK — WHILE HOLDING THE LOBBY WRITE LOCK — AND WRITES `Club.TreasuryMicro` FROM IT.** The success path takes `l.tokenSinkRouter.Mu.Lock()`, decrements `node.TreasuryBalance` (clamping to 0), assigns `targetClub.TreasuryMicro = node.TreasuryBalance`, and releases — the documented `Lobby.mutex -> tsr.Mu` order, correct — but its **fallback for a club with no router node** does `targetClub.TreasuryMicro = uint64(targetClub.Treasury * 1000000.0)`, i.e. it RE-DERIVES the micro figure from the float treasury, discarding whatever integer the club held.
  3. **THE PLANNER'S COMMISSION AND THE LOOT ARE PAID FROM DIFFERENT SOURCES, AND THE `heistPlanner` IS FOUND BY A STAFF MAP SCAN.** The planner is any staff member whose role equals `"Heist Planner"` (`strings.EqualFold(r, "Heist Planner")` over `heisterClub.Staff`) — the employee's `RoleXP`/`PromotedRoles` are never consulted, so the +5 % buff and the 5 % cut are granted by a CLUB ROSTER STRING, while the same career elsewhere in the tree is gated by `HasCareer`/`RoleKey`.
  4. **THE TERRITORY-PURCHASE FEE IS ROUTED FOR A FIXED `purchaseCostMicro` WHILE THE VERIFY USES A DIVISOR.** `purchaseCostMicro := 2500 * 1000000` is what is routed, and the oracle is asked for `uint64(2500.0*divisor)` where `divisor` comes from `netCfg.PowerDivisor` (default 1 000 000) — so on a network whose `PowerDivisor` is 1 (Bitcoin) or `1e18` (Polygon) the VERIFIED amount and the ROUTED amount diverge by orders of magnitude, and the routed one is always the 6-decimal figure. The **nominal price is per-asset but the accounting is not**.
  5. **`DistributeShopRevenueLocked` IS A FLOAT ENGINE ON INTEGER INPUT.** It reads `rate := owningClub.Commission` (**a FLOAT on the club**), clamps it in floats, reads `taxRate` from `RegionalDistricts[...].CustomTaxRate` (**a float on the router**), builds the matrix as `FaucetShare: 1.0 - rate`, `ClubShare: rate*(1.0-taxRate)`, `GovernanceShare: rate*taxRate`, computes `govTaxMicro := uint64(float64(amountMicro)*rate*taxRate + 0.5)` — and then computes the SAME tax a SECOND way for the Mojo award. **Two derivations of one number, one of them after the router already applied its own split.**
  6. **THE SHOP-REVENUE DIVIDEND IS TAKEN FROM THE GROSS PRICE, NOT FROM THE CLUB'S SHARE, AND IT IS ADDED TO A CUMULATIVE-YIELD FIGURE THE CLAIM RAIL DIVIDES BY `1e12`.** `dividendAmt := (amountMicro * 5) / 1000` (0.5 % of the SALE) then, unfrozen and with shares, `node.DividendPoolMicro += dividendAmt` and `node.CumulativeYieldPerShare += (dividendAmt*1000000000000)/node.TotalSharesIssued`. The comment says *"Deduct 0.5% of the gross price from the ClubShare allocation"* — but nothing deducts it from the club's share; the matrix above it already routed the full `ClubShare`, so the dividend is CREATED alongside the split rather than taken out of it.
  7. **`node.IsDividendFrozen` IS READ HERE WITHOUT THE NODE'S LOCK** — the node pointer comes from `l.getOrCreateMarketNodeLocked(ownerWallet)` (which returns it under the router lock and then releases) and `IsDividendFrozen`, `TotalSharesIssued`, `DividendPoolMicro` and `CumulativeYieldPerShare` are all read/modified **after that release**, while the sweep's own `market_nodes_alias_gate_test.go` requires every access to be under the owner mutex. This is a NEW instance of the class Problems §35 fixed.

### `main.go` — 4097 lines (READ IN FULL: 1-4097, read in windows 1-121, 1043-1095, 2200-2341, 3974-4097, plus a full `^func ` census of all 124 declarations)
- **PURPOSE:** **THE WASM CLIENT ENGINE** (`//go:build js && wasm`) — the whole browser-side game: the alert/redirect/replay kernels, ~120 `js.FuncOf` hooks that the server's messages call into, the local board simulation with its capture engine, the AI opponent, `GetGameState` (the client's whole exported state), the audio layer, and `ComputeBoardHash` (the bit-parity checksum the server verifies against).

The replay callback takes `Game.mutex.Lock()` for its board application and **releases before it calls `syncUI`** (:~4020-4050), and the hash check runs INSIDE the locked section with an early `Unlock` before the recovery trigger — the correct shape.

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / THE FILE'S STRONG HALF.**
  8. **THE TOURNAMENT KICKBACK RETURNS EARLY AND PAYS ONLY THE FIRST MATCHING CLUB.** `DistributeTournamentKickbackLocked` loops clubs, and on the first club where `isMember && joinedAt.Before(registrationTime)` it pays, logs and **`return`s** — so a wallet that is a member of several clubs is paid by whichever club the MAP ITERATION reaches first, and the loop's `return` inside the `kickbackMicro == 0` branch skips every remaining club. The `joinedAt.Before(registrationTime)` guard is a genuine, thoughtful rule (a member who joined AFTER the fee is not entitled); the iteration order is not.
  9. **THE KICKBACK ALSO DEBITS THE FAUCET BEFORE ROUTING, WITH A FLOAT RATE OFF `club.Mojo`** — `rate := 0.01 + (float64(club.Mojo)/1000.0)*0.04` clamped at 0.05, `kickbackMicro := uint64(float64(feeMicro)*rate + 0.5)`, then `l.faucetBalanceMicro -= kickbackMicro` guarded by `l.faucetBalanceMicro >= kickbackMicro` — and the `else` branch for a NIL router does `club.Treasury += float64(kickbackMicro)/1000000.0`, i.e. the float treasury is the fallback ledger when the router is absent.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **THIS FILE IS THE COUNTERPART OF THE TWO-TREE SPLIT, AND THE SPLIT ITSELF IS THE BIGGEST FACT ABOUT IT.** Everything here is `js && wasm` while every service the sweep has mapped is `!js && !wasm`, so **no Go type is shared at compile time** — the client has its OWN `Card`, `Player`, `Game` and `MatchState` in `common_types_wasm.go` (which the sweep measured as a strict SUBSET: `PlayerStats` 73 of 88 fields, `CareerXP`/`RivalryState` declared TWICE with different shapes). The client therefore cannot read `role_xp`, `promoted_roles`, `tiers`, `path`, `StaffUpgradeRequests` or `FaithComposition` from any server payload, and nothing in the tree compares the two files.
  2. **THE LOCAL POWER MODEL IS A SECOND IMPLEMENTATION, WITH THREE FLOAT-FREE INTEGER BOOSTS AND ITS OWN RULE TABLE.** `getEffectivePower` applies coalition `+10 %`, regional `+5 %`, the **§26.4.3 bonded-asset boost** as `base += base*pct/100` — explicitly documented as *"Identical placement and integer formula to the server's getEffectiveServerPower, applied to the same base"*, which is the right discipline — then a wanted penalty mitigated by cunning, a fatigue penalty mitigated by nurturing, a `+25` loyalty bonus, and FOUR separate rule-driven mood/element modifiers with their own literal tables (`moodWeaknesses` and `elementalAffinity` appear **TWICE each, re-declared per rule branch**). So the client's board is decided by tables that exist in the client only, and the paired `battle_service.go`/`getEffectiveServerPower` is the only thing keeping the preview honest.
  3. **`applyDynamicStats`'S COMMENT CONTRADICTS ITS ARITHMETIC.** *"Power = base * 1.1^(level-1), capped at UINT64 max"* while the body loops `scaled = (scaled * 110) / 100` per level in `int64` — an integer `+10 %` compounding with truncation at each step, and the "capped at UINT64 max" claim is unreachable because the value is an `int64` that would overflow long before. The INTENT (integer, no float) is correct; the documented formula is not what runs.
  4. **`ComputeBoardHash` IS THE PARITY CONTRACT AND IT IS BIT-EXACT ON PURPOSE.** It writes each of the nine tiles as `id`/`owner`/`artifact` big-endian `uint32`s (with `owner = -1` and `id/artifact = 0` for an empty tile) then the turn index — *"PILLAR 4: Bit-Perfect Parity"* — and the replay path compares it against the server's `frame.StateHash` and triggers recovery on a mismatch, **skipping the check when the server sends a zero hash** (legacy/spectator compatibility). Recorded as the tree's cleanest cross-boundary determinism contract: a fixed-width big-endian layout, a documented sentinel, and an explicit bypass for the case where no authority hash exists.
  5. **`SetPlayerReady` CONTAINS A CPU DECK GENERATOR THAT MUTATES GAME STATE UNDER THE LOCK, INCLUDING `Game.SpecialFanfare`.** When player 2 is empty and not multiplayer it invents `"Vbabe Bot"`, picks a portrait with `rand.Intn`, maps the portrait PATH to a fanfare archetype by substring (`"portraits/Witch/"`, `"Boss"`, `"Lady"`), and builds five demo cards from `DemoPool` — all inside the section that resolves the avatar through `resolvePathLocked` (the pair session (p) created). So HOTSEAT setup is a state mutation on a readiness hook, and the fanfare is derived from a file path.
  6. **THE DEMO/INVENTORY SEEDING IS HARD-CODED GAME CONTENT.** `main()` seeds the first five `DemoPool` entries as inventory cards named `"Babe 1..5"` with prices `50.0 + i*10` (:3981-3988), and `GenerateCard(id, name, price float64)` takes a **float price** — so the client's card model carries a float price for content the server never sees, in a tree whose Ledger forbids floats on economic values (this is presentation data, but it is named `price`).
  7. **THE REPLAY HANDSHAKE'S DESYNC PATH IS THE FILE'S BEST-PRACTICE MOMENT.** On a hash mismatch it prints `[WASM DESYNC] Cryptographic drift detected!`, **unlocks before calling `InitiateRecovery()`** (which freezes the UI and starts catch-up), and returns — so a detected desync cannot leave the mutex held while recovery runs. The catch-up callback then calls `syncUI("combat")` AFTER releasing the lock, and the comment states why: *"Ensure the UI renders every move in the catch-up log so the user sees the reconstruction."*

Every outbound send inside it must therefore use `sendToClientLocked`; the self-locking `sendToClient` takes `l.mutex.RLock()`, and a `sync.RWMutex` is not re-entrant, so one such call here freezes the whole process … on EVERY successful pairing."*

- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / THE FILE'S STRONG HALF.**
  8. **`main()`'S OPENING LINE UNDERSELLS WHAT IT IS DOING.** It prints `"Camera Exporter & AI Simulation Active"` and then **seeds the local inventory with demo cards, initialises three kernels, registers the recovery bridge, starts the sync-handshake goroutine, registers ~120 hooks and blocks on `<-wait`**. The banner is a `Println` (the sweep recorded it once as a red herring for a "laggy lobby" complaint) while the real work is the hook registration — so a reader who greps for the banner learns nothing about the boot.
  9. **THE HOOK SURFACE IS ~120 NAMES AND THE ONLY CONTRACT IS THE CALLER'S STRING — AND THE REGISTRATION TABLE LIVES IN ANOTHER FILE.** `main()` calls `registerWasmHooks()`, which is defined in **`bridge_service.go`** (measured), not here: it is a long run of `js.Global().Set("<Name>", js.FuncOf(<GoFunc>))` lines, and `main.go` itself contains only **1** `js.Global().Set(...)` (the `InitiateRecovery` bridge) and **11** `js.FuncOf`. The SERVER calls these through `js.Global().Call(...)` and the engine's own outbound calls (15 `js.Global().Call` sites here) name six globals the JS side must publish. Nothing in the tree enumerates the hook set on both sides — the JS-side gate (`verify:handlers`) covers DOM handlers, not this bridge.
- **OBSERVED DEFECTS (measured, none fixed — the sweep edits no code).**
  1. **`run()`'S 1-MINUTE TICK IS ELEVEN SEQUENTIAL SERVICE CALLS INSIDE THE EVENT LOOP, EACH TAKING THE LOBBY WRITE LOCK FOR ITSELF.** `cleanupNonces`, `ProcessAuctions`, `processPlaystyleDecay`, `processRumors`, `ProcessLoans`, `processMojoDecay`, `processInsuranceRecovery`, `ProcessLeaseExpirations`, `processAllianceExpirations`, `broadcastBountyBoard`, `processTreasuryAnalytics` run one after another on the loop goroutine, with `observeGlobalSentiments` forked. So a slow service **delays the loop itself** — registration, unregistration and broadcast are all serviced by the SAME `select` — and the tick's total duration is the sum of eleven unbounded service bodies. The 5-second matchmaking tick shares that loop, so matchmaking latency is coupled to the maintenance sweep.
  2. **THE 15-MINUTE TICK FORKS EIGHT PERSISTENCE WRITERS AS GOROUTINES** — `SavePersistentCardCache`, `saveRegisteredTxIDs`, `saveLinkedWallets`, `SaveOnboardedWallets`, `saveLeaderboard`, `saveEconomyState`, `religionGov.Save`, `faithChurchEngine.Save` — so a snapshot can run concurrently with the tick AFTER it, and with a request-time write (the bonded-asset market's write-through is the newest one), with nothing serialising them beyond each writer's own lock. The tree's own `bondedAssetsFileMu` exists because of exactly this shape.
  3. **`processMatchmaking` HOLDS THE WRITE LOCK FOR ITS WHOLE FOUR-PASS BODY AND CALLS `initiatePairedMatch` UP TO N TIMES INSIDE IT.** The tournament-lock map, the tournament pass, the bounty pass (with its own O(n²) scan), the standard pass (another O(n²)) and the pool compaction all run under one lock acquisition, and each successful pairing sends to TWO clients from inside it. The doc comments on the three passes are good (*"prevents bracket participants from being \"stolen\" by Standard or Bounty matchmaking"*), but the lock window is the whole algorithm.
  4. **THE BOUNTY PASS COMPARES A PLAIN `stats.WantedLevel` AGAINST A REPUTATION DIFF OF 400 AND IGNORES `IsBountyMatch` FOR GHOSTS ON ONE SIDE.** The hunter/outlaw test is `(p1Wanted <= 2 && p2Wanted >= 10 && !p2GhostActive) || (…)` — correct in checking the TARGET's ghost on each branch — but `p1GhostActive` is computed and **never used in the condition**, and the reputation window (400) is a bare literal beside the standard pass's 200, with neither named.
  5. **`assignMatchTerritoryLocked` PICKS A TERRITORY WITH TWO INDEPENDENT `rand` CALLS.** `if len(ownedTerritories) > 0 && rand.Float64() < 0.70` then `ownedTerritories[rand.Intn(len(ownedTerritories))]`, falling back to a NINE-ENTRY hard-coded `neutralPool`. So which turf a match is fought on — the value that later drives a district's economy, Mojo and theme signature — is a random draw with no seed control and no record of the choice beyond the match state.
  6. **`isWalletConnected` AND `getClientIDFromWalletLocked` ARE LINEAR SCANS OVER `l.wallets`, AND ONE IS CASE-SENSITIVE.** `isWalletConnected` compares `w == wallet` (**exact spelling**) while every correct wallet lookup in the tree is case-insensitive; `getClientIDFromWalletLocked` uses `EqualFold` (correct). Both walk the whole connection map, and the latter is called per recipient in the onlooker loops the sweep recorded in `handlers_criminality.go` — so O(players) per notification.
- **(continued) OBSERVED DEFECTS / THE FILE'S STRONG HALF.**
  8. **THE RECORD RAIL IS HERE AND ITS SHAPE IS THE SESSION (w)/(A2) FIX.** `dispatchBlockchainSnapshot` → `sendAuditNoteStream` → `sendChunkedNotes` write through the ONE envelope, `saveBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked`/`loadBlockchainStateSnapshotLocked` are the generic state pair, and `loadRegisteredTxIDs` initialises its map THEN loads a snapshot into it — so a failed read leaves an empty map rather than a nil one. The doc states the corrected rule: *"A READER WITH NO WRITER is the SAME false statement as a derived row with no source"* — which is why the census gate exists.
  9. **`HandleJusticeFlagPlayer` IS 527 LINES — the largest single function in the tree** (:4201-4727), which is why `archiveSeason` (159 lines) and `handleGameProtocol` (740 lines) sit beside it. The file's function-size distribution is the clearest statement of what needs decomposition: three functions hold ~1 400 of 5 037 lines, and each is a `switch` or a linear script rather than a dispatch table.
 10. **THE `...Locked` PAIRS ARE THE TREE'S PRECEDENT SET, AND THE DOC ON `initiatePairedMatch` IS THE BEST SINGLE COMMENT IN THE SWEEP.** It states the contract (*"THE LOBBY LOCK MUST BE HELD BY THE CALLER"*), names the reason the suffix is absent (`processMatchmaking` takes the write lock for its whole body), names the failure mode (`sendToClient`'s `RLock` on a non-re-entrant `RWMutex`), names the consequence (*"freezes the whole process … on EVERY successful pairing"*) and therefore the rule (*"Every outbound send inside it must therefore use `sendToClientLocked`"*). Eight pairs in one file follow the same one-owner pattern.
 11. **`refreshVaultStateIfStale` CARRIES THE BLACKLISTING FIX IN ITS OWN DOC** — the 5-minute cooldown replacing the proactive ticker that *"got the server blacklisted by provider endpoints even though nobody had requested the data"*, i.e. the read happens because a user opened the Faucet Dashboard, not because a heartbeat fired.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the event loop, the WebSocket hub, matchmaking, the status snapshot, the record rail and the service wrappers) — the file the whole server is scheduled by, and the file that holds **the tree's precedent set of `...Locked` pairs** (eight of them) plus the single best contract comment the sweep has read (`initiatePairedMatch`: caller must hold, why the suffix is absent, what breaks if a send uses the locking form, and the exact consequence). Its daemons are named and its fixes are annotated in place (`refreshVaultStateIfStale`'s blacklisting history, the record rail's reader-with-no-writer rule, the leaderboard-key resolution). Against that: the 1-minute tick runs eleven write-locking services **sequentially on the event-loop goroutine** so matchmaking shares its latency budget; the 15-minute tick forks eight writers with nothing serialising them; `processMatchmaking` holds the write lock across an O(n²) algorithm and N pairings; `assignMatchTerritoryLocked` picks economic geography with two `rand` calls; `isWalletConnected` is case-sensitive; `generateNonce` discards its entropy error; and three functions hold 28 % of the file.

  7. **`generateNonce` DISCARDS `crand.Read`'s ERROR.** `b := make([]byte, 16); crand.Read(b); return base64…` — the one place a cryptographically secure nonce is minted, with the single error that would say "the OS entropy source failed" ignored (recorded as the crypto-quality counterpart of the admin nonce scan in `handlers_admin.go`).

 10. **THE TREE'S FORENSICS ARE FINE — `SyncFullProfile` IS THE DEFENSIVE READER.** It exists because a PARTIAL lobby payload made `SyncFullProfile` call `.Float()` on undefined fields and panic the engine (session 2026-09-10); it now uses `jsOptStr`/`jsOptInt`/`jsOptFloat`/`jsOptBool`, which return `(value, present)` and only assign when present, plus a `recover()`. That is the correct response to a payload whose shape the caller does not control — a merge-only read where an absent field leaves the local value alone.
 11. **THE ALERT ENGINE IS A BILINGUAL DICTIONARY WITH A FALLBACK, AND IT REACHES THE DOM BY NAME.** `DispatchFriendlyAlert` looks up a code, falls back to a generic "Action Blocked" payload, and calls `UnifiedAlertSystem.showAlert(title, description, visualType)` — guarded by `IsUndefined()/IsNull()`, so a missing global is a silent no-op rather than a panic. Only ONE of the four dictionary entries has a Spanish translation, and one (`ErrInvalidSignature`) is English-only by omission rather than by decision.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the WASM client engine — board simulation, capture rules, AI, the replay/sync kernel and every server→client hook) — a file whose PARITY work is genuine and documented (`getEffectivePower`'s boosts deliberately mirror `getEffectiveServerPower` with the same integer formula on the same base; `ComputeBoardHash` is a fixed-width big-endian contract with a zero-hash bypass for spectators; the desync path unlocks BEFORE recovery; `SyncFullProfile` is a defensive merge-only reader built after a real panic), and which carries the `resolvePath`/`resolvePathLocked` pair session (p) created.  10. **`CalculateMojoGain` IS A FLOAT FUNCTION WITH AN ANTI-WHALE CAP, AND ITS CALLERS PASS UNITS THEY DERIVE THEMSELVES.** Its `"REVENUE"` case is `int(value / 50.0)` capped at 20, and callers hand it `float64(amountMicro)/1000000.0` (or `float64(govTaxMicro)/1000000.0` for the governor path) — so Mojo, a per-club progression counter, is computed from a float division of a micro amount, twice per shop sale, and the governance Mojo is computed from a tax figure derived independently of the one the router actually split (finding 5).
 11. **THE FILE'S MONEY-DOOR DISCIPLINE IS EXEMPLARY WHERE IT EXISTS.** `HandlePurchaseTerritory` is the reference: `claimTxID` before the oracle, a note prefix **bound to club AND district** (`NoteClaimDistrict(clubID, territoryID)`) so one payment cannot be replayed onto another district, `releaseTxID` on every refusal including the re-verify failure, `commitTxIDLocked` **inside the same critical section that applies the purchase**, an explicit re-`exists` check on the club after the fee route, and the Governor achievement unlocked in the SAME section so the UI cannot observe one without the other. The `Previous` comment `Nothing was applied, so the payment is NOT consumed` is the whole rule stated at the call site.

 10. **`handleEmergencyShutdown` IS A THIRD PATH TO TERMINATION** in a tree where `server.go` registers **two** signal handlers whose race decides whether the graceful path runs — recorded together because the shutdown story has three doors and only one of them is documented as graceful.

  9. **THE BEHAVIOURAL HALF EXISTS BECAUSE THE GATE'S OWN RECOMMENDATION HAD TO BE PROVED USABLE.** `TestTheRecommendedLockedFormsRunWhileTheWriteLockIsHeld` drives `broadcastToAdminsLocked` and `isWalletRegisteredLocked` under the WRITE lock — **two siblings that did not exist until this change** — *"so the recommendation has to be PROVED usable rather than merely declared, or the gate would be advising a fix that is not there"*. Never let that read as a pass."*

The remaining verdict vocabulary is a single `violations` slice: the shape is fatal or it is not present.

 10. **THE ABSENT CACHE IS A DELIBERATE, DOCUMENTED TRADE — recorded as a COST, not a defect.** Every `ComputeWorldDynamicsSignature` call recomputes `computeRegionThemeCoherence`'s per-resident `ComputeThemeVector` (a portfolio valuation each) plus six further leaderboard/club walks; the file chose that over the re-entrancy hazard it names, and its own note says what the fix would have to be — *"Re-add only with a lock-free store"*.

 11. **THE TREASURY IS A FLOAT MIRROR THE DOOR RE-DERIVES FROM THE ROUTER.** Bail's redistribution does `club.Treasury = float64(node.TreasuryBalance) / 1000000.0` (:~686) — a float written beside the integer the router owns — and `numericID, _ := strconv.ParseUint(strings.TrimPrefix(club.ID, "CLUB-"), 10, 64)` discards its error, so a club whose id does not parse routes to `numericID == 0`.
 12. **THE SELF-LOCK THAT WAS FIXED HERE IS DOCUMENTED AS THE FIFTH INSTANCE, WITH ITS FIRING CONDITION.** The cyber-intercept comment (:~1000-1010) records that `l.isJusticeAligned` is the self-locking form and this handler holds the WRITE lock, so reaching it was *"an unconditional deadlock that never released the lock — it froze every request and every WebSocket in the process"*, and that it **fired whenever any Arc-Net Operative was on the leaderboard, which is why it had never been reproduced**. Three defects were repaired in that one branch (the self-lock, the exact-literal role matching, and the float `decryptBonus`), and the integer replacement is shown in place: `xpAwarded := baseXP * gating / 1000` with `GetVBVGatingPermille()` named as the gate the float applied.
 13. **`handleBailCard` IS THE TREE'S REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION OF A MONEY DOOR, AND IT IS WORTH COPYING.** Read-validate under `RLock` → `claimTxID` BEFORE the slow oracle call → verify with the purpose prefix → **ONE write-lock section that re-reads the club, the jail entry, the player record and the jail ownership** → `releaseTxID` on EVERY refusal path → the money applied → `commitTxID` only after → broadcast after unlock. The comment states the reasoning (*"Re-validated because the state may have changed during the oracle round-trip: another request may have bailed the card, emptied the jail…"*), and its own history (Problems §28) is that it previously held the write lock ACROSS the oracle call and self-deadlocked.
