 15. **THE HOSTAGE REGISTRY IS ITS OWN MUTEX AND IS USED CORRECTLY.** `VictimRegistry.Mu` is taken for the register and for each cleanup, `RegisterKidnap` *cleans an expired slot before refusing*, and the top-level map entry is deleted when the last attacker leaves (:~40-52, :~500-510, :~745). It is the one sub-structure in this file with a clean lock-and-cleanup story.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the hostage/ransom/bail/raid economy and of the Justice onlooker hooks it feeds) — a file where the MONEY DOOR is the tree's reference shape (txid reserved before the oracle, one re-validating critical section, commit only after the money moved) and where one branch carries a complete, honest repair note for the fifth self-deadlock; and where, around that, the XP every one of these events awards is multiplied by a gate that reads a PERMANENTLY-FALSE value, the onlooker awards are five full-leaderboard sweeps per event, the raid's 5 000 $VBV is minted and its outcome is a `rand.Float64()` roll that charges the loser, and `CareerHasRole` is trusted in six places by a file that documents two paragraphs earlier why it cannot be.

  1. **THE GATE CARRIES A LIVENESS PROBE INSIDE ITSELF, AND ITS REASON IS A MEASURED FAILURE.** `TestNoSameMutexRelock` builds a synthetic `mustBeSeen` that takes the same mutex twice and **FAILS if the detector reports nothing for it** — *"THE DETECTOR IS BLIND: … A gate whose matcher silently failed reports a clean repository for ever"* (:700-716). The comment states plainly that this is not hypothetical: **the first version of this detector reported 0 for EVERY input** because `mutexTarget` matched the METHOD name instead of the RECEIVER, and only the control caught it (Problems §32).
  2. **PASS 2 HAS ITS OWN ANTI-VACUITY GUARD, ALSO FROM THAT FAILURE.** `methodTakes == 0` is fatal, because *"a clean edge report would be the report of a blind pass rather than of a clean tree"* (:804-810). So both passes refuse to answer at all if their registry is empty.
  3. **BOTH BASELINES ARE EMPTY AND FAIL IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** `sameMutexRelockBaseline` and `sameMutexRelockViaCallBaseline` are `map[string]string{}` (:668, :676), keys are **"Type.Method  expr" WITHOUT line numbers** *"so an unrelated edit elsewhere cannot invalidate a line and report a false STALE"* — with the line numbers travelling in the failure message instead. A NEW re-lock fails, and a baselined one that is gone ALSO fails, because *"a baseline that outlives its reason reads like a clean repository while legitimising rot"*.
  4. **THE TWO CONTROLS ARE THE STRONGEST IN THE TREE, AND THEY CARRY THE REAL SHAPES.** Pass 1: **15 functions, 5 must report, 10 must not** — and the five are the shapes that matter: write-then-read (the exact door shape), **recursive read** (the class session (m) turned from a "note" into a failure), read-then-WRITE escalation, held on BOTH branches, and re-lock INSIDE a loop; the ten include a proper release/re-acquire, two DIFFERENT objects (*"the EXPRESSION is the identity, not the field name"*), a goroutine literal, a `Lock()` method on a type the derivation did NOT classify as a mutex, and **a mutex held on one branch only — explicitly named as this gate's documented false-NEGATIVE direction**. Pass 2: 6 shapes, 2 must report (via a method and via a read lock) and 4 must not (a different object, a `go` callee, a call AFTER release, and a callee taking a DIFFERENT field), **plus a check that EVERY pass-2 finding names a witness edge** and that ambiguity is NOT over-eager (the control's names are unambiguous by construction, so `ambiguous != 0` is an error).
  5. **THE TYPE RULE IS PINNED SEPARATELY** (`TestSyncMutexTypeRuleIsNotOverBroad`, :~915): `sync.RWMutex`, `sync.Mutex` and `*sync.Mutex` must match; `sync.Map`, a plain int, another package's `RWMutex` and a channel must not. A matcher that accepted `sync.Map` would report a mutex that is not one.
  6. **THE DERIVATION IS CHECKED AGAINST THE TWO FIELDS THE LIVE DOORS USED** (`TestMutexRelockDerivationSeesTheTwoLiveDoors`): it must see `Mu` (EntityMarketNode's) and `mutex` (Lobby's) *"or it cannot see the shape this gate exists for"*, and it prints the whole derived name list every run.
  7. **THE PASS-2 REGISTRY EXCLUDES `*Lobby` BY NAME AND SAYS WHY** (:490-494) — `if recvType == "" || recvType == "Lobby" { continue }` — so the same-mutex class on the Lobby is owned solely by `selflock_gate_test.go`. That is the ONE-OWNER rule applied to gates, and it is why the two gates can both be clean without either being blind.
  8. **A NESTED MUTEX PATH IS NOT MODELLED.** `if field == "" || strings.Contains(field, ".") { continue }` (:526-528) — *"`x.Mu` is the shape this pass models; a nested path is not guessed at"* — so `x.inner.mu` acquired twice by one body is invisible to pass 2's registry (pass 1 renders such a path fine, because its key is any renderable expression). The conservative direction, stated.
  9. **THE RESIDUAL FALSE-NEGATIVE SET IS NAMED RATHER THAN IMPLIED:** a re-lock on a branch-only-held mutex (pinned by `fineOneBranchOnly`); a chain deeper than one edge (`DEPTH 1 ONLY` — *"the next step if a measured instance ever appears"*); a `defer`red callee and a call in a `range` expression; and an unrenderable receiver expression (skipped, never guessed).
 10. **HONEST CONTEXT: THE HOST CANNOT RUN THE RACE DETECTOR.** The header states `go test -race` needs cgo and this machine has no C toolchain, so nothing in this file is a race proof — it is a census of a shape that cannot return. Recorded because the distinction is the difference between "this code deadlocks" and "this code might race", and the tree has both classes with separate gates.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the direct same-mutex re-lock class, on ANY receiver type, direct and one edge deep) — a gate with TWO negative controls carrying the real door shapes, an in-gate liveness probe born from a measured total failure of its own first version, empty baselines that fail on NEW and STALE with keys deliberately free of line numbers, a pass-2 anti-vacuity guard, a separately pinned type rule, a derivation check naming the two fields the live incidents used, and a `*Lobby` exclusion that keeps each shape owned by exactly one gate. Every limit it has is written into it, and two of those limits (branch-only-held, depth 1) are pinned as MUST-NOT-REPORT shapes rather than left to a reader's imagination.

  9. **`playerStatsToID` IS DEAD, SELF-DESCRIBED SCAFFOLD** (:706-710): *"This is a placeholder - in production this would resolve via the lobby's wallet-to-ID map"* — it returns `stats.ID` and takes the struct **BY VALUE**, the copylock shape `go vet` flags elsewhere.
 10. **THE FILE'S OWN HISTORY IS ITS STRONGEST QUALITY, AND IT IS ALL IN PLACE.** The header (:30-46) states the two competing spellings at ~50 call sites, that `GetRivalPairName` compared with `==` so the other spelling answered `""` — *"not an error, not a log entry and not an XP award"* — and that `RoleXP` is keyed by whatever the WRITER passed. `roleAliases` declares **THREE genuine word-differences with the file that proves each** (`intagent→intelagent`, `mutationauditor→mutationlogauditor`, `aos→aosleader`) while the fold makes separators and casing free; `rivalPairTable` carries **FIVE inline tombstones** naming the removed duplicate rows and the direction that could never fire; `ResolveRivalXPAward`'s comment records the four stacked defects (six sites reading the defender's 30 % share, the underflow to 18 446 744 073 709 551 605, the float mult) and the STRUCT response; and every permille owner names the float expression it replaced as *"the same number before the division"*.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the career vocabulary, the tier gates, the rival matrix and every integer XP composition) — the file that made ONE integer owner REAL: `math` removed, permille throughout, four float getters demoted to declared display wrappers, `GetVBVGatingPermille`/`GetRivalPairModifierPermille` owning their ladders, `RoleKey` folding ~50 call sites' spellings onto one key, and a struct return that makes the wrong-field bug impossible. The outstanding defects are all of ONE KIND — **the older float helpers kept their float shape and their drifted comments**: two of them document $VBV ladders their bodies cannot reach, one name exists on two receivers with different arithmetic, one pair is still priced in a direction the table calls unreachable, and one read-shaped helper writes the record.


  2. **CANONICALISATION IS THE WHOLE ANALYSIS, AND IT SAYS SO.** Each mutex identity is `"<StructType>.<Field>"` resolved THROUGH THE DECLARATIONS, because *"without it `ace.mu` and `l.aiEngine.mu` are two unrelated strings and the whole analysis is meaningless"* (:47-50). The control PINS that property: `fineOtherObject(other *loInner)` takes `other.mu` after `o.mutex` and must contribute the SAME identity as `o.inner.mu` — asserted by exact edge counts, not by inspection.
  3. **THE CONTROL'S COUNT ASSERTIONS ARE THE REAL PROOF, NOT ITS PASS/FAIL.** It requires exactly **1** `loOuter.mutex -> loInner.mu` edge (so the call-edge resolution is not blind) and exactly **2** reverse edges (`badIntra` + `fineOtherObject`) — *"a third would mean a shape that releases (or a goroutine literal) contributed an edge it must not"* — and exactly **1** inversion, named. A gate whose control only counts inversions would pass while its resolver returned nothing.
  4. **THE `...Locked` SEED IS A FIX THE GATE APPLIED TO ITSELF, AND THE REASON IS IN THE FILE.** A callee named `...Locked` takes no lock of its own BECAUSE the caller holds it, so its held set is taken from its CALL SITES (:636-641, :697-712, DEPTH 1, deterministic walk order). Session (q) recorded why: with both fixes applied, re-introducing the inversions reported **0** because the locked block had MOVED into a helper — *"a gate that stops seeing the code it was written for is worse than no gate"*.
  5. **FOUR FLOORS PREVENT A VACUOUS PASS.** The gate FAILS if the derivation finds zero mutex-typed fields, zero struct declarations, zero methods taking a mutex, **or zero order edges** (:809-822) — a distinct failure message per floor, so a broken derivation cannot read as a clean repository.
  6. **THE BASELINE IS EMPTY AND FAILS IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** A NEW inversion fails with a fix recipe attached (snapshot/release/take, or a `...Locked` form, or a baseline entry WITH its reason), and a baselined inversion that no longer exists ALSO fails (:830-845). An EMPTY baseline is the strongest possible statement: there is nothing to hide behind.
  7. **A REGRESSION PIN NAMES THE TWO ORIGINAL PAIRS WITH THEIR MECHANISMS** (:849-866) — so even if both directions were re-introduced in a way the inversion detector wrapped differently, the named pairs fail with the daemons and doors that made them live.
  8. **THE LIMIT THE FILE STATES PLAINLY, AND ONE THE SWEEP ADDS.** Stated: ONE CALL EDGE (a callee's acquired receiver fields resolved against the CALL-SITE receiver expression; a name declared by two receivers with different takes is **SKIPPED, never guessed**; a method with no take is not an edge); "anything whose type cannot be resolved from a receiver/param/local declaration WITHOUT GUESSING is SKIPPED AND COUNTED", with the census **printed on every run so the blind spot is visible instead of assumed away**; and it is *"an ORDER graph, not a proof of reachability"* (:52-60). Added by the sweep: a lock held through something OTHER than a receiver field — a mutex reached through an interface, a package-level mutex, or a mutex owned by a type this pass cannot resolve — is invisible, which is the same class as the sibling gates' `*Lobby`-only registry.
  9. **THE GATE MEASURES A SECOND SUBJECT FOR ITS SIBLING.** `loAnalysis` carries `accesses`, `aliasFields` and `aliasPairs` — the ALIASED-MAP subject (`market_nodes_alias_gate_test.go`'s) with the evidence being the alias assignment sites themselves (:86-91). So the two gates share a walker ON PURPOSE while keeping separate subjects, which is the ONE-OWNER pattern applied to analysis code.
 10. **`loSources` EXCLUDES `_test.go`** (:765-767) and fails if it finds nothing — so the control's synthetic file is the only way a `_test`-shaped inversion is ever measured, which is why the control exists rather than a second real-tree arm.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the cross-mutex lock-order class) — a DERIVED subject, canonicalised identities, an EMPTY baseline that fails on NEW and STALE, four anti-vacuity floors, a control that pins the resolver by exact edge counts, a self-applied `...Locked` seed with its own reason recorded, and the two real inversions documented with their daemons, doors and the queue-a-writer mechanism. Its limits are written into it, its blind-spot census is printed every run, and it is the precedent the sweep has used to judge every other gate.

  9. **FIVE CONCURRENT PAYOUT GOROUTINES EACH OPEN THEIR OWN ALGOD CLIENT AND RE-READ VAULT STATE** (:889-916) — `algod.MakeClient`, `SuggestedParams`, the mnemonic, and one box read per asset — with `wg.Wait()` then a single unlock, and **the `oracleSemaphore` the REGISTRATION path uses to bound indexer pressure is not applied here**.
 10. **`ProcessTournamentResult` WRITES BOTH PLAYERS' HISTORY FROM A COPY AND WRITES IT BACK TWICE** (:430-490) — `dup` guards make a re-report idempotent, which is correct, and the function relies on the caller's lock by contract; `FinalizeTournament`'s own comment records that DNF awards reach it *"bypassing standard battle service"*.
 11. **THE PAYOUT-FAILURE PATH LEAVES THE POT INTACT AND THE PLAYER UNPAID.** `DispatchTournamentRewards`' errors are logged per rank (:734-736) and `wg.Wait()` returns regardless, so a rank whose dispatch failed simply does not appear in `payoutTxIDs`; the effective pot was never debited for it (the deduction happens inside the shift on success), so the money stays in the faucet and the placement is silently empty of reward — with an admin broadcast sent only on SUCCESS (:747-750).
 12. **`IsWalletRegistered` IS A LINEAR SCAN OF `paidParticipants` AND IS CALLED UNDER `RLock` WHILE THE SAME SLICE IS APPENDED UNDER `Lock`** (:205, :243, :961-967) — correct as written (the scan is inside the read lock), recorded because the slice is the ONLY registration record: there is no map, no index, and `paidParticipants` appears in **no record family**, so a restart loses every paid entry while the on-chain buy-in remains spent.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the tournament lifecycle, the bracket, the multi-asset payout rail and the archive) — a file with genuinely careful concurrency (a real double-checked lock, a reservation taken BEFORE the oracle call with the TOCTOU window explicitly retired, the bracket cloned and closed before the payout loop, per-asset opt-in and balance gating with a `skipped` list, a deterministic payouts hash over deduped GIDs, and an honest `Links = nil` where a fabricated txid used to be).   8. **`applyDistrictScanner` RE-WALKS EVERY CLUB AND EVERY TRAP MAP TO BUILD A CHAT REPORT** (:956-970) — `for _, club := range l.clubs { for key := range club.ActiveBuffs { … } }`, on the item-use path, inside the held write lock.

Its rewards are floats and random draws, and four doors exist twice.


- **VERDICT:** owner (of the composition root, the boot sequence, the two network loaders and the Lobby-level wrappers) — a boot that is genuinely ordered (registry reconcile before the reward template, the aliased map bound under its owner, the router built before the id that needs it, warmups unawaited, genesis reserves logged only when nothing was recovered) with its own past defects documented in place (the Voi id nil dereference, the `VBVBalance` sample, the always-true demotion broadcast, the silent `BASE_REWARD` parse).   7. **A ROOT FAILURE REPORTS EVERY FRAME FAILED WITH NO PER-SKU REASON.** `res.Failed = len(skus)` (:456-459) is honest in count, but nothing writes a per-frame reason, so the catalogue's `DerivativeNote` for those frames still says *"the server is still deriving the pack"* rather than the real cause.
  8. **THE MANIFEST IS COMMITTED ONCE AT THE END, SO A CRASH LOSES THE WHOLE PASS.** `derivativeStoreRecord` updates memory per frame (deliberately, so a status reader sees progress) and `saveDerivativeManifest` runs only after the loop (:481-484) — one atomic temp+rename for the entire pass, while the PNG files are already on disk. Re-derivation then overwrites them correctly, but the file's own idempotency promise rests on a manifest that a kill mid-pass discards.
  9. **`fileSize` SWALLOWS ITS ERROR.** It returns 0 on failure and its comment says *"the caller reports that separately"* (:825-829), yet `derivePNGToWidth` uses it directly for BOTH the passthrough `Bytes` and the written `Bytes` (:776, :797) with no separate report — a **0-byte measurement** can be recorded as a measured fact in a payload whose entire premise is measured facts.

Here they are caches and the served `GeneratedAt` reads one of them, so this is a note rather than an owed fix.
 11. **THE ONE `sync.Once` THAT IS CORRECT HERE — and worth contrasting with `placeholder_assets.go`.** The warmup is once-per-process by design and its WORK is idempotent by source hash, so a second boot re-derives only what changed; the CATALOGUE's `sync.Once` in the sibling file has no such invalidation, which is why the pair reads as one correct and one stale decision about the same class of cache.
- **VERDICT:** owner (of the light renditions and their manifest) — the strongest example in the tree of MEASURED FACTS (bytes from the file written, pixels from the image encoded, sha256 of the actual bytes, and the source SKU/hash/size all named), an INTEGER-only deterministic filter, atomic manifest writes, idempotency by source hash, a priority list TAKEN FROM the slide declaration so it cannot drift, and a served census that deliberately omits a per-pass fact a resting read cannot know. Its one real defect is the premultiplied narrowing in the filter (a semi-transparent halo, on the tier the app paints at low opacity behind chrome); the rest are edge contracts whose COMMENTS promise more than their bodies check.

Eleven entries above carry a ``Display limit`` note: a tool truncation hid a middle window during the first read. Seven of those windows have now been **re-read and closed**, and each entry own note is retained as the honest record of the FIRST read. What the closed windows yielded:

|---|---|---|
| `courthouse_service.go` | `:101-220` | The rival award is applied **AFTER the lock is released**, from a COPY taken under it; `commitTxID` runs only after the fine is applied (**a crash between the two leaves the payment replayable** — stated as acceptable, not hidden); the float `fine_paid` was REPLACED by `fine_paid_micro` after a repo-wide check that no client read it. |
| `reward_registry.go` | `:93-208` | `seedPrimaryRewardTokenLocked` is the ONLY creator of the primary entry and **never lets a restored value overwrite the environment**; the `VBV` symbol is claimed only when `VBV_ASSET_ID` matches the env asset; pruning drops the **empty asset id** as well as the unregistered; the served view is role-ordered with an asset-id tie-break; `Views()` = lock + delegate. |
| `ui_tree_theming.go` | `:67-254` | The canonical tree list (incl. the five operations consoles, with the reason recorded in place); the pool is round-robin **across characters** with pins removed and non-purchasable frames excluded; a tree the pool cannot cover is served **SHARING another frame and reports `SharedBy: 2`** rather than pretending uniqueness; client ids append after the canonical set; uniqueness is computed. |
| `gaming_os.go` | `:124-311` | Every read takes the read lock and sorts deterministically; **`complianceEngine` is a PACKAGE-LEVEL GLOBAL with its own mutex** — state outside the Lobby and in no record family; `handleOSModuleRegister` registers a module from a request body with a **caller-supplied `monthly_rate` and no authority check**. |
| `record_envelope.go` | `:90-311` | Header length and chunk capacity are computed for the **HIGHEST chunk index** (the worst case, since it has the most digits); the fixed-point loop is bounded at 32 iterations and **refuses non-convergence**; every note is measured against the cap; the chunks are **re-joined and compared with the source base64** (proof of coverage, not an assumption); `assembleRecords` sorts nonces DESC and treats a `total` disagreement or a repeated chunk index as INCOMPLETE. |
| `local_model_promotion.go` | `:90-285` | `TriggerBuild` sets `building` under the lock and **releases before spawning** the process; the env carries identity/tier/backend/derived port; a failure sets `requires_manual_build` with the reason; on success `RunnerPath` is recorded as an identity-namespaced name **without verifying the file exists**; `Save` = snapshot under the owner lock → chain mirror → atomic rename. |
| `auction_service.go` | `:102-369` | The bid escrows the internal balance and refunds the previous bidder **only if that bid was virtual**; settlement takes `(bid*10+50)/100` commission, pays the seller the net, credits the art-gallery club treasury, and **PAYS THE SELLER EVEN WHEN `pullApprovedTokens` FAILS** (the comment says it maintains the token-sink promise); the on-chain record fires only at `amountBase >= 100.0` and its payload carries a **float `amount`**. |
